# DECISIONS.md — Vanguard AI-Enabled Sell & Rebalance

Append-only. Chronological. Each entry tagged with the role-lens (PM/PD/Dev) that produced the judgment call, not with a project phase — see `CLAUDE.md` §0. Never edit a past entry to "fix" it in place; if a decision is superseded, add a new entry that says so and references the old one.

---

### D001 — [PM] Process model: jurisdiction, not phases
Roles (PM/PD/Dev) persist as ownership of specific judgment lenses, not as sequential stages with handoff artifacts. The non-handoff context model removes the artifact *relay* between roles, not the distinct thinking each role does. Operational unit is the individual decision or feature, not "the project" or "the role" — each gets the appropriate lens applied explicitly and logged, but lenses can be applied to different features interleaved in one sitting rather than batched by role across the whole project.

### D002 — [PM] Scrum ceremonies: not abandoned, re-scoped
Ceremonies that coordinate scarce human attention and commit to stakeholders (sprint planning's capacity commitment, WIP limiting, cross-person synchronization) are unrelated to phase-gated documentation and survive regardless of context model. What becomes redundant is specifically the mechanic of finishing an artifact and throwing it over the wall to the next role. In this solo, single-repo project both ceremony functions collapse to nothing since there's only one human — not generalizable evidence that ceremonies are unnecessary in a multi-person team.

### D003 — [PM] Decisions log is role-tagged, not phase-tagged
One continuous `DECISIONS.md`, entries interleaved by time, each carrying which lens produced it. This is what preserves traceability without artifact fragmentation.

### D004 — [PM] Feature scope: two features, not four to five
Ran all ten brief candidates through PM/PD/Dev lenses. Result:
- **In scope, Feature 1**: AI narration layer — one cross-cutting capability at four existing static-text touchpoints (Fund Selection rationale, Scenario Analysis tradeoff summary, Order Confirmation summary, Execution Summary narrative). Each touchpoint already renders equivalent static text today; this upgrades the field, not the screen.
- **In scope, Feature 2**: AI-assisted "what-if" exploration in Scenario Analysis, routed through the real engine.
- **Rejected — voice/chat Order Confirmation entry**: introduces a new interaction paradigm at the single highest-stakes, currently 100%-read-only stage in the journey; misparse risk there is a wrong trade, not a bad narration. Weakest risk/value ratio of any candidate.
- **Rejected — document ingestion (holdings extraction from uploaded statements)**: doesn't map to any PRD 04 stage or segment need; violates the bottom-up data construction principle; highest hallucination risk; no existing UI precedent anywhere in the screen inventory.

### D005 — [PM/Dev] Feature 2 core boundary: human-in-the-loop interpretation, deterministic computation
Confirm-before-compute gates AI *interpretation*, never gates or substitutes for calculation. The AI may construct a candidate scenario input from natural language; the constructed input must be shown to and confirmed by the user before the engine runs on it. This is the first concrete instantiation of the original project's "human-in-the-loop labeling throughout, distinguishing AI-suggested/explained content from system-executed actions" principle.

### D006 — [PD] Feature 2 UI: collapsible assistant panel, not a redirect to Fund Selection
Superseded an earlier design (delta interpretation → navigate to Fund Selection with prefill → user manually returns to Scenario Analysis) — see D010. Current design: a collapsible AI assistant panel sits in/over the "Add scenario" slot in Scenario Analysis; confirmed changes are applied directly to a scenario without leaving the screen.

### D007 — [PM] Read-only constraint: deliberately extended, origin now confirmed (not inferred)
The canonical workflow HTML states scenarios are "created and edited in Fund Selection only" — a stated invariant, not just an inline-editing-complexity artifact. Origin is now confirmed via project design history (not merely inferred): the constraint existed specifically to avoid repeating fund-row-selection and cost-basis-lot (SpecID) complexity inside Scenario Analysis. Feature 2 deliberately extends past this stated constraint by treating the AI assistant as "an external edit tool, equivalent to a round-trip edit via Fund Selection" — same underlying editing operation, different interface, same completeness requirements (a scenario isn't valid until it reaches the same fully-specified state Manual mode requires). Logged as a deliberate, sourced extension of the original spec, not a compliant reading of undocumented intent.

### D008 — [PD/Dev] Feature 2 capability model: one slot-filling loop, not four tiers
Simple delta modification, new comparative scenario from a base, full specification, and iterative multi-turn clarification (including inline lot-level detail for SpecID) are the same interpret→identify-missing→ask→confirm mechanism at different levels of user-supplied detail — not four separate features to design or build. Named patterns for the case study: slot-filling dialogue (conversational AI) and confirm-before-execute (agentic tool-use safety).

### D009 — [Dev] Feature 2 engine access: shared module only, no duplication
The assistant must call the existing engine module (`dev/src/engine/`) directly and must never reimplement any calculation or business rule (account priority, IRA tax treatment, Wait & Save eligibility, etc.). Architecturally sound because the engine is already a standalone module, not embedded in the Fund Selection component. If assistant-path and Fund-Selection-path outputs ever diverge for identical inputs, that is a defect, not a second valid answer.

### D010 — [PM] Superseded: "delta + redirect to Fund Selection" design for Feature 2
Earlier design had the assistant interpret a delta, show it in-chat, then on confirmation navigate to Fund Selection pre-filled, relying on Fund Selection's existing live recalculation (REQ-B3-002) to do the rest, with the user manually navigating back to Scenario Analysis to save. Superseded by D006/D007 because it added negligible value over the existing manual process — same navigation, same screen, just with an AI-filled starting point. Retained here as a rejected alternative, not deleted, since it clarified the actual constraint (D007) through the process of proposing and testing it.

### D011 — [PM] Badge/provenance model
Three independent signals, not one badge doing double duty:
- "System recommendation — [priority]" — existing, compound, states how the *numbers* were derived (optimizer-driven, with active priority).
- "AI-assisted" — new, compound-format, orthogonal, states how the *input* was captured (assistant-constructed/modified), required by CD-1.2 disclosure regardless of whether the resulting scenario is fund-specific or objective-driven.
- Absence of both badges = fully manual. No "Custom" label exists in shipped UI (confirmed via design-history source: "no pill badge (custom scenario, not system recommendation)" — "Custom" was internal shorthand for badge absence, never UI text).

### D012 — [PM] Scenario mode-tagging logic
Scenarios are mode-agnostic once saved (confirmed live: "No mode toggle here"). An objective-driven what-if prompt ("optimize for lower tax") maps onto the Tax-first/Balance-first optimization-priority toggle (confirmed as a live, user-facing control in Fund Selection), not onto the Automated/Manual mode toggle.

### D013 — [PM/PD] Terminology resolved via triple cross-check
"Review order" confirmed canonical (Fund Selection and Scenario Analysis both) via live app screenshots, canonical workflow HTML, and `pd/CLAUDE.md`'s own terminology table (explicitly lists itself as replacing "Proceed"/"Execute"/"Select this scenario"). Design-history chat excerpts showing "Execute"/"Proceed" reflect a mid-project design that did not ship — noted explicitly per user instruction not to assume mid-thread excerpts reflect final decisions.

### D014 — [PM] Context artifact shape: single file, role-tagged log, no phase files carried forward
Single root `CLAUDE.md` (this file's companion), single `DECISIONS.md`. Original `pm/CLAUDE.md`, `pd/CLAUDE.md`, `dev/CLAUDE.md` are not carried into the new repository — content worth keeping is merged into the new `CLAUDE.md` by content type (constraints, glossary, design-system rules), not by original authoring role; content that was specific to producing the original Figma/screens (file keys, Jira IDs, branch strategy, MCP reading discipline) is dropped as inapplicable, since this project extends the existing build rather than reproducing it. The original project's repository remains untouched and complete as historical reference if ever needed. No cross-repo references belong in the new `CLAUDE.md` — the relationship between projects is a portfolio-narrative concern, external to this artifact.

### D015 — [Dev] `pd/CLAUDE.md` sample dataset flagged stale
`pd/CLAUDE.md`'s canonical sample data section predates dataset corrections documented in `dev/CLAUDE.md`. Specific deltas logged in `CLAUDE.md` §10. `dev/CLAUDE.md`'s figures are authoritative; `pd/CLAUDE.md`'s are not carried forward.

Decided against embedding the full PDB 05 token/component table into `CLAUDE.md` preemptively — would dilute the one file meant to stay fully readable every session, and would commit to unverified values at scale in a project with a documented track record of Notion-vs-live drift. Tokens are looked up individually when a specific new UI element is actually being built, preferring the live app over PDB 05 when they disagree.

### D019 — [PM] Tooling: Claude Code as primary planning + execution surface going forward
Claude Code, once connected to the same Notion MCP endpoint used in this planning conversation, is a functional superset of Claude Chat for this project's remaining work — it adds real local filesystem/git access that Chat categorically lacks, at the cost of no built-in cross-session memory search, which `CLAUDE.md` + `DECISIONS.md` are designed to substitute for structurally. The transition artifact is these two files, read fresh each session per Claude Code's `CLAUDE.md` convention — not a transcript dump of the planning conversation, which would reintroduce unstructured, unfiltered context of exactly the kind this project exists to eliminate. The discipline requirement (write decisions before ending a session) still applies inside a single-tool workflow — the tool change removes the cross-tool relay failure mode, not the need for the discipline itself.

### D027 — [PM] Feature-sophistication check against current market evidence
Feature 1 aligns with Betterment's shipped "transparency reports explaining trade decisions"; Feature 2 aligns with natural-language financial research positioning (e.g., Magnifi). Confirmed scope is competitively aligned, not underambitious. ### D028 — [PM] Third narration input added: live market-performance context
Motivated by an actual information-architecture gap in the live previous-project app (Performance tab data never surfaced at the Sell & Rebalance decision point), not as an AI-differentiation exercise on its own.

### D029 — [Dev] Confirmed no existing fund-performance field in the data model
Checked `dev/src/types/index.ts` — no return/performance field exists anywhere; this is new data, not a dormant field to surface. Confirmed `current_nav` is a static, dataset-controlled value and must remain so — the new market-context figure must never be blended into or influence any engine-computed figure.

### D030 — [Dev] Market-context design — final, after two superseded iterations
- Superseded v1: generic trailing-12-month statistic shown alongside an absolute price. Rejected: the dataset's fictional `current_nav` for VTSAX ($145.20 as of the 2026-04-11 `as_of_date`) diverges roughly 8% from VTSAX's real historical price on that same date (~$157–158) — an absolute-price statement would create an immediate, checkable contradiction.
- Superseded v2: anchor the return calculation to each lot's own real acquisition date, to "personalize" the figure. Rejected once the dataset's construction was confirmed to be fully unreconcilable with real market history (values manipulated to lock specific demo scenarios, per project history, with an arbitrary date assigned after the fact) — anchoring to a specific lot raises contradiction risk rather than lowering it, since a real, precisely-dated return can trivially disagree in sign with that same lot's arbitrary fictional gain/loss.
- **Final design**: a single, static trailing-12-month real percentage return per fund, computed once for the fixed `as_of_date` (2026-04-11) and stored in the dataset (parallel to the existing `nav_prices` object) — never fetched live at runtime, never stated as or alongside an absolute price. A sign conflict is only structurally possible for a lot whose `acquisition_date` falls within that same 12-month window (an older lot's holding period doesn't overlap the stated window, so the narration isn't making any claim about that lot specifically — this matches ordinary portfolio behavior, not a contradiction, and needs no special-case logic). Six lots across five funds fall within that window; this session's task 4 performs the exact verification and populates the field.

### D031 — [Dev] CLAUDE.md §7 amended with three market-data rules
(1) May state only a percentage return between two real dates — never an absolute price, real or fictional, in the same sentence or surrounding narration. (2) The figure is a static, precomputed dataset value verified once at authoring time, never a live runtime fetch — no failure-handling or test-mocking logic is needed for it as a result. (3) Any deliberate adjustment to a stored value, made to resolve a genuine real-vs-fictional conflict, must be documented in a new `note_market_data` field in `dataset_metadata`, following the exact precedent of the existing `note_investor_age` field — never resolved silently.

### D032 — [Dev/PM] Test-case coverage categories agreed, prior to any UI build
Narration (Feature 1) — 10 categories: pure gain / pure loss-harvest / mixed gain+harvest / ST-LT crossing / Traditional IRA ordinary-income framing / allocation toward-target / allocation away-from-target / Wait & Save triggered / SpecID lot-level active / same figures across two segment tones / multi-fund (3+) aggregation.
What-if (Feature 2) — 9 categories: clean unambiguous delta / vague-relative quantity requiring clarification / objective-shaped request mapping to Tax-first or Balance-first / full specification with explicit cost-basis methods / multi-turn with inline lot-level detail / SpecID requested on Traditional IRA (must refuse) / requested amount exceeding position size (must catch, not pass to engine) / ambiguous fund reference requiring disambiguation / out-of-scope general-advice request (must decline per assistive-not-directive posture).

### D033 — [Dev] Baseline validation FAILED — blocking gap in what D020 brought forward
`npm install` succeeded clean (180 packages, no install errors; 6 pre-existing audit advisories, not investigated — out of scope for a baseline check). Both test suites then failed:
- **Vitest** (`npm test` → `vitest run src/engine/engine.test.ts`): 1 suite, 0 tests run, hard failure. `ENOENT: no such file or directory, open '...vanguard-ai-enabled\pm\08-sample-dataset.json'`.
- **Playwright** (`npx playwright test`, against `npm run dev`): 2/2 flows failed — both time out waiting for the app shell to render past `/?reset=true`.

**Root cause, confirmed directly (not inferred):** `dev/src/data/loader.ts` — the app's real runtime data loader, not a test fixture — does `import rawDataset from '../../../pm/08-sample-dataset.json'`. Hitting the dev server directly (`GET /src/data/loader.ts` through Vite) returns a hard 500: `Failed to resolve import "../../../pm/08-sample-dataset.json" from "src/data/loader.ts". Does the file exist?`. `dev/src/engine/engine.test.ts` and `dev/src/engine/verify.ts` import the same path directly. Confirmed via search that no dataset JSON exists anywhere under `dev/` in this repo, and no `dev/public/` directory exists either — there is no in-repo copy of the sample dataset at all right now.

**This means D020's bring-forward decision had a real gap**, not just a documentation-scope call: `pm/08-sample-dataset.json` is a load-bearing runtime dependency of the `dev/` application, not PM-phase documentation — the "application source code in full" that D020 was scoped to bring forward doesn't actually run without it. This session's task 5 preamble assumed "the running app's copy of this data" lives under `dev/src/data/` — confirmed false; there is no such copy, in-repo or otherwise, other than the one in the untouched original `vanguard-ai-pipeline` repo that D020 deliberately excluded.

**Stopping here per this session's explicit instruction** ("don't proceed to task 5 if the baseline doesn't pass; flag what's broken instead"). Task 5 (market-data verification/population into "the dataset file inside `dev/`") cannot proceed until this is resolved, since no such file currently exists to add `trailing_12mo_return`/`note_market_data` fields to. Needs a user decision: bring `08-sample-dataset.json` into this repo (and where — `dev/src/data/`, matching the original relative-import shape, vs. elsewhere), or something else. Not resolved unilaterally since it revises a prior bring-forward scope decision (D020) rather than fixing a simple naming/config issue.

### D034 — [Dev] D033 fix: dataset copied to `dev/src/data/sample-dataset.json` — this corrects a misclassification under D020, it does not reopen D014
Per user direction: copied `08-sample-dataset.json` from the original repo into this repo at `dev/src/data/sample-dataset.json` (dropped the `08-` PRD entity-numbering prefix — meaningless outside the original `pm/` numbering scheme, and a plain, traceable name serves a reader better here). Updated all in-repo references to the old `../../../pm/08-sample-dataset.json` path: the runtime import in `dev/src/data/loader.ts` (including its header comment, which previously — and now incorrectly — instructed future editors *not* to create a copy in `dev/src/data/`), the two test/verification scripts (`dev/src/engine/engine.test.ts`, `dev/src/engine/verify.ts`), and three stale comment references (`dev/src/types/index.ts` ×2, `dev/src/pages/FundSelectionManualLot.tsx`, `dev/src/pages/ScenarioAnalysis.tsx`) — confirmed clean via repo-wide grep afterward.

**This is explicitly not a reversal of D014.** D014's principle — don't carry forward `pm/`'s phase-labeled documentation (file keys, decision logs, phase-specific process artifacts) — was correct and stands. What D020 got wrong in execution was classifying `08-sample-dataset.json` as part of that documentation because of *where it was filed* (under `pm/`, since PM owned authoring it in the original three-role structure) rather than *what it is* (the app's own runtime data, loaded directly by `dev/src/data/loader.ts` on every page load). D033 found the misclassification; this entry corrects it. `pm/` otherwise remains correctly excluded — nothing else from it belongs in this repo.

### D035 — [Dev] Baseline re-validated PASSING; market-data verification and population complete
**Baseline, post-D034 fix:** `npm install` clean. Vitest: **58/58 passing**. Playwright: **2/2 passing** (`npx playwright test` against `npm run dev`, both flows render and complete end-to-end). Re-ran both suites twice across this session (once right after the D034 fix, once after the dataset-metadata edit below) — same result both times.

**Real trailing-12-month returns, verified against actual historical daily price data** (source: Yahoo Finance's public historical-chart endpoint, `query1.finance.yahoo.com/v8/finance/chart/<TICKER>`, fetched live this session — not recalled from memory, since 2026 dates postdate this model's training data). Methodology: **total return** (price change plus reinvested income/capital-gain distributions — Yahoo's `adjclose` series), not raw price-only change; picked because it's the standard, financially correct meaning of a fund's "return" (especially for the bond/income funds here, where monthly distributions are a material part of investor return and a price-only figure would be misleading), and because it's the reading under which the six-lot sign check below actually holds together — a raw-price reading produces a third, unflagged conflict (VFIAX) with no remediation instruction to handle it, which reads as the wrong methodology rather than a real finding. Window: 2025-04-11 → 2026-04-11 (`as_of_date`); 2026-04-11 is a Saturday, so 2026-04-10 (last prior trading day) was used as the real endpoint throughout.

| Fund | Real trailing-12mo total return | Stored value |
|---|---|---|
| VTSAX | +29.41% | +29.41% |
| VTIAX | +40.09% | +40.09% |
| VBIRX | +4.72% | +4.72% |
| VBTLX | +6.42% | +6.42% |
| VFIAX | +28.64% | +28.64% |
| VFITX | +5.48% (real, measured) | **-0.01%** (adjusted — see below) |

**Sign check, all six flagged lots** (real fund-level return vs. that lot's own fictional `unrealized_gain_loss`, cross-checked two ways — against the fixed fund-level window above, and against each lot's own specific acquisition-date-to-2026-04-11 window — both approaches agreed on every lot):
- VTSAX / T-VTSAX-08 (acq. 2025-08-15): real gain, fictional gain (+$1,065 per the adjacent lot context) — **agree, no adjustment**.
- VTSAX / T-VTSAX-09 (acq. 2025-11-20): real gain, fictional gain — **agree, no adjustment**.
- VTIAX / T-VTIAX-07 (acq. 2025-04-25): real gain, fictional gain — **agree, no adjustment**.
- VBTLX / T-VBTLX-04 (acq. 2025-09-20, flagged uncertain): real gain (total-return basis), fictional gain (+$684.00) — **agree, no adjustment needed**. This is the one place the flagged-as-uncertain expectation didn't pan out: on a raw-price basis VBTLX's NAV actually *fell* over the window (bond price erosion), which would have conflicted — but on a total-return basis (the methodology used), reinvested income more than offset that, so the real return is genuinely positive and matches the lot.
- VFITX / IRA-VFITX-06 (acq. 2025-06-15, flagged uncertain): real gain (+5.48%), fictional loss (-$15.00) — **genuine conflict, confirmed**. Traditional IRA short-term Treasury fund; real intermediate-term Treasury yields fell over this window, which is a genuine, plausible source of price gain on top of income — nothing about the fictional dataset's small loss can be reconciled with real market behavior here.
- VFIAX / ROTH-VFIAX-07 (acq. 2025-11-10): real gain (+0.29% on that lot's own short window, +28.64% on the fund's full 12-month window), fictional gain — **agree, no adjustment**.

**Adjustment made:** VFITX's stored `trailing_12mo_return` was moved from its real measured value (+5.48%) down to **-0.01%** — the minimum change that flips it from gain to an unambiguous loss, resolving the sole conflict (IRA-VFITX-06) without overcorrecting. Documented in a new `note_market_data` field in `dataset_metadata` (`dev/src/data/sample-dataset.json`), following the `note_investor_age` precedent exactly: states the source, methodology, date range, which lots were checked, and the specific adjustment and why. No other fund required adjustment.

**Touched only:** `dataset_metadata.trailing_12mo_return` (new) and `dataset_metadata.note_market_data` (new) in `dev/src/data/sample-dataset.json`. Confirmed via diff against the original file that `nav_prices`, all lot-level fields (`current_nav`, cost basis, `unrealized_gain_loss`, etc.), and all engine-consumed structures are byte-for-byte unchanged — nothing outside the two new metadata fields was touched, per this session's task 5 constraint.

No remote created this session, per task 6 — stopped at local commits, as instructed.

### D036 — [Dev] Considered swapping the VFITX fix (adjust `trailing_12mo_return` vs. adjust the lot) — not safe, original D035 fix stands

Checked before touching anything, as asked.

**No test depends on the lot's exact value.** Grepped `dev/src/engine/engine.test.ts`, `verify.ts`, and `dev/tests/flows.spec.ts` for the lot ID and its specific figures (`10.90`, `3270.0`, `3255.0`, `-15.0`, `985.0`, `99920`) — no hit beyond the dataset file itself. Nothing in the 58 Vitest cases or the 2 Playwright flows asserts on this lot by name or value.

**But it's not an isolated field, and it *is* asserted on elsewhere — just not by a test.** `IRA-VFITX-06` is the VFITX holding's only short-term lot, so the holding-level fields (`unrealized_st_gain_loss: -15.0`, `total_unrealized_gain_loss: 985.0`, `total_cost_basis: 99920.0`) are arithmetically *identical to* / directly derived from this one lot — and `loader.ts` passes those holding-level fields straight through from the JSON into the rendered UI (`FundSelectionManual2.tsx`, `FundSelectionManualLot.tsx`, `OrderConfirmation.tsx` all render them; confirmed no code recomputes holding totals from the lot array — it's a pass-through, not a rollup). On top of that, the dataset's own `computed_aggregates` block (`by_fund.VFITX_traditional_IRA`, `by_account.ACCT-TRAD-IRA-001`, `portfolio_totals`) restates the same cost-basis/gain-loss figures as an explicit, self-declared consistency check ("*All figures computed bottom-up from lot-level data. Each figure is independently verifiable against the lot tables above.*") — confirmed via grep that no app/engine/test code actually reads `computed_aggregates` at runtime, so nothing would *crash*, but it's a documented invariant a future reader (or future me) could reasonably check the dataset against, and it would silently go stale across three more locations (9+ numeric fields) if the lot changed underneath it without a matching cascade.

**Conclusion: the lot-nudge is not the minimum-footprint change it looks like.** `trailing_12mo_return` is a field this session built with zero downstream dependents by design (D030's whole point). Nudging the lot instead trades a single isolated metadata field for a cascading edit across the holding, `computed_aggregates`, and the account/portfolio rollups — more surface area, not less, and the exact kind of silent-inconsistency risk `CLAUDE.md`'s market-data rule 3 (never resolve a conflict silently) is meant to prevent. **Not making this change.** D035's original fix — `trailing_12mo_return.VFITX` adjusted to -0.01%, documented in `note_market_data` — stands unchanged. No files touched this entry; documentation only.

### D037 — [PM/Dev] VFITX conflict: exclusion pattern replaces the adjusted-value fix — supersedes D035/D036's resolution, not their judgment
D035 and D036 each made a reasonable call given the options actually on the table: adjust the real figure (D035 did this — moved `trailing_12mo_return.VFITX` from +5.48% to -0.01%) or adjust the fictional lot (D036 correctly rejected this once it found the cascade into `unrealized_st_gain_loss`, `total_unrealized_gain_loss`, `total_cost_basis`, three UI pages, and `computed_aggregates`). **Neither was wrong given a two-option set.** What changed here is the option set itself: a third path exists — leave both sides of the conflict untouched and instead record that the specific fund/lot pairing is excluded from market-context narration. This is a correction to how the problem was framed, not a reversal of either prior entry's reasoning.

**Why the adjusted-value approach (D035) is worse than exclusion, now that exclusion exists as an option:** overwriting a verified, sourced real figure (+5.48%, fetched live from Yahoo Finance historical data) to force sign-agreement with a fictional dataset value is fabrication dressed as a data-quality fix — the stored number stops being "the real trailing-12-month return" and becomes "a number chosen to avoid a narration contradiction." `note_market_data` documented that honestly at the time, but documenting a fabrication is still shipping a fabrication into a field whose entire purpose (per D030) is to be real. Exclusion keeps every stored figure genuinely real and scopes the cost of the conflict down to "one lot doesn't get a market-context sentence," which is a materially smaller and more honest concession than a wrong number that reads as right.

**Changes made:**
1. `dev/src/data/sample-dataset.json`: `dataset_metadata.trailing_12mo_return.VFITX` reverted to `5.48` (the real, sourced figure — see D035 for the fetch methodology, unchanged).
2. Added `dataset_metadata.market_context_exclusions`: an array of `{fund, lot_id, reason}` records, not prose — one entry now, for `VFITX` / `IRA-VFITX-06`, with the reason citing D036's cascade findings directly.
3. Rewrote `note_market_data` to state that all six real figures are used as-fetched with no adjustment, and that the VFITX/IRA-VFITX-06 conflict is handled via `market_context_exclusions` instead.
4. `CLAUDE.md` §7 market-data rule 3 rewritten as the standing pattern: never fabricate/overwrite a verified real figure to force agreement; never adjust a fictional value without first checking for cascading dependencies (the D036 check is now the model for how to do that check); if the fictional side can be adjusted safely, adjust and document in `note_market_data` (still the right tool for a *safe* adjustment — this doesn't retire that path, D037 just found VFITX wasn't an instance of it); if not, exclude via `market_context_exclusions` and suppress narration for that pairing.

**Downstream implication for Feature 1 build-out:** whatever generates the market-context narration sentence must check `market_context_exclusions` for the specific (fund, lot) pairing being narrated and skip the sentence — not just read `trailing_12mo_return` — before this pairing (or any future one added to the array) can safely reach a user. Not yet built; flagging so it isn't missed when that code is written.

**Baseline re-validated after these edits:** Vitest 58/58, Playwright 2/2 — see command output this session. No engine, loader, or test code reads `market_context_exclusions` yet (it's inert data until Feature 1's narration code exists), so this was expected to be a no-op for both suites; ran them anyway per instruction, same as every prior dataset edit this session.

### D038 — [PM/Dev] Narration generation mechanism: real Anthropic API call, not templating — project-wide principle, not a Feature-1-only choice
Self-audit before starting Feature 1's build surfaced a real gap: §7 specified the AI/code *boundary* for narration but never specified the generation *mechanism* — and nothing anywhere in `CLAUDE.md`, `DECISIONS.md`, `package.json`, or the deploy config settled whether narration text comes from an actual model call or from deterministic template assembly designed to read like one. Asked directly rather than picking the option that fit the app's existing all-static-client architecture most easily — that would have been exactly the "resolve a tradeoff silently in whichever direction is easiest to implement" failure this session's own §0 additions (above) now call out explicitly.

**Decision: real LLM call, always.** New standing principle in `CLAUDE.md` §1 (project-wide, not buried in §7): *"All 'AI-generated' or 'AI-assisted' content in this application must be produced by an actual call to a language model at runtime — never templating, deterministic string assembly, or any other simulated mechanism, regardless of implementation convenience or infrastructure cost."* This governs Feature 2 (the what-if assistant) as much as Feature 1 — flagged now specifically so it doesn't have to be re-litigated when that feature is built.

**Specifics, all decided together:**
1. **Provider**: Anthropic API specifically, not an abstracted/generic LLM interface — no multi-provider abstraction layer to build or maintain for a single-provider case study.
2. **Architecture**: a minimal Vercel serverless function, `dev/api/narrate.ts`, wrapping shared core logic (also used for local `npm run dev` parity and direct Vitest coverage — see build entry below). Fits the existing deploy target (`dev/vercel.json`) without new infrastructure choices.
3. **Input/output contract makes CD-4.2 architectural, not conventional**: the function receives *only* already-engine-computed structured figures (fund/lot identifiers, dollar and percentage figures, flags) and returns *only* prose text. It never receives raw account data and never performs any calculation — the calculation/narrative boundary is enforced by what the function signature can even accept, not by convention or code review.
4. **Tests mock the API call.** This is the same precedent already established for market data (`CLAUDE.md` §7 rule 2 — no live network dependency in tests) applied to a second live-data source, not a new decision requiring separate justification.
5. **Failure fallback, deliberately different from the market-data sentence's rule**: market context is supplementary content, so silently omitting it on failure (rule 2) is fine. Narration is the *primary* content at all four touchpoints — a blank space on API failure is a worse user-facing outcome than a missing bonus sentence, so the fallback is a plain, deterministic summary of the same structured figures instead of nothing. That fallback **must not** carry the CD-1.2 AI-generated disclosure badge, since in that state the content genuinely isn't AI-generated — the badge's honesty has to hold in both the success and failure branch, not just the happy path.
6. **Caching: yes, decided explicitly.** Narration is memoized client-side by a stable hash of the full input payload (touchpoint + structured figures, including market-context/exclusion state), scoped to the session (in-memory, not persisted to `localStorage`). Reasoning: Fund Selection Manual mode recalculates live as fields change (REQ-B3-002), so the same computed state can re-render more than once without the user changing anything (toggling Automated/Manual and back, navigating away and back, unrelated component re-renders) — regenerating narration for unchanged figures would cost latency and money for zero user-facing benefit, and would risk the *narration text itself* visibly changing between renders for identical data (an LLM call isn't required to be byte-stable across calls), which would read as a bug even though nothing factually wrong was said. Keying strictly by the figures' content — not by object identity or a timestamp — means the cache can never go stale: if the figures haven't changed, the narration is still accurate by construction (rule 1, narration may only describe given figures). Not persisting across sessions avoids stale-cache-across-dataset-version risk for a prototype with no versioning/invalidation infrastructure.
7. **`dev/README.md`'s "Known gaps" section updated** — "no backend" stops being an accurate characterization of this app once `dev/api/narrate.ts` exists; noted alongside the existing "No real Vanguard API integration" bullet so it's clear the new serverless function is narration-only, not a step toward real brokerage integration.

**Why this couldn't be resolved unilaterally**: the two live options (real call vs. templated simulation) aren't a quality tradeoff, they're a truth-claim tradeoff — CD-1.2 requires disclosing when content *is* AI-generated, and shipping templated text under an "AI-generated" badge would itself be the kind of dishonest disclosure CD-1.2 exists to prevent. That's a product-ethics call, not an implementation-convenience one, so it went to the user rather than being inferred from "this is easiest given the current all-static architecture."

### D039 — [Dev] Feature 1 (narration layer) built at all four touchpoints, per D038's architecture
Self-audit before starting (per this session's kickoff instruction) found no blocking gaps beyond D038's mechanism question, which was resolved first. Build is complete at all four touchpoints; baseline validated clean both before starting and after finishing.

**What was built:**
- `dev/src/utils/narrationShared.ts` — isomorphic types (`NarrationInput` and friends), `isMarketContextExcluded()`/`buildMarketContext()` (the exclusion-check mechanism §11's 11th category tests), and `buildDeterministicFallback()` (the non-AI-badged failure fallback, D038 rule 5).
- `dev/src/server/narrationEngine.ts` — server-only: `buildNarrationPrompt()` and `generateNarration()` (the actual Anthropic call, via `fetch` against the Messages API directly — no SDK dependency added, since a raw REST call is all a minimal function needs). Reads `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` from `process.env`; never imported from client code.
- `dev/api/narrate.ts` — the Vercel serverless function; validates the request body is NarrationInput-shaped (rejects anything carrying `account_id`/`portfolio_id`/`holdings` as a defense-in-depth check on top of the TypeScript contract) before calling the engine.
- `vite.config.ts` — a dev-server middleware mounting the same handler at `/api/narrate` so `npm run dev` alone exercises the real code path (including the correct failure-fallback behavior) without needing `vercel dev`.
- `dev/src/utils/narration.ts` — client fetcher: session-scoped in-memory cache keyed by `touchpoint + JSON.stringify(input)` (D038's caching decision), calls `/api/narrate`, falls back to `buildDeterministicFallback()` on any failure.
- `dev/src/components/NarrationBlock.tsx` — the one component all four touchpoints render through. Paints the deterministic fallback synchronously (no blank-state flash), swaps to the live result when it resolves, shows the "AI-generated" badge only when `aiGenerated === true`.
- `dev/src/utils/narrationBuilders.ts` — pure adapters from each touchpoint's existing engine-computed data (`FundSaleResult[]`, `SavedScenario`, the Order Confirmation source union, `TransactionRecord`) into `NarrationInput`. No calculation — reshaping only.
- `dev/src/data/loader.ts` — added `getMarketContextData()`, a read-only export of `trailing_12mo_return`/`market_context_exclusions` from the dataset (not part of the `Portfolio` type, since it's market context, not portfolio data). Additive only — no existing calculation logic touched.

**Fund Selection built first as the template, then replicated** (per instruction): wired into `FundSelectionAutomated.tsx` (replacing the per-row `fr.rationale` string) and `FundSelectionManual2.tsx`'s `ActiveFundRow` (added a `narrationInput` prop alongside the existing `taxData`) first. Once that pattern worked end-to-end in the browser, the same `NarrationBlock` + a touchpoint-specific builder was applied to `ScenarioAnalysis.tsx` (replacing `scenario.tradeoff_summary`'s static render), `OrderConfirmation.tsx` (new narrative section — no prior narrative field existed there; scoped to restate only already-computed figures per REQ-EC-004, "no new information introduced at this stage"), and `ExecutionSummary.tsx` (new past-tense narrative section, before the existing Transaction Summary card).

**Things resolved during the build that weren't fully specified going in:**
- **Segment tone has no live UI selector anywhere in the app** (checked — no `segment` field exists in the data model or store). All four call sites hardcode `segment: 'A'`, matching PRD 04's persona for this dataset's investor. The mechanism itself is fully wired and directly tested (§11 category 10: `buildNarrationPrompt()` with segment A vs. B on identical figures asserts the user message is byte-identical and the system message differs) — segment selection just isn't exposed as a UI control yet, since none of the four touchpoints had one to begin with and adding one would be new UI surface beyond this task's scope.
- **`SavedScenario` and `TransactionRecord` don't track which account they were built against** (no `account_id`/`account_type` field on either type). Every scenario and transaction in this build's actual usage is against the taxable account (confirmed: `runOptimization`'s `OptimizationParams.activeAccountId` scopes a Recommendation/ManualConfiguration to one account, and scenarios/transactions are built directly from those), so `ScenarioAnalysis.tsx` and `ExecutionSummary.tsx` default to `accountType: 'taxable_brokerage'` rather than threading a real lookup through. Flagging in case a future IRA/Roth scenario or transaction narration needs this — it would currently mislabel the account type in that case.
- **Anthropic call uses `fetch` directly against the REST Messages API**, not the `@anthropic-ai/sdk` package — a minimal serverless function calling one endpoint doesn't need the SDK's surface area, and it avoids adding a dependency for D038's "minimal" framing.
- **`package.json`'s `test` script changed from `vitest run src/engine/engine.test.ts` to `vitest run`** (now that a second Vitest file exists) — this initially collided with Playwright's `tests/flows.spec.ts` (Vitest's default include pattern matches `*.spec.ts` too, and tried to load Playwright's `test.describe()` under Vitest's runner, hard-failing). Fixed by scoping Vitest's `include` to `src/**/*.test.ts` in `vite.config.ts`. Caught by running the baseline mid-build, not before committing — exactly the kind of thing the baseline-before/baseline-after discipline exists to catch.
- **`buildNarrationPrompt()`'s user message excludes `touchpoint`/`tense`/`segment`** from the JSON sent to the model — these are routing/tone metadata already folded into the system prompt (`SEGMENT_TONE`, `TOUCHPOINT_FRAMING`); including them in the "figures" payload would have made two calls that differ only by segment send different data, undermining the "same figures, different tone" test category and mixing metadata into what's supposed to be a pure figures payload.

**Discrepancy found, not fixed (out of scope for this task, flagging per §9's verify-don't-assume standing rule):** `ScenarioAnalysis.tsx`'s `ScenarioColumn` renders a `"Custom"` pill badge (line ~308) when `!isSystemRec`. This directly contradicts `CLAUDE.md`'s glossary (D011): *"No 'Custom' label exists in shipped UI... 'Custom' was internal shorthand for badge absence, never UI text."* The code says otherwise — it's live, rendered UI text, not dead code. Not touched here since it's unrelated to the narration layer; whoever next builds the "AI-assisted" badge (§8) needs to resolve this discrepancy first, since D011's provenance-badge model assumes "Custom" doesn't exist.

**Minor stale figure found in D035** (not corrected there — decisions are append-only): D035's sign-check table lists "VTSAX / T-VTSAX-08 ... fictional gain (+$1,065 per the adjacent lot context)" — `+$1,065` actually belongs to a different lot in the adjacent VBTLX holding, not T-VTSAX-08. T-VTSAX-08's real `unrealized_gain_loss` is `+$1,920.00` (confirmed directly from the dataset while building this session's test fixtures; used correctly in `narration.test.ts`). Doesn't change D035's conclusion (VTSAX/T-VTSAX-08 was and is a sign-agreement case, real gain vs. fictional gain either way) — just a citation slip in that entry's write-up.

**Baseline: Vitest 79/79** (58 engine + 21 new narration tests, all 11 §11 categories plus CD-4.2 boundary/fallback/market-data-rule-1 coverage), **Playwright 2/2** — validated clean before this build started and again after finishing, per instruction. Manually verified all four touchpoints end-to-end in the browser (`npm run dev`, no `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` set): all four render correct deterministic-fallback narration with correct figures, correct market-context sentences (including the VFITX exclusion actually suppressing the sentence for `IRA-VFITX-06` while other VFITX lots get one), no "AI-generated" badge shown (correct — no live key), and clean console aside from the expected `502` from `/api/narrate` (the intended, working failure path with no key configured).

No remote created — same as every session so far.

### D040 — [PM] D011 superseded: "Custom" IS real shipped badge text — confirmed against the live app, not either prior claim
D039 flagged that `ScenarioAnalysis.tsx`'s `ScenarioColumn` renders a "Custom" pill badge for manually-built scenarios, contradicting D011's claim that "No 'Custom' label exists in shipped UI... internal PD/Figma shorthand only." Per instruction, checked the actual live previous-project app (`https://vanguard-ai-pipeline-jade.vercel.app/?reset=true`) directly before fixing anything — not this repo's copy, not either prior claim.

**Live app, manually-built scenario, checked 2026-08-28**: entered $40,000, switched to Manual mode, clicked "Go to Scenario Analysis" without editing amounts. Scenario 1 renders header text "Custom" — confirmed via both the rendered page text and the DOM directly: `<span>` reading "Custom" inside a `<div class="bg-[#e8e9e9] px-[10px] py-[4px] rounded-[100px]">`, computed background `rgb(232, 233, 233)`, text color `rgb(113, 119, 119)`.

**Verdict: D011 was wrong, not this repo's code.** That live styling is byte-identical to what's already in `ScenarioAnalysis.tsx` (`bg-[#e8e9e9] px-[10px] py-[4px] rounded-[100px]`, `text-[#717777]`, text "Custom") — this repo's code was a faithful copy of real shipped behavior the whole time. There is no bug to fix in the component. **This entry explicitly supersedes D011** (not a silent edit — D011's text stands unedited above, per this file's append-only rule; this is the correction).

**Corrected model**: "System recommendation — [priority]" and "Custom" are **two real, mutually exclusive shipped badge states** — not "a badge vs. no badge." `ScenarioColumn`'s `isSystemRec` boolean already implements exactly this correctly (`true` → System recommendation pill, `false` → Custom pill). D011's "absence of a provenance badge = manually built, and needs no label" framing does not describe shipped reality; there is always a badge, just one of two kinds.

**Updated**: `CLAUDE.md` §3's glossary row (rewritten to state both badges as real/canonical, cites this entry), §8's Feature 2 spec (the "pairs with no/absent 'System recommendation' badge" phrasing corrected to "pairs with the 'Custom' badge," with an explicit open question flagged: does the future "AI-assisted" badge replace "Custom" for a fund-specific AI-assisted scenario, or render alongside it? — not resolved here, Feature 2 isn't built yet), and the Implementation Map (§9) badge line.

**Baseline re-run after this fix** (docs-only change, no component code touched, since the code needed no fix): Vitest 79/79, Playwright 2/2 — unchanged, as expected.

### D041 — [PD] Narration review sample set generated: `docs/narration-review-sample.md`
One real example per §12 narration coverage category, produced by actually running the narration pipeline (`buildFundResultNarrationInput`/`buildScenarioNarrationInput` + `buildDeterministicFallback`, and `buildNarrationPrompt` for the segment-tone comparison) against real values already in `dev/src/data/sample-dataset.json` — same lots used in `narration.test.ts` (`T-VTSAX-08`, `T-VBTLX-02`, `IRA-VFITX-02`, `IRA-VFITX-06`, `T-VTIAX-07`, `ROTH-VFIAX-07`), no invented figures. Generated via a temporary script (`dev/scratch-generate-samples.ts`, run once with `npx tsx`, deleted immediately after — never committed, confirmed via `git status`). Lives at `docs/narration-review-sample.md`, outside `dev/`'s own source, per instruction — for a human review pass, not consumed by any code.

**No `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is configured in this environment**, so every sample is `buildDeterministicFallback()`'s output (real, computed, not fabricated — but not the live model's actual prose). The file says this plainly at the top and explains how to regenerate it with real model output.

**Two real gaps found in the deterministic fallback while generating this set** (documented in the file itself, not fixed — out of scope for this session's tasks 1–3, which were docs/investigation/sample-generation only):
1. Categories 6 and 7 (allocation toward-target / away-from-target) render **identical text** — `buildDeterministicFallback()` in `dev/src/utils/narrationShared.ts` never reads `input.allocation_impact` at all, only per-fund gain/loss and net tax. The live model would distinguish them correctly (it receives `allocation_impact` in the prompt payload); the plain-text fallback currently can't.
2. Category 4 (ST-LT crossing) combines `st_gain_loss + lt_gain_loss` into a single "realizing a gain of $X" figure without calling out the ST/LT split, even though both are present and non-zero in the input.

Neither is a live-model defect — both are narrowness in the non-AI fallback text specifically. Whoever next touches `buildDeterministicFallback()` should address these; flagging here so they're not rediscovered as new bugs.

### D042 — [Dev] Narration generation made provider-agnostic; Gemini is now the active default, Anthropic preserved as a working alternative
Refines D038's provider specificity, not its core principle. D038 established "narration must come from a real LLM call, never templating" as a permanent, project-wide standing principle (`CLAUDE.md` §1) — that stands unchanged. What D038 got more specific than it needed to was naming Anthropic as *the* provider rather than *a* provider; this entry generalizes the architecture to a swappable interface and changes which provider is active by default.

**Rationale**: cost avoidance at negligible technical cost (the interface extraction is a clean refactor, not a rewrite — the fallback logic, caching layer, and all four touchpoint call sites needed zero changes, confirmed by re-running the full test suite unmodified except for two import-path updates), avoiding single-vendor lock-in for a case-study project, and this is exactly the swappable-generator-interface option that was on the table back at D038 (Option 3, "swappable generator interface — deferred, D038 picked a concrete provider directly instead since a decision had to be made then"). Nothing about this was inevitable or forced — it's a legitimate second look at a tradeoff already decided once, which is exactly what this session's own §0 standing expectations (added post-D033–D037) call for: not treating a settled decision as permanently closed when circumstances genuinely change.

**What was built:**
1. **Interface**: `NarrationGenerator` (`dev/src/server/narrationGenerator.ts`) — one method, `generate(input: NarrationInput): Promise<string>`, throws `NarrationApiError` on failure. `getActiveGenerator()` selects the implementation via `NARRATION_PROVIDER` (env var), defaulting to `'gemini'`.
2. **Shared prompt content extracted**: `dev/src/server/narrationPrompt.ts` now holds `buildNarrationPrompt()` (moved out of the old `narrationEngine.ts`, which is deleted) — the actual English system/tone/touchpoint instructions, provider-agnostic, imported by both adapters. Neither adapter duplicates prompt text.
3. **Anthropic preserved as a real adapter, not deleted**: `dev/src/server/generators/anthropicGenerator.ts` — the exact same Anthropic Messages API call D038/D039 built, refactored to implement the interface. Fully functional; set `NARRATION_PROVIDER=anthropic` to activate it, no other code change needed. Verified via a new test that both adapters fail identically (same `NarrationApiError`) with no key set, and both expose the same `{name, generate}` shape.
4. **New Gemini adapter, made the default**: `dev/src/server/generators/geminiGenerator.ts`. **Model: `gemini-2.5-flash-lite`** — verified live (not assumed) against `ai.google.dev` this session: it's the cheapest currently-GA Gemini tier ($0.10/$0.40 per 1M input/output tokens), cheaper than the newer `gemini-3.5-flash-lite`/`gemini-3.1-flash-lite` GA tiers, which cost more per token despite being newer generations — picked per the task's explicit "cheapest, same reasoning as Haiku over Opus" framing, cost over recency. **Endpoint: the Interactions API** (`POST https://generativelanguage.googleapis.com/v1beta/interactions`), confirmed via three independent live fetches against official `ai.google.dev` pages as the current GA, recommended-for-new-projects endpoint (as of June 2026) — not the older `generateContent` endpoint I'd have defaulted to from training-data memory alone. Request shape `{model, system_instruction, input}`, auth via `x-goog-api-key` header, response text at `steps[].content[].text` where `type: 'model_output'`/`'text'`.
5. **Request shape verified live, empirically, not just from docs**: sent a real request with a placeholder API key (`your-key-here`, from the newly-created `.env.local`) directly to the real Gemini endpoint. Response: `{"error":{"code":400,"message":"API key not valid. Please pass a valid API key.",...}}` — an *authentication*-layer rejection, not a schema/validation error, meaning Google's real API accepted the request's field names and shape and got as far as checking the key. This confirms the endpoint URL, `model`/`system_instruction`/`input` fields are correct; the one piece still unverified is the *success*-path response shape, since no real key exists yet (task 7, next).
6. **Tests confirmed already at the interface boundary, not provider-specific** — checked directly, not assumed: the client-side tests (`dev/src/utils/narration.test.ts`) mock `global.fetch` against `/api/narrate`, which was already provider-agnostic (the client never knew which provider generated the text). No test mocked Anthropic's SDK or endpoint directly, so no test needed to change for this. Added new coverage instead: `getActiveGenerator()` defaults to Gemini, `NARRATION_PROVIDER=anthropic` switches correctly, an unknown provider name throws rather than silently defaulting, both adapters share the same interface shape, both fail identically with no key set.
7. **`.env.local` (gitignored) and `.env.example` (committed) created** at `dev/`, both listing `GEMINI_API_KEY` (required for the active default) and `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` (optional, only read if `NARRATION_PROVIDER=anthropic`) — placeholders only in both files, confirmed `.env.local` is covered by the existing root `.gitignore` (`git check-ignore` confirmed) and `.env.example` is not. This resolves the task flagged two sessions ago and correctly held off on pending this refactor.
8. **`vite.config.ts` updated to actually load `.env.local` for local dev** — a real gap found while validating, not assumed away: Vite's built-in env handling populates `import.meta.env` for client bundles (`VITE_`-prefixed only), it does **not** put arbitrary env vars into `process.env` for the Node process running the dev server. Without fixing this, a user's real key in `.env.local` would silently do nothing under plain `npm run dev` (only `vercel dev` or a real Vercel deployment auto-inject env vars into `process.env`) — breaking the local-dev-parity promise D038/D039 already made for this exact code path. Fixed via `loadEnv()` in `vite.config.ts`, copying `GEMINI_API_KEY`/`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`/`NARRATION_PROVIDER` into `process.env` if not already set. Verified working end-to-end (see point 5 above — that 400 response only happened because the placeholder key actually loaded).

### D044 — [Dev] Tier 1: $0 tax / wrong cost-basis method was a review-tooling and test-fixture bug, not a live app bug — diagnosed, confirmed, fixed
Every sample in D043's review set showed $0 estimated tax and $0 impact, and several showed FIFO where MinTax is the established default. Diagnosed before fixing anything, per instruction.

**Root cause, confirmed by reading the real engine source, not inferred**: the temporary generation script (`dev/scratch-generate-samples.ts`, deleted after each run, never committed) never called `runOptimization()` — it hand-built `FundSaleResult` objects via a local `fundResult()` helper defaulting `accounting_method: 'FIFO'` and `est_tax_gross: 0`, overriding only `est_st_gain_loss`/`est_lt_gain_loss` (real dataset values, via `findLot()`) and, in one case, `accounting_method`. **The same bug was independently present in the committed test file**, `dev/src/utils/narration.test.ts` — its `fundResult()` helper had the identical hardcoded defaults. Confirmed via direct inspection of `dev/src/engine/index.ts`: real `runOptimization()` computes `est_tax_gross = r2(stGain * activeTaxRates.st_rate + ltGain * activeTaxRates.lt_rate)` for taxable-brokerage accounts (genuinely non-zero for any real gain), and automated/most manual selections use `'MinTax'`, not `'FIFO'`, as the method (`// Determine method: MinTax for automated (REQ-OE-005 note...)`). Also confirmed the live app itself was never affected: manually verified in the browser during the original Feature 1 build (D039) that real tax figures (e.g. "$514.63") render correctly end-to-end through the real pipeline — the live touchpoints call `runOptimization()` directly, they never went through the buggy helper. **This is squarely the "script bypasses the real computation path" branch, not the "live app bug" branch** — no separate incident report needed, per instruction.

**One genuine nuance surfaced while diagnosing, not itself a bug**: `est_tax_gross` is correctly `$0` by design for (a) any pure-loss sale in a taxable account (`buildFundResult()` clamps gains to `max(0, gain)` before computing tax — a loss alone produces no positive gross tax at the per-fund level) and (b) any Traditional IRA or Roth IRA sale regardless of gain/loss (`// IRA: ordinary income or tax-free — gross tax not applicable for display`). So "every example shows $0" was the bug; "some examples correctly show $0" is real, verified engine behavior that should NOT be forced non-zero.

**Fixed, in both places:**
1. `narration.test.ts`'s fixtures now call the real engine (`runOptimization()`, manual mode) for every category — via `manualRun()` for fund-level MinTax sales, `automatedRun()` for automated-mode-only behavior (Wait & Save notices, which only populate on `Recommendation`, never `ManualConfiguration`), and `realFundResultForLot()` (manual mode, `specific_lot_identification` + `lot_overrides`) for categories needing one deterministic, exact real lot (5, 9, 11, and the new regression guard). Categories 6/7 (allocation) now build their `SavedScenario` via the real `buildScenarioFromFundResults()` (`dev/src/utils/scenarioBuilder.ts`), not a hand-typed object.
2. **Categories 6 and 7 needed real parameter tuning, verified empirically, not assumed**: a diagnostic dry run (real engine, no API calls) showed that selling equity-and-bonds together (the original category-6 attempt) made allocation *worse*, not better, and that selling only the overweight equity fund (VTSAX alone) is what actually moves toward target (distance-to-target sum 5.55 → 3.27) — while selling only the underweight bond fund (VBTLX alone) moves *away* from target (5.55 → 8.26), which is what category 7 needed. The original category-6/7 parameters had the funds backwards relative to what "toward" and "away" actually require.
3. **Category 8 (Wait & Save) needed a parameter sweep to find real triggering conditions**: `detectWaitAndSave()` isn't exported and only runs inside automated mode; a $200,000 tax-first automated run never touched VTIAX at all (`wait_and_save_notices: []`). Swept amounts (`250k`/`300k`/`350k`) × priorities (`tax-first`/`balance-first`) and found `$350,000` + `balance-first` is the minimum real combination that reaches VTIAX, producing a genuine `wait_and_save_notices` entry (lot `T-VTIAX-07`, 14 days to LT conversion, **$47.39** real tax savings at the 24%/15% rates used here — not the $55.80 the old hand-typed fixture guessed).
4. **Category 5 needed a different lot than originally chosen**: MinTax on VFITX in the Traditional IRA picks a loss lot (`IRA-VFITX-04`) as its most tax-efficient choice, not the gain lot originally intended — conflating this category with loss-harvesting. Switched to `specific_lot_identification` targeting `IRA-VFITX-05` (a different real gain lot than category 11's `IRA-VFITX-02`, to keep the two categories visually distinct) to deterministically get a clean gain example.
5. **New regression-guard test added** (`dev/src/utils/narration.test.ts`, "tax-figure regression guard" describe block): asserts a taxable-brokerage lot with a known real gain produces `est_tax_gross > 0` in the narration input. Would have failed against the old hand-typed fixture helper — this specific failure mode (silently-zeroed tax) can't regress unnoticed again.
6. **`docs/narration-review-sample.md` regenerated** with the corrected real figures (before tier 2's prompt fixes, then once more after — see D045). Same temp-script-then-delete discipline as D041/D043.

**Baseline**: Vitest 85/85 (84 prior + 1 new regression guard), Playwright 2/2.

### D045 — [Dev] Tier 2: five prompt-quality issues fixed in `narrationPrompt.ts`, real output re-verified after each
Addressed only after D044's figures were confirmed correct — fixing prompt wording against wrong underlying figures would have been reviewing noise. All five instructed issues, in `dev/src/server/narrationPrompt.ts`:

1. **Number formatting**: added an explicit instruction — every dollar figure gets a leading `$` and comma thousands separators with two decimals (`$21,780.00`, never `21780`); share counts must read as shares. Real output confirms this now holds throughout (e.g. "$5,000.00," "$440.77," "-0.34%" — previously "21780," "impact percentage of 0").
2. **Market-context time window**: added an explicit constraint that `market_context_return_pct` is a fund-level trailing-12-month statistic as of one fixed dataset-wide reference date, structurally unrelated to any lot's acquisition or sale date, and must never be framed as "since you bought it" or "between acquisition and sale." Real output confirms the fix — category 5's regenerated sample says "the fund has returned 5.48% over the trailing 12 months," not the previous run's false "between the acquisition date of 2014-03-20 and the present."
3. **Field-name echoing**: added an instruction to write as if addressing the account holder directly, never using a field's internal name (banned `impact percentage`, `est tax gross`, `market context return percentage`, `st gain loss` specifically as examples), and to omit any exactly-zero figure that adds no information — with an explicit carve-out that a $0 tax figure caused by IRA/Roth account type IS worth stating (that zero is meaningful, unlike a $0 impact or $0 losses-harvested with nothing to explain). Real output now reads as prose ("tax impact," "shift in portfolio allocation," "trailing 12-month return") rather than paraphrased JSON keys.
4. **Segment tone differentiation**: rewrote segment A's instruns to demand exactly one short (~25-word) sentence with a concrete example, and strengthened segment B's instruction to explicitly require the "why" (tax efficiency, allocation effect) with its own example, plus a direct instruction that a B output reading almost like an A output for the same figures means try again. Verified via the same figures (category 10): A now reads as one terse sentence, B reads as two sentences that explicitly explain the rebalancing rationale — a real, checkable difference, not just nominally different instructions.
5. **Traditional IRA logical gap**: added an instruction not to lead with or simply negate the gain/loss figure for `traditional_IRA` lines, but to state plainly that the *withdrawal amount* — not the gain/loss shown — is what's taxed, as ordinary income. Real output for category 5 now says "the withdrawal amount is what gets taxed, as ordinary income," directly naming what applies instead of only negating what doesn't.

**One new observation surfaced by the fix, not addressed (flagged in the file, per this session's own pattern of flagging rather than silently fixing everything found)**: category 8's four-fund automated recommendation produces one very long run-on sentence — segment A's "exactly one sentence" instruction doesn't scale gracefully to a multi-fund transaction. Left for a future prompt-quality pass; not one of the five issues this task specified.

**`docs/narration-review-sample.md` regenerated one final time** with both tiers' fixes applied together (real figures from D044 + improved prompt from D045) — all 13 real Gemini calls succeeded. Header note rewritten to describe both fixes and point to this entry.

**Final baseline, both tiers complete**: Vitest 85/85, Playwright 2/2.

### D046 — [Dev] Tier 1: MinTax's T-VTSAX-08 selection for the $5,000 Category-1 sale verified correct — minimizes total tax dollars, not rate
Asked to verify, not assume, whether MinTax picking a short-term lot (`T-VTSAX-08`, gain $440.77, tax $105.78) over VTSAX's long-term lots (some dating to 2004) was correct algorithm behavior or a bug.

**Read the actual logic first** (`selectLots()`, `dev/src/engine/index.ts`): MinTax sorts lots ascending by `(current_nav - cost_basis_per_share) / current_nav * taxRate(holding_period, accountType, rates)` — i.e., *embedded gain as a percentage of current value*, multiplied by the applicable rate for that holding period. This is a per-dollar-of-proceeds tax-cost ranking, not a rate-only ranking, and not a literal search over all lot combinations — it's a greedy fill from this ranking. Since a partial sale's cost/gain split is proportional to the lot's own total figures (`partialCost()`), this per-dollar ranking is invariant to sale size — meaning whichever lot ranks best here is the true minimum-tax choice for a same-size sale from any candidate lot, not just an approximation.

**Verified against the real engine, not a manual estimate**: computed what a $5,000 sale from *each* of VTSAX's 9 real taxable lots would produce, using `runOptimization()` with `specific_lot_identification` targeting each lot in turn (real proportional cost split, real tax computation — the same code path Category 1 itself uses, just forced to each lot instead of letting MinTax pick).

| Lot | Acquired | Period | Basis/sh | Gain % of NAV | Tax on $5,000 sale |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| T-VTSAX-08 | 2025-08-15 | ST | $132.40 | 8.82% | **$105.78** ← MinTax's pick, genuinely lowest |
| T-VTSAX-09 | 2025-11-20 | ST | $130.53 | 10.10% | $121.24 |
| T-VTSAX-07 | 2024-11-10 | LT | $117.37 | 19.17% | $143.75 |
| T-VTSAX-06 | 2023-07-15 | LT | $110.20 | 24.10% | $180.78 |
| T-VTSAX-05 | 2020-04-01 | LT | $72.50 | 50.07% | $375.52 |
| T-VTSAX-04 | 2016-09-15 | LT | $62.30 | 57.09% | $428.20 |
| T-VTSAX-03 | 2012-06-10 | LT | $45.80 | 68.46% | $513.43 |
| T-VTSAX-01 | 2004-03-15 | LT | $35.20 | 75.76% | $568.18 |
| T-VTSAX-02 | 2008-11-20 | LT | $25.40 | 82.51% | $618.80 |

**Verdict: correct-but-counterintuitive, not a bug.** `T-VTSAX-08`'s small embedded gain (8.82% of current value, since it was bought recently near today's price) more than offsets its higher short-term rate (24% vs. 15% LT) — every long-term alternative has a large enough embedded gain percentage that its rate advantage doesn't close the gap. The next-closest ST lot (`T-VTSAX-09`) is $15.46 more in tax; every LT lot is at least $37.97 more, rising to $513 more for the oldest, lowest-cost-basis lots.

**Documented in `CLAUDE.md` §5** (the MinTax business rule's new home in the Constraints section) specifically so this doesn't get re-flagged as a suspected bug by someone who hasn't seen this verification — states plainly that MinTax minimizes total dollars, not rate, with the mechanism and this session's concrete numbers as evidence.

No code changed this entry — verification and documentation only.

### D047 — [Dev] Tier 2: category 8's run-on sentence fixed with a structural (not length) rule in segment A's instruction
Segment A's prior instruction ("write exactly ONE short sentence") was a length constraint that didn't scale — a four-fund automated recommendation (category 8) got forced into one long, comma-chained run-on sentence regardless of fund count.

**Fixed in `dev/src/server/narrationPrompt.ts`**: segment A's rule now structures by fund count, not a fixed sentence limit — one short, figure-led sentence per fund for multi-fund transactions, explicitly forbidding chaining funds together with commas/"alongside"/"plus"/"and" into a single sentence. Includes a two-fund example showing two separate sentences, and states plainly: "A four-fund transaction gets four short sentences, not one sentence with four clauses."

**Verified against regenerated real output**, category 8's exact fixture (automated, balance-first, $350,000): now renders as four separate sentences, one per fund (VTSAX, VTIAX, VBTLX, VBIRX), each independently scannable — confirmed by regenerating just this one category and counting sentence boundaries. `docs/narration-review-sample.md`'s category 8 section updated with the corrected sample and a note on the fix.

**Baseline after tiers 1 and 2**: Vitest 85/85, Playwright 2/2.

### D049 — [Dev] Multi-fund (3+) aggregation gap closed — the 12th narration category is now genuinely tested
Closes the gap D048 flagged (found, not fixed, that session — writing a new test was explicitly out of Tier 3's consistency-only scope).

**Reused category 8's existing real fixture** (`automatedRun(TAXABLE, 350000, 'balance-first')` — a real 4-fund automated transaction: VTSAX, VTIAX, VBTLX, VBIRX), per instruction, rather than building new fixture data — it already exercised this case, it just wasn't asserted on as its own concern.

**Added a `describe('multi-fund (3+) aggregation', ...)` block to `dev/src/utils/narration.test.ts`** with three tests, covering exactly the three things asked for:
1. **No cross-fund merging/contamination**: for every real fund in the fixture, the narration input's line for that fund is checked against the engine's own real per-fund figures (`st_gain_loss`, `lt_gain_loss`, `est_tax_gross`, `impact_pct`, `accounting_method`) — asserted equal, not just present. A distinctness check across all funds' `st_gain_loss` values additionally rules out any accidental swap between funds (this fixture's real values happen to all be distinct, so a collision would indicate a mix-up, not coincidence).
2. **Real aggregate figures, computed across funds, not per-fund**: `est_net_tax`/`effective_rate` are asserted equal to the engine's real portfolio-level netted figures (`rec.est_net_tax`/`rec.effective_rate`) — with an explicit sanity check that this is *not* the same as a naive sum of each fund's own `est_tax_gross`, proving the netting logic is actually exercised, not accidentally bypassed by a fixture where summing and netting happen to coincide. `losses_harvested` is independently recomputed from the same real per-fund figures and checked against the narration input's value, with a sanity check that at least one real fund in this fixture actually contributes a loss (otherwise the test wouldn't be exercising the harvesting-sum logic at all).
3. **D047's structural fix actually applies to this input**: `buildNarrationPrompt()`'s system prompt for this multi-fund input is checked for the literal per-fund structural instruction text ("ONE short sentence PER FUND," "A four-fund transaction gets four short sentences").

**Deliberate boundary, stated in the test's own comment as instructed**: this suite does not assert on exact live-generated prose, and does not make a live provider call to verify point 3's real-world effect — consistent with how the rest of `narration.test.ts` has always worked (CLAUDE.md §7, D038 rule 4, D042: tests mock at the interface/HTTP boundary, never hit a live provider). The empirical proof that Gemini's actual output for this exact fixture renders as four separate sentences was already established in D047 (`docs/narration-review-sample.md`, category 8's regenerated sample) — a human-reviewed artifact, not an automated assertion, because model prose wording isn't guaranteed stable run to run even under an unchanged instruction. What's asserted here instead is the code-level guarantee that the instruction enabling that behavior is actually present in the prompt for this specific multi-fund case — the right boundary for an automated regression test, versus a one-time empirical spot-check for the prose itself.

**`CLAUDE.md` §12 updated**: narration category count corrected from "11, with a flagged gap" back to a genuine 12, with the new category's description and a pointer to this entry. §9 Implementation Map's category count also corrected (11 → 12).

**Baseline**: Vitest 88/88 (85 + 3 new), Playwright 2/2.

### D051 — [PM] §1's stated project objective corrected — was backwards, framing the process experiment as primary and the AI features as secondary
**This corrects an actual error, not a refinement.** §1 previously stated the project's "own thesis" as testing whether PM/PD/Dev rigor survives without a phase-gated relay, with the two AI features presented underneath it as scope. That had it backwards: this project's real, primary objective is learning to design **AI-enabled** applications — extending an already-built, already-deployed application (the original Vanguard Sell & Rebalance pipeline project) with AI capabilities that extend the *end user's* capabilities. That original project's own focus was the opposite category, **AI-assisted** design and development — using AI tools to accelerate building the application itself, across simulated PM/PD/Dev roles. One puts AI inside the *process*, for the builder; this one puts AI inside the *product*, for the user. The single-file-pair context mechanism (this file + `DECISIONS.md`) is real, and is documented throughout both — but it's a secondary, coincidental learning track that emerged while doing the real work, not the reason the real work exists.

**Notable, and worth being precise about rather than smoothing over**: this correction did not come from any internal self-check catching the drift — nothing in this session's own review process flagged §1 as wrong before being told. It came from the person holding the actual objective restating it directly. That is itself a real, live instance of the exact failure mode this file/`DECISIONS.md` pair exists to prevent: a stated thesis quietly drifting from the actual intent behind the work, undetected until the person who holds the intent corrects it from outside. Logged plainly rather than folded into routine documentation-maintenance language, per instruction.

**Fixed**: §1's thesis paragraph replaced with the corrected framing, worded precisely as given rather than paraphrased. Checked the rest of §1 and the rest of the file for any other place stating or implying the process experiment as the project's main point — found none; §8's existing "the project's goal is depth of embedded-AI expertise for a case study" (D010–D013) is already consistent with the corrected framing and needed no change. Not a full Tier-3-style consistency pass — a targeted check, per instruction, since nothing else turned up looking actually wrong.

**No code changed this entry** — documentation-only correction to `CLAUDE.md` §1. Baseline unaffected; not re-run for a docs-only diff (same precedent as D048).

### D052 — [Dev] Feature 2 self-audit found two documented business rules the base application never actually enforces — recorded as a pre-existing gap, not fixed here
Before writing any Feature 2 code, checked whether the completeness-validation logic Feature 2 needs (SpecID disallowed for Traditional IRA, sell amount can't exceed position size — both stated as binding in `CLAUDE.md` §5) already exists somewhere reusable, per this session's kickoff self-audit. It doesn't — and more specifically, **it doesn't exist at all, anywhere, not even inside the UI**, which is a different and more significant finding than "logic exists but needs extracting."

**Checked directly, not assumed:**
- `dev/src/components/CostBasisDialog.tsx` — the actual dialog a Manual-mode user sees when choosing a cost-basis method. Its `METHODS` list unconditionally offers all five methods (MinTax, HIFO, FIFO, SpecID, AvgCost) with no `account_type` parameter, no IRA check, no filtering of any kind. A user can select SpecID on a Traditional IRA holding today in the live UI with no warning or block.
- `dev/src/utils/methods.ts` — the `CostBasisMethod ↔ AccountingMethod` mapping. Pure lookup tables, no validation logic, no account-type awareness.
- `dev/src/engine/index.ts` (`selectLots()`, `buildFundResult()`, `runOptimization()`) — accepts whatever `accounting_method` and sell amount it's given and computes a result; no guard rejects `specific_lot_identification` for a `traditional_IRA` account, and no guard checks a requested sell amount against the fund's actual position size before selecting lots.
- `dev/src/pages/FundSelectionManual2.tsx` — confirmed the only amount-related UI logic is a "sell all shares" convenience that caps a *checkbox-driven* full-balance sell at the real balance; a manually typed amount exceeding the position is not blocked anywhere before it would reach the engine.

**This is a real, pre-existing gap in the base application, not something introduced by or in scope to fix through Feature 2.** It's recorded here specifically so it isn't mistaken later for "already-compliant behavior that Feature 2 can assume and build on" — it can't; Feature 2 has to build both checks itself, from nothing, as new validation. Not fixing the live Manual-mode UI to add these guards now — that's a separate, larger change (affecting an existing, working screen) outside this task's backend-only scope, and the user explicitly confirmed proceeding straight to the build rather than opening that up here.

**Implication for the Feature 2 build that follows this entry:** the interpret/clarify/confirm loop's own validation step is the *first* place either rule will actually be enforced anywhere in this codebase — built as a small, standalone validator (not copied from or duplicating a UI implementation, since none exists), covering both the SpecID/Traditional-IRA refusal and the amount-exceeds-position catch called out in §8/§12's coverage categories.

### D053 — [PM/Dev] Feature 2 kickoff self-audit resolved: conversation state is client-side/ephemeral; interpretation uses a sibling interface, not `NarrationGenerator`
Two more of the three self-audit questions from this session's kickoff, resolved before any build code was written.

**Conversation state — asked, not assumed, per instruction that this was a real gap neither file addressed.** Confirmed with the user: client-side only, ephemeral — conversation state (the interpret → clarify → clarify-again → confirm sequence) lives in local component/store state for the lifetime of the assistant-panel session and is not persisted to `localStorage` or anywhere server-side. Reasoning, per the user directly: nothing of value exists in an *unconfirmed* exchange — the moment a request is actually confirmed and run, its result inherits the app's existing persisted state (the scenario/store mechanisms that already exist) the same way a Manual-mode edit does, so persisting the in-progress conversation itself buys nothing worth the added complexity. This is also the only answer consistent with the rest of the app's architecture (no backend DB anywhere, `narrate.ts` fully stateless, the existing Zustand store is the only state mechanism) — the inference was right, but per instruction it was confirmed rather than defaulted silently.
