**Interpretation output shape — resolved by direct inspection, no ambiguity requiring a question.** Read `dev/src/server/narrationGenerator.ts`: `NarrationGenerator`'s contract is `generate(input: NarrationInput): Promise<string>` — structured figures in, prose out, unconditionally. Feature 2 needs the reverse direction plus a fork the existing contract has no room for: natural language (plus conversation history) in, and *either* a structured candidate engine-input object *or* a natural-language clarifying question out. Extending `NarrationGenerator` to cover this would overload one interface with two incompatible response shapes for two different consumers — not a refinement, a different contract. **Decision: a new, sibling interface** (`WhatIfInterpreter` or equivalent), with its own Gemini/Anthropic adapters, reusing only the *pattern* already proven for narration — env-var-driven provider selection (mirroring `NARRATION_PROVIDER`), and testing at the interface/HTTP boundary rather than mocking a provider SDK (D038 rule 4, D042) — not the `NarrationGenerator` type or its adapters directly. The two features' generation code stays fully independent; only the architectural pattern is shared, per instruction to check whether the plumbing should be shared without duplicating something that doesn't actually fit.

No code changed by this entry itself — decisions only, ahead of the build entry that follows.

### D054 — [Dev] Feature 2 backend built: interpret/clarify/confirm loop, real engine invocation, all 9 §12 categories as real tests
Backend only, per this session's instruction — the assistant-panel UI is a separate, later task; nothing built here requires it to be tested. Built in the order specified: the simplest coverage category (a clean, unambiguous, fund-specific delta) working end to end against the real engine first, then the clarify branch, then the harder refusal/disambiguation/objective-routing cases.

**What was built** (full paths recorded in `CLAUDE.md` §9, not repeated here per that section's own rule):
- `dev/src/utils/whatIfShared.ts` — isomorphic types (`WhatIfCandidate`, `WhatIfInterpretationInput/Result`, `WhatIfTurn`) and `buildPortfolioReferenceContext()`, the narrow grounding data (funds/lots for the active account only — no cost basis, no tax figures) the interpreter is allowed to reference. `WhatIfCandidateFundSelection` is a type alias for the engine's own `ManualFundSelection`, not a parallel redefinition, since the shapes are identical (D009's "no duplication" principle applied to types, not just logic).
- `dev/src/server/whatIfPrompt.ts` — the provider-agnostic prompt, per D053's sibling-interface decision. Carries the core boundary ("never state, invent, or infer" an unstated value → must clarify), the MinTax-default-when-unspecified carve-out (not a guessed financial fact, since it matches this app's own existing default elsewhere), the SpecID/Traditional-IRA and general-advice refusal instructions, and the amount-exceeds-balance clarify instruction — all as one shared system prompt string, mirroring narrationPrompt.ts's shape.
- `dev/src/server/whatIfResponseParser.ts` — structural-only parsing of the model's JSON response into one of the three result shapes (clarify/refuse/confirm), including markdown-code-fence stripping. Deliberately separate from business-rule validation.
- `dev/src/server/whatIfInterpreter.ts` + `dev/src/server/generators/geminiInterpreter.ts` (default) / `anthropicInterpreter.ts` (working alternative) — `WHATIF_PROVIDER` env var, defaults to Gemini, mirrors `NARRATION_PROVIDER`'s pattern exactly but kept as an independent setting.
- `dev/src/utils/whatIfValidation.ts` — the validator D052 flagged as needing to be built from nothing: grounding checks (rejects a hallucinated fund_id/lot_id), the SpecID/Traditional-IRA refusal, the amount-exceeds-position-size catch, and completeness checks (missing amount, missing optimization priority, empty selection list). Returns a `kind: 'refuse' | 'clarify'` distinction — a hard rule violation or hallucination refuses outright (no amount of follow-up fixes it), while a fixable gap like an over-large amount routes back to a clarifying question instead, restated in plain language with the real balance.
- `dev/src/utils/whatIfCandidateToParams.ts` — pure reshaping onto `OptimizationParams`, no calculation, same role as `narrationBuilders.ts`.
- `dev/src/utils/whatIf.ts` — the orchestration: `interpretWhatIfTurn()` (browser, calls `/api/interpret`), `interpretWhatIfTurnWith()` (server/test entry point, takes an injected interpreter), and `confirmWhatIfCandidate()` (the confirm step — validates once more, then calls `runOptimization()` directly). Every "confirm" result is re-validated against the real reference data before being trusted, downgrading to "refuse" or "clarify" per the validator's `kind` — this is what makes the core boundary architectural rather than a prompt instruction a model could ignore, the same reasoning CD-4.2's enforcement already established for narration.
- `dev/api/interpret.ts` — the Vercel function, with the same defense-in-depth request-shape check `narrate.ts` uses. Dev-server parity added to `dev/vite.config.ts` alongside `/api/narrate` (kept as two independent middlewares rather than one generalized factory, so either endpoint's dev wiring can change without touching the other).
- `dev/.env.example` updated with `WHATIF_PROVIDER` documentation, same pattern as `NARRATION_PROVIDER`.

**Test discipline, applied "more directly" than D044's original narration case, per this session's instruction**: `dev/src/utils/whatIf.test.ts` never independently reconstructs an expected engine result. Every category that reaches "confirm" asserts on the structured candidate object first, then runs that *exact* candidate through the real engine (`confirmWhatIfCandidate()` → `runOptimization()`) and asserts on the real output — e.g. category 1's real MinTax tax figure, category 4's real FIFO/HIFO accounting methods surviving into the engine's own `fund_results`, category 5's real Roth-IRA $0 tax. Categories 6 and 7 (the two refusal-shaped cases) deliberately mock an interpreter that gets it *wrong* — returns a "confirm" with SpecID on a Traditional IRA, or an amount exceeding the fund's real balance — specifically to prove `whatIfValidation.ts` is what actually stops it, not just the prompt text neither of these mocks even tries to follow. Real dataset values used throughout, cross-checked against the dataset directly (VBIRX's real $84,402.00 taxable balance for the amount-exceeds-position category; VBIRX/VBTLX both literally have "Bond" in their fund name for the ambiguous-reference category, despite being different asset classes for allocation purposes — a genuine ambiguity, not a contrived one).

**What's mocked, stated explicitly per this project's established pattern (CLAUDE.md §7, D038 rule 4, D042, D049)**: this suite makes no live call to Gemini or Anthropic. The *interpretation* step (natural language → structured result) is exercised through hand-authored test doubles standing in for "what a correctly-behaving model would return," the same role `global.fetch` mocking plays for narration — this tests the orchestration, the validator, and the real engine integration exhaustively, and does not attempt to verify that a live model actually produces good interpretations of free-form English, since that would require a live call outside this suite's established boundary.

**Baseline**: Vitest 121/121 (88 prior + 33 new), Playwright 2/2, `tsc --noEmit` clean. `CLAUDE.md` §9's Implementation Map and §12's what-if coverage section both updated in place (not appended, per §9's own rule) with the real file list and the "what's mocked and why" note.

**Not built, explicitly out of scope for this task**: the assistant-panel UI in Scenario Analysis, and the "AI-assisted" badge (§8's still-open question from D040, unchanged). The pre-existing base-application gap this build's self-audit found (SpecID/Traditional-IRA and amount-exceeds-position enforced nowhere before this) is recorded separately in D052 and was not retrofitted into the live Manual-mode UI — only Feature 2's own new validator closes it, for Feature 2's own code path.

### D055 — [Dev] Feature 2 backend verification, pre-UI: two prior fixes confirmed by mutation testing, real live-model prose read and one genuine bug found/fixed, validator adversarially boundary-tested and one genuine bug found/fixed
Requested before touching the UI, at the same rigor Feature 1 was eventually held to (D044–D047). All three checks below found something real — none was a clean pass.

**1. The two "fixed in passing" bugs, verified by actually breaking the underlying behavior and re-running, not by re-reading the diff:**
- *Test 6 (SpecID/Traditional-IRA refusal), before the fix*: the assertion was `expect(() => confirmWhatIfCandidate(badMock as ..., ...)).toBeDefined()` — `badMock` (the interpreter test double, not a candidate) was passed where a `WhatIfCandidate` belonged, and `.toBeDefined()` checks that the arrow-function *reference* exists, which is trivially always true — the function was never actually invoked, so nothing inside it (the validator, the SpecID/IRA rule, anything) was ever exercised. Concretely: this assertion would still have been green even if `whatIfValidation.ts`'s entire SpecID/Traditional-IRA check were deleted. Verified this directly: temporarily replaced the real check with `if (false) { ... }` and re-ran the corrected test (`confirmWhatIfCandidate(badCandidate, ...)).toThrow(WhatIfError)`) — it failed exactly as expected (`expected 'confirm' to be 'refuse'`), then passed again once the check was restored. That's the actual proof the corrected version exercises the real rule, not a different accidental pass.
- *Test 8 (ambiguous fund reference), before the fix*: filtered `taxableRef.funds` by `asset_class === 'domestic_bonds'`, expecting `['VBIRX', 'VBTLX']`. This is not a case of "silently confirming the wrong thing" — it failed loudly on first run (`expected ['VBTLX'] to deeply equal ['VBIRX', 'VBTLX']`), because VBIRX's real asset_class is `short_term_reserves`, not `domestic_bonds` — only one fund matched. The risk was in how it could have been "fixed" carelessly: narrowing the expected array to `['VBTLX']` would have made the assertion pass while silently converting the test's own claim from "this is a genuine two-way ambiguity" to "there is exactly one bond-asset-class fund" — the opposite of what category 8 exists to demonstrate, and the mock's clarify question naming both funds would have kept passing regardless, since it never depended on the grounding check. The actual fix instead switched the filter to `fund_name.includes('Bond')` — correct because a user says "my bond fund" based on a fund's real display name, not its internal allocation-bucket field, and both VBIRX ("Short-Term Bond Index") and VBTLX ("Total Bond Market Index") genuinely contain "Bond" in their real names. Re-ran the original `asset_class`-based line against the current test file to reproduce the exact original failure as independent confirmation, then restored the real fix.

**2. Real generated interpretation prose read cold, via a temporary script calling the live Gemini API directly** (`dev/scratch-whatif-review.ts`, run via `npx tsx`, deleted immediately after, never committed — same discipline as D041/D043/D044/D045/D047's review-sample scripts). Covered the three requested cases plus a regression check:
- Objective-shaped ("I want to reduce my tax impact — raise $20,000"): correct and clear both times it was generated — "optimized for the lowest tax impact across your taxable brokerage account holdings."
- Vague/relative quantity ("Sell a bit more of my bonds"): correct — named both real bond funds by name and ticker (VBIRX, VBTLX) and asked for both the fund and the amount, better than the hand-authored test-fixture summary it stands in for.
- **Multi-turn/inline-lot-detail found a real, live-verified bug**: turn 1 ("I want to use SpecID to sell some VFIAX from my Roth IRA") was wrongly **refused** — *"Specific lot identification is not available for a Roth IRA account"* — and turn 2, given that wrong turn-1 history, refused again while misnaming the account entirely — *"...is not available for a Traditional IRA account"* (the account was Roth IRA throughout). The SpecID/Traditional-IRA rule was generalizing to "any IRA" and, worse, defaulting to a canned "Traditional IRA" phrase regardless of which account was actually in play. This is exactly the category of bug structural testing (asserting on the candidate object) cannot catch, since it never reaches a candidate — only reading the actual prose surfaced it.

**Diagnosed and fixed in `dev/src/server/whatIfPrompt.ts`**: the account_type value and the SpecID rule were far apart in one long joined system-prompt string, and the rule's wording ("on a Traditional IRA account... regardless of phrasing") never told the model what to do for the *other* two account types, leaving it to over-generalize. Rewrote the prompt to (a) state the account's exact `account_type` string immediately, with an explicit note that `taxable_brokerage`/`traditional_IRA`/`roth_IRA` are not interchangeable; (b) restate the account_type value directly inside the SpecID rule itself, with an explicit instruction that SpecID is fully available for `roth_IRA` and `taxable_brokerage` and that "Traditional IRA" must not be mentioned for those two; (c) added a re-check instruction ("before writing a refusal for this reason, re-read this account's actual account_type value above and confirm it is literally 'traditional_IRA'"). Re-ran the same live script after the fix: turn 1 now correctly asks a clarifying question instead of refusing, turn 2 now correctly produces a full `confirm` candidate (and the model derived `targetSaleAmount: 21932` itself from the lot's real `current_value`, matching the dataset exactly) — and a regression check against the real Traditional IRA case confirms the refusal still fires correctly there, unchanged. Fix verified live, empirically, both directions, not assumed from re-reading the new prompt text.

**3. Adversarial boundary-testing of the validator found a second real bug**, this one via direct code inspection prompted by the request rather than a live call: `AMOUNT_EPSILON = 0.01`, intended purely as floating-point-noise tolerance, was wide enough to also swallow a genuine one-cent overage — `sell_amount > current_balance + AMOUNT_EPSILON` is `false` when `sell_amount` is *exactly* one cent over the balance (both sides equal), so a request for $84,402.01 against a real $84,402.00 VBIRX balance would have incorrectly validated as OK and been passed to the engine, silently under-enforcing the exact rule this validator exists to be. **Fixed** by shrinking the tolerance to `0.001` (well under one cent, still absorbs genuine float noise like `+1e-10`). **Six new adversarial tests added** to `dev/src/utils/whatIf.test.ts` (`describe('boundary cases — almost valid, not quite', ...)`): exact-balance amount (valid), one-cent-over amount (invalid — this is the case that would have failed to catch the bug), sub-cent float noise (still valid), exact-lot-shares (valid), one-hundredth-of-a-share-over (invalid), a non-SpecID method on a Traditional IRA (valid — proving the rule is method-specific, not account-wide), and SpecID on a plain taxable brokerage account (valid — proving the rule names Traditional IRA specifically, not "any non-Roth account"). Verified the new one-cent-over test actually catches the bug by temporarily restoring `AMOUNT_EPSILON = 0.01` and confirming that specific test fails (`expected valid: true` where `false` was required), then restoring the fix and confirming it passes again — the same before/after proof pattern used for check 1 above.

**Baseline after all fixes**: Vitest 128/128 (121 prior + 7 new: 6 boundary tests plus one regression-guard-shaped assertion folded into the existing suite), Playwright 2/2, `tsc --noEmit` clean. No leftover scratch or `.bak` files (checked directly, not assumed).

**Why this matters beyond the two bugs themselves**: both were real defects that "the tests pass" and "the structural candidate looks right" would have missed entirely — one only showed up in live model prose no automated assertion was reading, the other only showed up at an exact numeric boundary no prior test happened to sit on. This is the concrete version of the general lesson Feature 1 needed two full review passes to learn (D044–D047): structural correctness, prose correctness, and boundary correctness are three different claims, and passing tests for one says nothing about the other two.

### D056 — [PD/Dev] Feature 2 UI built: the assistant panel in Scenario Analysis, badge composition resolved, two real bugs found and fixed via live use
Self-audit resolved four questions before writing any UI code, per this session's kickoff instruction — none required stopping to ask:

1. **Placement (D006)**: the literal "Add scenario" slot in `ScenarioAnalysis.tsx` is a 160px dashed box — too narrow for a chat surface with an inline lot table. Resolved as: the box stays as the trigger (D006's "sits in/over"), opening a full drawer rather than expanding in place; the existing manual-navigation behavior is kept as a second, equal option inside the same slot (D007 — the assistant is an additional edit tool, not a replacement).
2. **Design precedent**: checked PDB 05 directly rather than assuming none exists. Found real precedent this project hadn't used yet — a named `--drawer-width: 30rem (480px)` token ("Side drawer width") and elevation tokens for drawers/modals, plus this same tool's own "Q5 inline drawer" pattern (a text link that expands to reveal an editor) — the collapsible affordance D006 describes. No chat-specific component exists anywhere in the design system or the live previous-project app (correct — this is the first conversational surface either has ever had), so the drawer container has real precedent, the message-list content inside is necessarily new. Built the drawer at the real 480px token width, reusing this codebase's own scrim/overlay pattern (`CostBasisDialog.tsx`, `ScenarioAnalysis.tsx`'s `DeleteDialog`).
3. **Badge composition (D011/D040)**: resolved the open question left by D040 — one pill, compound text ("AI-assisted — Custom" / "AI-assisted — [priority] optimized"), not two adjacent badges. No precedent anywhere in this design system for stacking two badges, and the glossary's own worked examples (§3) are single compound phrases. `SavedScenario` gained an additive `ai_assisted?: boolean` field; `ScenarioColumn`'s existing badge text switches on it, keeping the existing pill color as the System-recommendation/Custom signal.
4. **Structured lot content**: `FundSelectionManualLot.tsx`'s real lot table is ~800px across six columns — doesn't fit a 480px drawer. Built a purpose-built 4-column compact variant (lot id, holding period, shares, value) instead of reusing that component — this maps exactly onto `WhatIfFundLotReference`, the grounding shape the backend already exposes, so no new data plumbing was needed. Picking a lot composes a natural-language utterance ("Sell all 40 shares of lot X") and sends it through the existing `interpretWhatIfTurn()` call — no new backend path, per this task's UI-only scope.

**What was built** (paths recorded in `CLAUDE.md` §9): `dev/src/components/WhatIfPanel.tsx` (the panel — message list, confirm/clarify/refuse rendering, the inline lot table, the text input), wired into `ScenarioAnalysis.tsx` via a new `✨ Ask the assistant` link inside the existing "Add scenario" slot. `dev/tests/whatif-panel.spec.ts` — the first Playwright test of the whole conversational loop through real UI (mocks only `/api/interpret`, same HTTP-boundary-only mocking policy as every other test in this project — CLAUDE.md §7, D038 rule 4).

**Root-level `.claude/launch.json` added** — needed to preview the app at all via the Browser tool from the repo root; not a reversal of D020 (which was about `dev/`-only bring-forward scope for the initial bootstrap commit, not a standing objection to a root launch config existing later).

**Live verification, per instruction, not trusted from a green suite alone — found two real bugs, both fixed and re-verified live:**
1. **The browser-preview tool's own managed dev server could not reach the internet** (`Gemini API request failed: fetch failed` — genuine network-level failure, not an auth error) — confirmed this was environment-specific, not a code defect, by starting the exact same dev server directly via the terminal instead (full network access, same as the D055 scratch script) and confirming real Gemini calls succeeded identically through both `/api/narrate` and `/api/interpret`. Noting this here so a future session doesn't waste time debugging "the API doesn't work" when it's really "this particular preview mechanism can't reach the internet, use a directly-started server instead."
2. **A confirmed request silently failed to save once 3 scenarios already existed** (the store's `REQ-SC-001` cap correctly no-ops and logs a console warning, but the panel had no knowledge of this and unconditionally showed "Confirmed" / "Added to Scenario Analysis" regardless) — found by actually filling the cap and confirming a fourth request live, not by reasoning about the code. **Fixed**: `WhatIfPanel` now takes a `scenarioCount` prop and checks it against the real cap before claiming success, showing an honest "you already have 3 scenarios saved" message instead when it's full. Re-verified live: the false-success message is gone, replaced by the accurate one.
3. **A second, unrelated request got merged into the first, already-executed one** — after confirming "Sell $3,000 of VBTLX" (applied immediately), asking "Sell $2,000 of VTIAX" produced a summary combining *both* funds, because the conversation history sent to the interpreter still included the first (already-completed) exchange with no signal that it had already been acted on. Found by sending two independent real requests back-to-back live, not by unit testing (Vitest's fixtures never chain two calls through a shared, evolving history the way real use does). **Fixed**: a confirmed request now clears the turn history sent to the interpreter (the visible chat log is untouched) — a completed transaction starts a fresh conversational slate, so the next utterance is read as independent rather than additive. Re-verified live: the second confirm now reads "You are selling $2,000 of Vanguard Total International Stock Index Fund Admiral Shares (VTIAX)," not a combined two-fund summary.

**Full live walkthrough, all three required interaction shapes, via a real Gemini call each time**: (a) clean confirm — "Sell $5,000 of VTSAX" → correct plain-language summary → confirmed → real engine picks lot `T-VTSAX-08`, real $105.78 tax, matching D046's independently-verified figure exactly; (b) clarify → multi-turn inline lot detail — "I want to use SpecID to sell some VTSAX" correctly triggered the validator's own clarify (the model tried to jump straight to a confirm without naming a lot; the code-level validator caught it, not just the prompt — direct live proof the D052/D055 architecture works, not just a unit-test claim) → the compact lot table rendered all 9 real VTSAX lots correctly → picking one composed and sent the follow-up utterance automatically → correct confirm card → confirmed successfully; (c) the scenario-cap and cross-turn-bleed cases above.

**Baseline**: Vitest 128/128 (unchanged — no backend code touched), Playwright 3/3 (2 prior + 1 new), `tsc --noEmit` clean.

**Not built, unchanged from D054**: nothing further deferred — this closes out Feature 2's originally-scoped UI. Still open, deliberately: nothing; D040's badge question is resolved (point 3 above).

### D057 — [Dev] Account switching enabled — genuinely not a contained fix: found and fixed a systemic, pre-existing tax-computation bug reachable for the first time, plus several account-display bugs; D052's gap confirmed now live; the Roth IRA SpecID claim finally earned at the product level
Implements the fix Part A (this session, prior entry) diagnosed: the three account rows (Taxable Brokerage / Traditional IRA / Roth IRA) on Fund Selection had no click handler anywhere, in either mode, and `setActiveAccountId` was never called from any component. Asked explicitly to verify, not assume, whether wiring it up was actually as contained as that diagnosis predicted. **It was not.**

**1. Wired up, in both modes:**
- `FundSelectionAutomated.tsx`: all three rows now call a `switchAccount()` handler; a new effect re-runs `runOptimization()` when `activeAccountId` changes (previously only ran on mount), otherwise switching left the previous account's stale recommendation on screen. Split the previously-overloaded `taxableAcct` variable (was silently doing double duty as both "the Taxable Brokerage account" and "whichever account is active") into `activeAcct` and `taxableAcct`.
- `FundSelectionManual2.tsx`: fully restructured the account-row block. Previously: a permanently-"active, expanded" row hardcoded to Taxable Brokerage regardless of `activeAccountId`, plus two hardcoded accordion rows (Traditional IRA, Roth IRA) whose `RadioDot` was hardcoded `false` and whose click handler only toggled a read-only lot preview (`toggleAccount`) — never a real switch. Now: the "expanded" row generically shows whichever account is active (label, masked number, balance, allocation all derived from `activeAccountId`), and the other two render generically via `otherAccounts.map()`, each switching on click. The read-only preview-without-switching accordion behavior is deliberately dropped — once an account is genuinely reachable, the real editable table is strictly more useful than a read-only preview of it. Switching clears the in-progress manual session (`clearManualSession()`) — necessary because `manualActiveFundIds`/`manualAppliedAmountsCents`/etc. are keyed by fund_id (ticker) alone, and the same ticker (VBTLX) is a real, distinct holding in two different accounts (taxable and Traditional IRA) — without clearing, switching would silently bleed one account's in-progress amount into another account's identically-named fund.
- `FundSelectionManualLot.tsx`'s own copy of the same three-row block was deliberately left as a read-only accordion, not wired to switch — this is the SpecID lot-detail sub-view for one specific fund already mid-edit; switching accounts away from that context mid-edit doesn't correspond to any real user intent here, unlike the two top-level entry pages.

**2. The systemic bug, found by walking through all three accounts on every surface as instructed, not by assuming the happy path**: every dollar-tax computation that goes beyond a single fund's own `est_tax_gross` — portfolio-level "EST. NET TAX," the FIFO-comparison "you saved $X" banner, Transaction History's per-fund tax display — independently recomputes `stGain * st_rate + ltGain * lt_rate` capital-gains math, and **none of these ever checked account type**, unlike `buildFundResult()`'s already-correct per-fund gate. This was invisible until now because no automated run, manual scenario, order confirmation, or transaction had ever been built against a non-taxable account before — Traditional IRA and Roth IRA were simply unreachable. The very first live automated run against Roth IRA showed a fabricated **$1,818.30 "EST. NET TAX"** and a fabricated **"↓ you saved $1,090.98 compared to selling without optimization"** banner, on an account that owes zero capital-gains tax under any method. Found and fixed at every site (each gated to the pattern `buildFundResult()` already established — `account.account_type === 'taxable_brokerage' ? computed : 0`), verified live after each fix:
  1. `engine/index.ts` — `runOptimization()`'s automated-mode portfolio-level `estNetTax` netting.
  2. `scenarioBuilder.ts` — `buildScenarioFromFundResults()`'s manual-mode `est_net_tax` (hoisted the account lookup earlier in the function to gate this).
  3. `FundSelectionManual2.tsx` — the Summary Banner's own independent net-tax computation (this is the one that showed the fabricated $1,818.30 live).
  4. `FundSelectionManualLot.tsx` — four separate copies of the same computation (per-fund banner on method change, on mount, on lot-shares commit, and the cross-fund `combinedBanner` useMemo), plus a fifth, the per-fund gross-tax display in the expanded lot view.
  5. `OrderConfirmation.tsx` — the manual-mode branch's `estNetTax`, and separately the FIFO-baseline comparison (`fifoTax`) that fed the fabricated "you saved $X" claim into `TransactionRecord.tax_savings_vs_fifo`.
  6. `TransactionHistory.tsx` — the per-fund `estTax` shown in past-transaction cards.
  Confirmed via `narration.test.ts`'s category 6 test failing correctly (`expected 0 to be greater than 0`) the moment the `scenarioBuilder.ts` fix landed on a fixture that hadn't been passing `activeAccountId` — fixed the fixture to pass `TAXABLE` explicitly rather than relying on an implicit default, since the fixture genuinely is taxable and should say so.

**3. Account-display bugs, also found only once non-taxable accounts became reachable:**
  - `OrderConfirmation.tsx` showed the literal raw enum string `"roth_IRA ...8148"` as the order summary header instead of a friendly label — its fallback for a non-taxable account type was the raw `account_type` value itself. Extracted a single shared `accountTypeLabel()` helper into `utils/format.ts` (replacing a duplicate local copy that had grown in `FundSelectionManual2.tsx`) so this mapping exists in exactly one place.
  - `TransactionRecord` (like `SavedScenario` before D057) never tracked which account a transaction was executed against — `ExecutionSummary.tsx` and `TransactionHistory.tsx` both hardcoded `taxable_brokerage`/looked up the taxable account unconditionally for settlement-fund masked numbers and narration account-type framing, meaning a Roth IRA transaction's Execution Summary showed the *taxable* account's masked number as the settlement destination, and Transaction History showed one single page-level masked number for every row regardless of which account each historical transaction was actually against. Added `account_id`/`account_type` to `TransactionRecord` (same additive pattern as `SavedScenario.account_id`/`account_type`), set at submission time in `OrderConfirmation.tsx`, read back correctly in both downstream pages — `TransactionHistory.tsx` now resolves the masked number per-transaction-card, not as one shared constant.

**4. D052 gap confirmed now live-reachable, priority genuinely changed, not fixed here (out of scope for this task's explicit list)**: with the selector working, opened `CostBasisDialog` on VBTLX in a real, active Traditional IRA account in Manual mode. It still offers "Specific identification (SpecID)" completely unconditionally — no warning, no disabling, nothing distinguishing it from the other four methods. **This is no longer a theoretical, unreachable gap** — a real user can reach this exact dialog, select SpecID on a Traditional IRA holding, and proceed toward building a scenario the app's own binding rule (CLAUDE.md §5) says must be refused. Feature 2's assistant still correctly refuses this (D052–D055's validator), but the UI path for a manually-built scenario has no equivalent guard at all. Flagging this with real urgency for whoever picks it up next — this is a live, shippable-as-is defect, not a background note.

**5. The Roth IRA SpecID product-level claim, finally earned rather than asserted**: D056 flagged, correctly, that its own "live-verified Roth IRA SpecID" claim was backend-only (a standalone script calling the interpreter directly, bypassing the UI, store, and account context entirely) because there was no way to reach a Roth-IRA-scoped assistant through the actual product. With switching now real: selected Roth IRA on Fund Selection → navigated to Scenario Analysis (activeAccountId persists across routes, as global store state) → opened the assistant → "I want to use SpecID to sell some VFIAX" → correctly clarified asking which lot → real compact lot table rendered all 7 real ROTH-VFIAX lots → picked lot ROTH-VFIAX-07 via "Sell all" → correct confirm card ("selling all 40 shares of lot ROTH-VFIAX-07 ... for a total sale of $21,932") → confirmed → **Scenario 3 added, badged "AI-assisted — Custom," VFIAX $21,932.00 via Spec ID, EST. TAX $0.00, Federal Tax $0.00** — genuinely correct, and genuinely reached through the real UI this time, not a script standing in for it.

**Environment note, not a code issue**: mid-session, the long-running dev server on port 5173 (pre-existing, not started this session) got into a state where Vite's HMR reported repeated `500`/`[hmr] Failed to reload` errors and the browser was executing a stale bundle that pre-dated the `accountTypeLabel` import, throwing `ReferenceError: accountTypeLabel is not defined` in the actual `<OrderConfirmation>` component tree — `tsc --noEmit` was clean throughout, confirming this was a dev-server/HMR staleness issue, not a real code defect. Started a second, disposable dev server on a different port to keep verifying without disturbing the original; the original server recovered fully on its own after a genuine full-page reload (not a soft HMR patch) and was used for the final baseline run. Noting this so a future session recognizes the symptom (a `ReferenceError` for a name that's clearly imported in the source, immediately after a burst of `[hmr] Failed to reload` messages) as a server-state problem to route around, not a regression to chase in the source.

**Baseline**: Vitest 128/128 (127 prior + fixture fix, no new tests added this entry — verification and bug-fixing only), Playwright 3/3, `tsc --noEmit` clean.

**Answering the question directly, as asked**: no, this was not a contained fix. The click-handler wiring itself was exactly as small as predicted. What it was not contained against was a systemic, pre-existing assumption — baked into roughly ten independent call sites across five files — that the active account is always taxable brokerage, which nothing had ever been able to test because nothing had ever been able to reach a different account. Enabling the selector didn't introduce these bugs; it was the first thing in the project's history capable of proving they were there.

### D058 — [Dev] Account-switching layout coherence: two positional bugs fixed (Automated heading/fund mismatch, Manual list reordering), fixed canonical account order established as the standing rule, confirmed as new code from D057's own wiring, verified live — a distinct finding category from D057's tax-computation gating
Found by manually clicking through account switching for layout/interaction correctness specifically, not tax-computation correctness — the same category split D044–D047 made between narration figures and narration prose. D057's own tests and grep-based verification covered whether the right numbers appeared; nothing had checked whether they appeared under the right heading, or whether the list itself stayed still. Both bugs trace to the same coupling: selection state (activeAccountId) was tied to list position, not just to which row expands.

**1. Automated mode — expanded heading and displayed funds could refer to different accounts.** `FundSelectionAutomated.tsx` rendered its fund-table block (column header + `rec?.fund_results.map()` rows) in a fixed DOM position directly under the Taxable Brokerage header, regardless of `activeAccountId` — switching to Traditional IRA or Roth IRA updated which account's funds were computed (D057 wired that correctly) but the table itself never moved, so a Traditional IRA or Roth IRA fund could render under the Taxable Brokerage heading. **Fixed**: restructured into an IIFE that computes `fundTableSection` (column header + fund rows) once, then renders all three account header rows in fixed order and places `{activeAccountId === X?.account_id && fundTableSection}` immediately after each — the table can now only ever appear directly beneath the row it belongs to, structurally, not by DOM-position convention.

**Bonus bug fixed in passing, found while already inside this block**: `buildFundResultNarrationInput()`'s call here hardcoded `accountType: 'taxable_brokerage'` regardless of which account was actually active — meant any Traditional IRA or Roth IRA sale's narration was always framed with capital-gains language, never the correct ordinary-income (Traditional IRA) or tax-free (Roth) framing, a direct CD-3.1/CD-4.2 accuracy violation once non-taxable accounts became reachable (D057). Changed to `activeAcct?.account_type ?? 'taxable_brokerage'`. Verified live: switching to Traditional IRA now correctly narrates "Traditional IRA withdrawals are taxed as ordinary income regardless of this gain/loss figure"; Roth IRA shows no tax-treatment caveat at all, consistent with $0.00 tax.

**2. Manual mode — switching accounts moved the whole list.** `FundSelectionManual2.tsx` derived `otherAccounts = allAccounts.filter(a => a.account_id !== activeAccountId)` and rendered the active account in a separate, always-first "expanded" block, with the two non-active accounts rendered after it via `.map()` in whatever order the filter left them. Since the active account was always pulled out of its natural position and placed first, every switch visually reordered the list — the newly-selected account always jumped to position #1. **Fixed**: removed `otherAccounts` entirely. Added a fixed `ACCOUNT_TYPE_ORDER = ['taxable_brokerage', 'traditional_IRA', 'roth_IRA']` and derived `accountsInCanonicalOrder` from it once. Replaced the old hardcoded-active-block-plus-`.map()`-for-others split with a single `.map()` over `accountsInCanonicalOrder` — each account renders its own header row generically (label/masked-number/balance/RMD-badge, all now driven uniformly by the account object rather than two different code paths for "the active one" vs. "the others"), with the same fundTableSection-computed-once-and-conditionally-placed pattern used in fix 1 rendered directly beneath whichever row matches activeAccountId.

**Standing rule, both pages now share it explicitly (comment left in both files)**: account rows render in fixed canonical order — Taxable Brokerage, Traditional IRA, Roth IRA, per `CLAUDE.md` §5's documented withdrawal-priority rule, which is also the order every screenshot in this project has consistently shown. Selecting an account controls which row's fund table expands underneath it and which row is highlighted — never the row's position in the list. Any future account-switching UI in this app should follow this same pattern rather than re-deriving it.

**Confirmed, not assumed, that this was new code from D057's own wiring, not inherited from the original app**: account switching itself was entirely non-functional in both this repo and the live original app before D057 — no click handler existed anywhere, setActiveAccountId was never called (D057, Part A). The otherAccounts filter-and-reorder pattern and the fixed-DOM-position fund table were both written as part of D057's own switching implementation, in this session's prior turn. There is no earlier version of either page's account-row rendering that this could have been inherited from — D057's diagnosis already established the selector had no prior working implementation to inherit a bug from.

**Live verification, per instruction — real interaction, not just a clean compile**: `tsc --noEmit` clean immediately after each edit, then manually switched between all three accounts in both modes via the running dev server (localhost:5190), reading back full page structure after each switch (read_page accessibility tree + get_page_text, capturing exact DOM order and content) rather than trusting the diff:
- Automated mode: Taxable Brokerage (default active, VTSAX/VBTLX funds correctly shown) → clicked Traditional IRA (order unchanged: Taxable, Traditional IRA, Roth IRA; VFITX now correctly appears under the Traditional IRA heading, narration correctly states ordinary-income framing) → clicked Roth IRA (order still unchanged; VFIAX correctly appears under Roth IRA heading, no tax-treatment caveat, $0.00 tax).
- Manual mode (inherits Roth IRA as active from the mode switch): order Taxable, Traditional IRA, Roth IRA with Roth IRA's VFIAX table correctly attached in position 3 (not moved to position 1) → clicked Traditional IRA (list order unchanged; VBTLX/VFITX correctly appear under Traditional IRA, still in position 2) → clicked Taxable Brokerage (list order unchanged; VTSAX/VTIAX/VBIRX/VBTLX correctly appear under Taxable Brokerage, still in position 1).
- In every case: list order never changed, the correct funds always appeared under the correct heading, and the selected/highlighted row always matched the expanded one.

**Screenshot limitation, disclosed rather than worked around silently**: real pixel screenshots were requested explicitly but were not obtainable in this session — the Browser pane tool's screenshot action failed every attempt ("the Browser pane is not displayed, so the page is not compositing frames"), including after fronting the tab and opening the pane fresh via preview_start; the Chrome extension surface (claude-in-chrome) was also not connected. Verification instead relied on the accessibility tree (read_page) and rendered text (get_page_text) read back after each real click, which captures exact DOM order and per-account content — the two things this task's bugs were actually about — even without a rendered image. Flagged to the user directly rather than claiming screenshots were taken.

**Baseline**: Vitest 128/128 (unchanged — no test files touched, this was a pure layout/structural fix with no new computed-value behavior to test), Playwright 3/3, `tsc --noEmit` clean.

**Why this is a distinct finding category from D057, not a continuation of it**: D057's own verification (grep-based account-type gating checks, narration test fixtures) exhaustively covered whether tax figures were computed correctly per account — a value correctness question. Both of this entry's bugs left every computed value correct throughout; the defect was entirely in where a correct value rendered relative to a heading, and whether a list held still — a spatial/positional correctness question no computed-value test, however thorough, was ever positioned to catch. Consistent with D044–D047 establishing the same split for narration figures vs. narration prose: passing tests for one kind of correctness make no claim about the others.

### D059 — [Dev] Two real bugs found live by the user after D058 shipped: engine silently under-fills a single-holding account's automated target, and a stale cross-account manual session breaks the very next Manual-mode visit
Found by the user actually using the D058 build, not by further automated testing — screenshots of a live Roth IRA switch showed the requested $25,000 collapsing to $15,000, and a report that switching into Manual mode "sometimes" cleared the sell amount entirely. Both are genuine, previously-undiscovered defects, not artifacts of D058's own layout fix — D058 only touched JSX structure/positioning; both of these are computation and cross-page state bugs that were simply never reachable before account switching existed (D057) and single-fund non-taxable accounts became selectable in Automated mode for the first time.

**1. Engine under-fills the automated target when an account can't absorb it within Phase 1/2's category split.** `selectFundsAutomated()` in `dev/src/engine/index.ts` splits the requested target into an `allocBudget` (60%/70% of target, by optimization priority) spent on overweight-asset-class holdings in Phase 1, and a `lossBudget` (the remainder) spent on loss-carrying holdings in Phase 2. Phase 3's gap-fill then tops up from any holding **not already touched** by Phases 1–2. For an account with several holdings this works — untouched holdings absorb whatever Phase 1/2 left unbudgeted. It silently breaks for an account whose *only* qualifying holding was already claimed by Phase 1 (or Phase 2): Phase 3 has nothing left to draw from, so the unspent `lossBudget` fraction of the target is never sold from anywhere, and the shortfall simply vanishes from the recommendation with no error, no warning, and no signal to the caller. Reproduced exactly as the user found it: Roth IRA holds only VFIAX (domestic equity, no unrealized loss); a $25,000 tax-first request set `allocBudget = $15,000` (60%), Phase 1 sold exactly that from VFIAX, Phase 2 found no loss holdings, and Phase 3 found no untouched holding to place the remaining $10,000 `lossBudget` into — final recommendation: $15,000 sold, $10,000 silently missing.

**Fixed**: added Phase 4 (`selectFundsAutomated()`, same file) — if a shortfall remains after Phase 3 and every holding in the account is already in `fundSales`, revisit already-selected holdings (loss-ascending order, same priority as Phase 3) and top each up to a higher target, up to its own `current_balance`. `selectLots()` is a pure function of a holding and a target amount (no internal state to corrupt), so topping up re-runs it against the holding's *new, larger* total rather than trying to append to the existing lot selection — a safe, complete replacement of that holding's entry in `fundSales`, not an accumulation that could double-count. Verified live, the same reproduction: Roth IRA + $25,000 now correctly sells the full $25,000 from VFIAX (previously $15,000). Also verified against Traditional IRA (single holding VFITX, real balance $100,905) with the same $25,000 request — correctly fills in full there too, confirming the fix generalizes rather than being special-cased to Roth. All 128 existing Vitest tests passed unchanged after the fix — every existing fixture uses a multi-holding taxable-brokerage account where Phase 3 was already sufficient, so Phase 4 never activates for them; this is additive coverage for a case nothing previously exercised, not a behavior change to the existing paths.

**Not separately fixed, and arguably shouldn't be**: `FundSelectionAutomated.tsx`'s SALE TOTAL display (line ~115) deliberately shows `targetSaleAmount` (the user's entered figure) rather than the recommendation's actual fund total, with a comment noting the two "may differ slightly... due to lot sizing." That comment was written assuming only small rounding gaps; it was actually masking this $10,000 engine shortfall by displaying the number the user typed instead of the number the engine delivered. With Phase 4 fixed, the two values now genuinely match whenever the active account's total balance can cover the request — which was true in every case reproduced here. A residual case remains open, not silently resolved: if `targetSaleAmount` exceeds the active account's *entire* balance across all its holdings, Phase 4's capacity cap (`current_balance`) means the engine still can't fully fill it, and the display would again show the entered target rather than what was actually achievable. Flagging this explicitly per the project's standing rule to surface an unresolved tradeoff rather than paper over it — not fixed here because it was outside what the user's live report actually exercised (every real account here had far more balance than the requested target), and a real fix (cap the input, or show an explicit shortfall warning) is a UI-design decision, not a one-line correction.

**2. A Manual session built for one account silently breaks the next Manual-mode visit after switching accounts from the Automated page.** `manualActiveFundIds`/`manualAppliedAmountsCents`/`manualCostBasisMethods`/`manualLotSelections` are keyed by fund_id (ticker) alone, with no account scoping — D057 already knew this and added `clearManualSession()` to guard it, but only inside `FundSelectionManual2.tsx`'s own `switchAccount()`. `FundSelectionAutomated.tsx`'s `switchAccount()` (also introduced in D057) calls only `setActiveAccountId()`, with no equivalent clear. Reproduced the exact intermittent behavior the user described: (a) Automated mode, Roth IRA, $25,000 → Manual — correctly seeds VFIAX/$25,000 (this is `FundSelectionManual2`'s **Case 1** mount-effect path: `manualActiveFundIds` was empty, so it seeds fresh from the store's `recommendation`); (b) back to Automated, switch to Traditional IRA (no clear fires here — this is the gap), confirm $25,000 correctly recomputes for VFITX; (c) Manual again — **broke**: `manualActiveFundIds` was *not* empty (still `['VFIAX']`, leftover from step (a)), so the mount effect took its **Case 2** path instead (\"closure values are valid, re-run engine\") and re-ran `runOptimization()` in manual mode with a `fund_selections` entry for `VFIAX` against the *Traditional IRA* account — which has no VFIAX holding at all. The engine's manual-mode loop (`account.holdings.find(h => h.fund_id === sel.fund_id)`) found nothing, silently skipped that selection, and returned empty `fund_results` — SALE TOTAL rendered as \"—\", no fund shown as active. This exactly matches \"sometimes, not always\": it only manifests when a Manual session for a *different* account already exists in the store at the moment of the next account switch, which depends on the user's prior navigation path, not on any single deterministic click.

**Fixed at the root, not at each call site**: moved the clear into `setActiveAccountId` itself, in `dev/src/store/useAppStore.ts` — it now clears `manualActiveFundIds`/`manualAppliedAmountsCents`/`manualCostBasisMethods`/`manualLotSelections`/`manualConfig` as part of the same `set()` call whenever the id actually changes (a no-op guard skips the clear when the id is unchanged, so redundant calls don't wipe in-progress work). This is deliberately centralized rather than patched into `FundSelectionAutomated.tsx`'s `switchAccount()` alongside Manual2's existing call — a per-call-site fix would have left the exact same class of bug waiting for the next new account-switching entry point; centralizing into the setter makes the invariant ("the manual session is always scoped to the currently active account") hold structurally, not by convention every caller has to remember. `FundSelectionManual2.tsx`'s own `switchAccount()` no longer needs its own `clearManualSession()` call — removed it (and the now-unused `clearManualSession` destructure) rather than leaving redundant dead logic beside the real fix. Verified live with the exact failing repro sequence (Roth IRA → Manual → Automated → Traditional IRA → Manual): now correctly shows Traditional IRA's $25,000 in VFITX at the final step, not the previous blank \"—\" state.

**Baseline after both fixes**: Vitest 128/128 (unchanged — no existing fixture exercises either the single-holding gap-fill path or the cross-account stale-session path, so nothing needed updating; this is uncovered ground, not a regression risk against what's already asserted), Playwright 3/3, `tsc --noEmit` clean.

**Verification method, same limitation as D058**: real pixel screenshots were still not obtainable this session — the Browser pane's `computer` tool reported zero-size viewport and coordinate clicks landed at (0,0)/off-canvas on every attempt, including after `resize_window` and re-fronting the tab. Used `read_page`/`get_page_text` as in D058, plus (new this entry, since coordinate-based `computer` clicks were entirely non-functional rather than just screenshot-blocked) `javascript_tool` to dispatch real DOM `input`/`click` events for driving the repro steps themselves — legitimate as a verification/inspection aid standing in for a broken click pipeline, not used to implement any part of the fix, which was made entirely in source.

**Why this wasn't caught by D058's own verification**: D058's live walkthrough exercised each account once per mode, confirming layout/order correctness — it never chained two different accounts through Manual mode in the same session, which is exactly the sequence both of these bugs needed to surface. Consistent with the pattern this project keeps re-learning (D044–D047, D055, D058 itself): a verification pass proves what it actually exercised, not the neighboring paths it didn't think to chain together — and here, the user's own live use found what the previous pass's walkthrough shape didn't.

### D060 — [Dev] Third account-switch bug found live by the user: switching directly between two non-taxable accounts *while already in Manual mode* lost the sell amount; root-caused to a stale-closure trap, plus an adjacent dev-only-masked bug found and fixed in Automated mode along the way
User reported this as a residual problem after D059 shipped: "switching account types between Traditional and Roth IRAs while already in manual mode, the sell amount is lost… switching back to Automated restores the sell amount." D059's fix (clearing the manual session centrally in `setActiveAccountId`) was necessary but not sufficient — it stopped the cross-account fund-id mismatch, but did nothing to *re-seed* the newly active account, since `FundSelectionManual2.tsx`'s seeding logic lived only in a mount-only `useEffect(..., [])` that never fires again once the component is already mounted and the user just clicks a different account row.

**Root cause, confirmed by direct reproduction** (Taxable $20,000 → switch to Traditional IRA in Automated → Manual mode, correctly seeded VFITX/$20,000 → switch directly to Roth IRA while still in Manual): `manualActiveFundIds`/`manualAppliedAmountsCents` correctly cleared (D059), but nothing re-populated them for Roth IRA — `SALE TOTAL` showed "—", VFIAX rendered as an inactive "Sell" row with no amount, matching the user's report exactly.

**Fix, first attempt — and why it initially still failed live**: added re-seeding logic directly to `FundSelectionManual2.tsx`'s `switchAccount()` — capture the dollar total that was in progress before the switch, compute a fresh automated-style recommendation for the *new* account against that same total (mirroring the mount effect's own "Case 1" seed-from-recommendation pattern), and seed the manual session from it. `tsc`/Vitest passed immediately, but a live re-test still showed empty results. Root cause of *that*: `switchAccount()` called `setActiveAccountId(accountId)` (updating the Zustand store synchronously) and then immediately called the memoized `runManualEngine()` callback — but that callback is `useCallback`-bound to the *current render's* closure, which still held the *old* `activeAccountId` at that point in the same synchronous function call (the store update doesn't force a fresh re-render, and thus a freshly-bound `runManualEngine`, until after this function returns). `runManualEngine()` therefore called `runOptimization()` in manual mode against the *old* account while asking it to sell the *new* account's fund_ids — a fund lookup miss inside the engine, empty `fund_results`, same "vanishes" symptom via a different mechanism than D059's. **Fixed for real** by not routing through `runManualEngine()` at all for this path — `switchAccount()` now calls `runOptimization()` directly, twice (once in `'automated'` mode to get the fresh per-fund recommendation, once in `'manual'` mode to build the real `ManualConfiguration`), passing the *new* `accountId` explicitly as a parameter both times rather than relying on any closure to have already caught up with the store. Verified live, both directions, from a genuine hard reload (not just an SPA navigation, to rule out leftover in-memory state from earlier testing masking the real bug): Traditional IRA → Manual ($20,000 seeded correctly) → switch to Roth IRA while still in Manual (now correctly re-seeds VFIAX at $20,000, full figures and AI narration intact) → switch back to Traditional IRA (correctly re-seeds VFITX at $20,000). No blank state at any step.

**A second, unrelated bug found and fixed while chasing the first one down — real, but only user-visible in a production build, never in this dev environment**: while instrumenting `FundSelectionAutomated.tsx` to understand why an *earlier* (pre-this-entry) manual repro attempt appeared to "self-heal" when switching back to Automated, traced it to `dev/CLAUDE.md`'s two mount/account-change effects: a mount-only effect guarded by `!recommendation`, and a separate account-change effect that explicitly *skips its own first invocation* on the assumption the mount effect already covered it. This combination is correct only if `recommendation` is genuinely absent on a fresh mount — but if the component mounts with a *stale* `recommendation` left over from a *different* account (exactly what happens after switching accounts while on some other page, then navigating back here), the mount effect's `!recommendation` check is false (something exists, just for the wrong account) and the account-change effect's guard skips its first render unconditionally — so *neither* effect recomputes, and the page should show the wrong account's stale figures. It didn't, in this dev environment, purely because React 18 StrictMode (enabled in `main.tsx`) double-invokes mount effects in development: the account-change effect's skip-guard ref gets flipped to `false` by its own first (React-discarded) invocation, so its *second* invocation — still within the same StrictMode-doubled mount, using fresh values off the store — genuinely fires and recomputes correctly. That's a **dev-only accident**, not a real fix: a production build (no StrictMode) would show the stale account's numbers with no recovery path. **Fixed properly**: collapsed both effects into one, keyed only on `[activeAccountId]`, with no skip-first-render guard at all — it now unconditionally recomputes for whichever account is actually active, on the initial mount and on every subsequent switch alike, so correctness no longer depends on a development-only side effect nobody wrote on purpose. Removed the now-unused `useRef` import alongside it. Confirmed via the debug-instrumented live console trace before removing the instrumentation (temporary, added and removed within this session, never committed) that this really was the mechanism — not a guess.

**Baseline after both fixes**: Vitest 128/128 (unchanged — neither bug's path was covered by any existing fixture, consistent with D059's pattern of these being genuinely uncovered ground, not regressions), Playwright 3/3, `tsc --noEmit` clean.

**Verification discipline note**: the false "it self-healed" observation earlier in this same investigation came from not controlling for accumulated in-memory app state across multiple SPA navigations in the same browser tab within one verification session — `window.location.href` assigned to a URL the tab was no longer actually at (after several client-side route changes) does trigger a real navigation, but earlier reasoning wrongly assumed a same-string comparison against the *original* load URL, and separately, this project's own account-switching investigations (D057–D059) had already established that Zustand session state is not reset by SPA navigation alone. The fix that mattered here was reached only after switching to `window.location.reload()` (a genuine hard reload) before each verification pass, plus temporary source-level console instrumentation to see actual effect firing order rather than inferring it from rendered output alone. Recording this here because the same trap — trusting a "clean" re-test that was actually still running on stale in-memory state from a prior attempt — is exactly the kind of mistake this project's own standing rules (CLAUDE.md §0, "verify, don't assume") exist to catch, and it cost real time in this entry specifically.

### D061 — [Dev] Scenario Analysis account-state audit (requested before resuming the Scenario Analysis punch list): WhatIfPanel and Scenario Analysis's own account handling are clean; found and fixed a real, analogous bug in "Edit scenario" — `startEditingScenario` never set `activeAccountId`
Requested explicitly as a pre-check before returning to the Scenario Analysis punch list, on the theory that D057–D060's account-state bugs (cross-account session leakage, a stale closure holding a previous account's value, an effect guard that only worked by StrictMode accident) were about shared store handling, not anything Fund-Selection-specific — and the Scenario assistant reads from the same central store, built before any of those bugs were known.

**1. `WhatIfPanel` — cross-account leakage while a conversation is in progress**: not reachable, by inspection. `activeAccountId` is a prop, not a `useCallback`-memoized closure value — `reference = useMemo(() => buildPortfolioReferenceContext(portfolio, activeAccountId), [portfolio, activeAccountId])` correctly depends on it, and `sendUtterance`/`handleConfirm` are plain functions re-created every render (no `useCallback` anywhere in this file), so neither has the stale-closure shape that broke `FundSelectionAutomated.tsx`. More fundamentally: `ScenarioAnalysis.tsx` has no account-switching UI of its own, and the panel is a fresh mount every time it opens (`{assistantOpen && portfolio && <WhatIfPanel .../>}`, no preserved identity across closes) — so there is currently no reachable path where `activeAccountId` changes while the panel is open with an in-progress or completed conversation. This is worth stating as a constraint, not just a clean result: if a future session adds an account switcher to Scenario Analysis itself, this panel's `messages`/`turnHistory`/pending `confirm` candidates would need an explicit reset on account change, the same class of fix D059 made for Manual mode's fund selection — not because anything is broken today, but because today's safety is structural (no trigger exists), not defensive (nothing guards against one).

**2. Memoized callback holding a stale account id, the specific D060 mechanism**: does not apply here — confirmed no `useCallback` in `WhatIfPanel.tsx` reads `activeAccountId` from a closure the way `FundSelectionManual2.tsx`'s `runManualEngine` did.

**3. Scenario Analysis's own display — stale scenarios from a previously-selected account**: not a bug, by design. Scenarios are intentionally account-agnostic in the comparison view — up to 3 scenarios can be (and, per the seeded canonical set, normally are) built against different accounts, each carrying its own `account_id`/`account_type` used correctly in its own narration (`scenario.account_type ?? 'taxable_brokerage'`, the existing D057-era fallback). `activeAccountId` on this page governs only one thing: what account a *new* scenario built through the assistant or "Add scenario" will default to — confirming item 5 of the punch list (surfacing which account governs the current view) is asking about a real, legitimate ambiguity, not a symptom of a bug found here.

**4. Real bug found, outside the three explicit check points but squarely the same category**: `startEditingScenario` in `dev/src/store/useAppStore.ts` restores `targetSaleAmount`, `mode`, and the full manual-session shape from the clicked scenario, but never touched `activeAccountId` — despite the function's own comment claiming to "restore session state from a saved scenario so Fund Selection shows the right context." Reproduced live, both directions, from a genuine hard reload with real in-app clicks (not the `navigate()` tool, which turned out to trigger a full reload and reset the store — a methodology trap this entry hit and had to work around; see the note below): built a real $10,000 Roth IRA scenario (VFIAX) and a real $5,000 Taxable Brokerage scenario, with `activeAccountId` left on Taxable → clicked "Edit scenario" on the Roth one → Fund Selection showed a **freshly-recomputed Taxable Brokerage $10,000 recommendation** (different funds, different tax figures) instead of the actual Roth VFIAX scenario data, because `targetSaleAmount` (10000) was restored correctly but `activeAccountId` stayed on whatever was already active, and the D060-fixed unconditional `[activeAccountId]` effect dutifully recomputed a completely different, wrong-account recommendation for that dollar amount. Confirmed the same failure mode in the opposite direction too (editing a Taxable scenario while Roth was active showed Roth's own fresh recommendation instead).

**Fixed**: both branches of `startEditingScenario` now set `activeAccountId: scenario.account_id ?? DEFAULT_ACTIVE_ACCOUNT` — falling back to the taxable default only for scenarios saved before D057 ever tracked `account_id` (the seeded canonical demo scenarios, which are genuinely taxable-only — same fallback precedent as the narration `accountType` gate). Re-verified live after the fix, both directions: editing the Roth scenario while Taxable was active now correctly switches to Roth IRA (radio selected, $10,000 VFIAX, matching narration) before Fund Selection renders anything.

**Baseline**: Vitest 128/128 (unchanged — no fixture covers `startEditingScenario`'s account restoration), Playwright 3/3, `tsc --noEmit` clean.

**Methodology note, worth recording since it cost real verification time in this entry**: `mcp__Claude_Browser__navigate` (the browser-tool's URL-based navigation) triggers a genuine full page reload of this SPA, not a client-side route change — every earlier use of it mid-investigation silently reset the entire Zustand store (scenarios, activeAccountId, targetSaleAmount, everything except the localStorage-persisted portfolio) back to init state, which produced several confusing false readings before this was recognized. The fix for this session was procedural, not code: use in-app clicks (buttons/links, driven via `javascript_tool` DOM events when the Browser pane's own click/screenshot tools aren't available — see D058/D059 for that separate, still-standing limitation) to move between pages whenever cross-page *state* needs to survive the transition, exactly as a real user's clicks would via `react-router`'s client-side navigation — and reserve the `navigate` tool for genuinely fresh entries into the app. Flagging this here so a future session investigating any cross-page state question doesn't re-lose time to the same trap.

### D062 — [Dev] Narration tense fix: Fund Selection and Scenario Analysis were narrating unexecuted, proposed sales in past tense — root cause was a field that already existed and was already correctly set, but silently discarded by the prompt builder
Item 1 of the Scenario Analysis punch list. Reported bug: Fund Selection and Scenario Analysis narrate proposed, unexecuted scenarios as if already executed ("Sold $15,000.00..."), when neither has actually sold anything at that point — only Order Confirmation (about to submit) and Execution Summary (already submitted and settled) should read as declarative fact, and per the bug report Order Confirmation was already correct.

**Root cause, found by reading rather than guessing**: `NarrationInput` already carried a `tense: 'prospective' | 'past'` field, and `narrationBuilders.ts` already set it correctly at all four touchpoints — `'prospective'` for Fund Selection rationale and Scenario Analysis tradeoff summary (and, by inheritance, Order Confirmation summary, which reuses one of those two builders — this is *why* Order Confirmation was already correct, not a separate design decision), `'past'` for Execution Summary narrative. The bug was entirely in `dev/src/server/narrationPrompt.ts`: `buildNarrationPrompt()` destructured `tense` out of the input specifically to *exclude* it from the user message (correct — it's routing metadata, not a figure), but never read it to build any instruction for the system prompt either — the field was captured into an unused, underscore-prefixed variable and discarded. The model had no signal to distinguish a proposed plan from a completed one, and defaulted to declarative past tense across the board — made worse by `SEGMENT_TONE.A`'s own worked example, which was hardcoded as "Sold $21,780.00 of VTSAX..." regardless of touchpoint, actively reinforcing the wrong tense for every segment-A call.

**Fixed**: added a `TENSE_INSTRUCTION: Record<NarrationTense, string>` map with an explicit, forceful instruction per value (prospective: "has NOT been executed... Narrate it in a proposed/conditional frame... NEVER use past-tense/completed-action phrasing"; past: "has already been executed and settled... Narrate it in the past tense"), placed early and prominently in the system prompt (`TENSE_INSTRUCTION[input.tense]`, right after the two structural rules that open the prompt). Also fixed `SEGMENT_TONE.A`'s hardcoded past-tense example, which would otherwise have kept fighting the new instruction — annotated it as "shown in past tense for illustration only — always follow the tense instruction above for the actual verb tense," rather than trying to maintain two full parallel example sets. Simplified `TOUCHPOINT_FRAMING.execution_summary_narrative`'s wording to drop its own now-redundant "in the past tense" phrase, since tense is a single, uniformly-applied instruction now rather than something restated ad hoc per touchpoint.

**New test coverage** (`dev/src/utils/narration.test.ts`, new top-level `describe('narration tense per touchpoint (DECISIONS.md D062)', ...)` block, +8 tests): confirms `tense` is `'prospective'` for all three of `fund_selection_rationale`/`scenario_tradeoff_summary`/`order_confirmation_summary` and `'past'` for `execution_summary_narrative`, that the actual prompt text sent to the model contains the correct `TENSE_INSTRUCTION` for both values (not just that the input object carries the right tag), and that the deterministic fallback (`buildDeterministicFallback`, used only on API failure) already respected tense correctly before this fix and continues to — it independently chose `'Selling'`/`'Sold'` per the same `NarrationTense` value already, so the fallback path was never part of this bug; only the live-model prompt path was.

**Verified against the real, live Gemini API, not just structurally** — regenerated all 11 categories of `docs/narration-review-sample.md` via a temporary script (`dev/scratch-narration-tense-review.ts`, run via `npx tsx`, deleted immediately after, never committed — same discipline as every prior review-sample regeneration, D041/D043/D044/D045/D047/D049/D055). Every one of the 13 real model responses (11 categories, one with an A/B segment pair and one with an excluded/non-excluded pair) came back correctly proposed/conditional — zero uses of "Sold" anywhere, consistent phrasing like "Selling $X... would realize..." and "This proposed plan would sell $X...". Then confirmed live through the actual running app, end to end, not just the review script: Fund Selection ($5,000 across VTSAX/VBTLX) → correctly prospective ("Selling $3,000.00 of VTSAX using MinTax would realize..."); Order Confirmation → still correctly prospective, unchanged; submitted the order → Execution Summary correctly switched to past tense ("Sold $3,000.00 of VTSAX (MinTax), realizing..."); Scenario Analysis (canonical Scenario 1) → correctly prospective. All four touchpoints confirmed live in the same session, not assumed from the unit tests or the review script alone.

**One pre-existing, unrelated quirk noted in passing, not fixed**: category 7's regenerated sample states an oddly-formatted effective-rate figure ("-0.025251%") for a loss-only, $0-net-tax scenario — flagged in `docs/narration-review-sample.md` itself as a pre-existing artifact of that scenario's stored `effective_rate` value, unrelated to tense, out of scope for this fix.

**Baseline**: Vitest 136/136 (128 prior + 8 new tense tests), Playwright 3/3, `tsc --noEmit` clean. `CLAUDE.md` §7 updated with an explicit per-touchpoint tense rule.

### D063 — [PD/Dev] Scenario Analysis punch list, items 2/3/5/6/8: lot table gain/share column, dollar-based "Sell all" fix, active-account display, assistant focus retention, two-tone badge text
Five smaller, independent items from the same punch list as D062/D064, bundled into one entry since each is a small, self-contained fix rather than its own architectural unit.

**Item 2 — gain/share column.** `WhatIfFundLotReference` (`dev/src/utils/whatIfShared.ts`) only carried `lot_id`/`holding_period`/`shares`/`current_value` — no cost-basis data, per that type's own original comment ("Deliberately narrow: no cost basis, no tax figures"). The real `Lot` type already has `cost_basis_per_share` and `current_nav`; added both to `WhatIfFundLotReference` and to `buildPortfolioReferenceContext()`'s mapping. **This narrows the original "no cost basis" boundary deliberately, not accidentally** — documented explicitly in both the type's and `PortfolioReferenceContext`'s doc comments: since this same shape feeds the interpreter's grounding data (not just the UI table), the boundary that actually matters — the interpreter may compare these to rank/select lots, but never compute or state a dollar gain/loss or tax figure itself, which stays the engine's job downstream — still holds. This addition is also a hard prerequisite for item 3b (below): a "prioritize the smallest gains" request is architecturally unanswerable without per-lot gain data anywhere in the interpreter's context. `InlineLotTable` (`WhatIfPanel.tsx`) gained a GAIN/SH column (signed, green/red colored, matching the app's existing gain/loss color convention), with other columns narrowed slightly (SHARES 56→48px, VALUE 70→62px, action 64→52px) to keep the row within the 480px drawer.

**Item 3 — "Sell all" composed a share-count utterance the interpreter doesn't reliably act on.** `whatIfPrompt.ts`'s instructions and every worked example are dollar-centric (`sell_amount`, "$X") — nothing tells the interpreter how to map a raw share count onto a candidate. Changed the button's composed utterance from `Sell all ${lot.shares} shares of lot ${lot.lot_id}` to `Sell all of lot ${lot.lot_id}, worth ${formatCurrency(lot.current_value)}` — the lot's own `current_value` already *is* the correct dollar amount for "sell all of it," so this needed no new data, only rephrasing.

**Item 5 — no visible indicator of which account governs a new scenario.** Added an "ACCOUNT FOR NEW SCENARIOS" label to Scenario Analysis's tax-bracket strip (`ScenarioAnalysis.tsx`), showing `accountTypeLabel(activeAccount.account_type)` + masked number — resolves the ambiguity D061 flagged (an account-restriction message from the assistant, e.g. a SpecID refusal on a Traditional IRA, had no visible referent for which account it meant without leaving the page).

**Item 6 — focus retention.** The assistant's input is `disabled` while `loading`, so it can't hold focus through a send — added an `inputRef` and a `useEffect` keyed on `loading` that refocuses once it flips back to `false` (covers a normal send and the initial mount; never fires on any other trigger since `loading` only changes around `sendUtterance`).

**Item 8 — two-tone badge text.** Split the existing scenario badge's `<span>` into two: an `"AI-assisted — "` prefix in `#6b21a8` (the same purple `NarrationBlock.tsx`'s "AI-generated" badge already uses — reusing the app's one existing AI-disclosure color rather than inventing a second one) and the remainder in the pill's own established color (`#085041` teal-text for System-recommendation, `#717777` gray-text for Custom) — the pill's background itself is unchanged, so the System-rec/Custom distinction still reads correctly at a glance. **Verified against a real screenshot per the explicit instruction, not just structurally** — built two real AI-assisted scenarios live (one Custom/gray, one System-recommendation/teal via an objective-shaped request), screenshotted the resulting page, and additionally read back the rendered `<span>` HTML directly (`text-[#6b21a8]` on the prefix, the correct existing color on the rest) to confirm the two-tone split renders correctly against both pill backgrounds, not just that the color constants are right in source.

**Baseline**: Vitest 136/136 (unchanged — items 2/3/5/6/8 are UI/prompt-plumbing changes with no new assertions of their own; D064 immediately after this entry adds the test coverage that exercises the lot-reference shape change), Playwright 3/3, `tsc --noEmit` clean. Live-verified: item 5's account label confirmed showing "Taxable Brokerage ...4782" correctly; item 8's badge confirmed via screenshot and computed-style inspection, both described above. Items 2 and 3 verified structurally (types, prompt content) in this entry; their live behavior (does the model actually read gain/share correctly, does the dollar-based "Sell all" utterance actually get recognized) is covered by D064's live verification pass and item 3b's dedicated live test, not repeated here.

### D064 — [PM/PD/Dev] Scenario Analysis punch list, item 4: modifying an existing scenario, not just creating new ones — real support, two distinct request shapes, real test coverage including a Playwright E2E flow
D007 already framed the assistant as "equivalent to a round-trip edit through Fund Selection," which covers modification in principle — the actual gap, as the punch list stated it, was that the original 9 what-if coverage categories never tested "modify Scenario N" as a request shape distinct from "create new," and no code path existed to update a scenario in place at all (`WhatIfPanel.tsx`'s `handleConfirm` only ever called `addScenario()`).

**Two real, distinct request shapes, one shared mechanism.** Rather than trying to get an LLM to reliably infer "modify vs. create new" purely from utterance phrasing (a real risk — silently overwriting a scenario the user didn't intend to touch would be a serious UX mistake, not a minor one), the deciding signal is a UI-level fact the client already knows for certain, not something the interpreter has to guess:
1. **Explicit modify target** — opened via a new "✨ Modify with assistant" link on each `ScenarioColumn` (`ScenarioAnalysis.tsx`, alongside "Edit scenario →" — same "additional, equivalent edit tool" precedent D006/D007 already established for the assistant panel itself). Sets `modifyingScenarioId` in page state; a confirmed request always **updates that scenario in place** (`updateScenario()`), regardless of how the user phrases the request.
2. **Implicit reference, no explicit target** — the general "✨ Ask the assistant" entry point (unchanged trigger). A request like "like Scenario 1 but sell more VBTLX" reads Scenario 1's real data as reference and constructs a candidate for a **new** scenario (`addScenario()`) — Scenario 1 itself is never touched.
