**New grounding data, added to the same shape both the display and the interpreter already share (same pattern as D063's gain/share addition)**: `WhatIfScenarioReference` (`whatIfShared.ts`) — each existing scenario's real `fund_selections`/`mode`/`optimization_priority`, labeled the same 1-based "Scenario N" the UI already shows (not the internal `scenario_id`, which the user never sees), plus an `is_modify_target` flag. `buildPortfolioReferenceContext()` gained two new optional parameters (`scenarios: SavedScenario[] = []`, `modifyingScenarioId?: string`) — backward compatible by default, so every prior call site and all 9 original what-if test categories needed no changes. `whatIfPrompt.ts` includes this list in the prompt with instructions to treat it as read reference data for a "like Scenario N" request, and — only when one entry has `is_modify_target:true` — an explicit instruction that the whole conversation is about modifying that one scenario, starting from its real data and applying only the stated change.

**`WhatIfPanel.tsx`**: two new props, `scenarios: SavedScenario[]` (threaded into the reference-context call) and `modifyingScenario?: SavedScenario` / `onScenarioUpdated`. Greeting changes to "Modifying Scenario N — tell me what to change" when a target is set. `handleConfirm` branches early on `modifyingScenario`: when set, it preserves the scenario's identity (`scenario_id`, `scenario_name`, `created_at`) while replacing everything the engine actually recomputed, calls `onScenarioUpdated` instead of `onScenarioAdded`, and skips the 3-scenario-cap check entirely (updating in place never needs a free slot) — confirmed via a real message ("Updated Scenario N in Scenario Analysis") distinct from the add-path's "Added to Scenario Analysis."

**New test coverage, both request shapes, per the punch list's explicit requirement:**
- `dev/src/utils/whatIf.test.ts`, new `describe('scenario modification — existing_scenarios grounding data ...')` block (+7 tests, real-engine fixtures per this file's own established discipline, not hand-typed): `buildPortfolioReferenceContext()` genuinely carries `existing_scenarios` with correct labels and `is_modify_target` flags; `buildWhatIfPrompt()` genuinely includes that data and the modify-target instruction only when one is set; a modify-target request's candidate (via the same `fixedInterpreter` test-double pattern every other category uses) runs correctly through the real engine; an implicit "like Scenario 1 but..." request produces a genuinely new, correctly-combined candidate while leaving the referenced scenario's own data untouched. Found and worked around a real, minor, pre-existing latent bug while writing these: `buildScenarioFromFundResults()`/`buildScenarioFromRecommendation()` id scenarios by `Date.now()` alone (`sc-manual-${Date.now()}` etc.), which can collide when two scenarios are built synchronously within the same test (not a realistic risk from actual UI interaction, but a real gap worth noting for whoever next touches scenario-id generation) — worked around by assigning explicit distinct ids in the one test that needed two comparable scenarios, not fixed in the source, since it's outside this task's scope.
- `dev/tests/whatif-panel.spec.ts`: new Playwright test, **the only thing that actually exercises the addScenario-vs-updateScenario branch, which is a React-component-level decision `whatIf.test.ts` cannot see** — opens Scenario Analysis with the 3 seeded canonical scenarios, clicks Scenario 1's "Modify with assistant," sends a request producing a different fund/amount than the original, confirms, and asserts: the scenario count stays at 3 throughout (never becomes 4), the "Updated Scenario 1..." message appears (not "Added to Scenario Analysis"), and Scenario 1's own rendered content reflects the new $22,000 sale.

**Baseline**: Vitest 143/143 (136 prior + 7 new), Playwright 4/4 (3 prior + 1 new E2E), `tsc --noEmit` clean.

**Live verification status, honestly reported rather than assumed**: the mechanism above is verified structurally (real engine, real validator, real store update path via the new Playwright test) but has **not yet been verified against a real live model call** — the Gemini free-tier quota (500 requests/day) was exhausted partway through this session's work (D062's 13-call review-sample regeneration plus many live UI narration/assistant calls earlier in this session) before this item's live pass could run. Flagged explicitly rather than silently skipped or claimed as done — live verification of items 2–4 and 3b together is the immediate next step once quota allows, not assumed to already have passed because the structural/Playwright coverage did.

### D065 — [Dev] Groq added as a third provider (not default) for both features — here's what we actually know about it now, not just "an adapter was added"
Built `groqGenerator`/`groqInterpreter` following the exact Gemini/Anthropic adapter pattern, wired into both provider registries (`groq` alongside `gemini`/`anthropic` in `narrationGenerator.ts` and `whatIfInterpreter.ts`), `GROQ_API_KEY` added to `.env.example`/`.env.local`/`vite.config.ts`'s dev-server env passthrough. **Gemini stays the default in both files** — Groq is available via `NARRATION_PROVIDER=groq`/`WHATIF_PROVIDER=groq`, same status Anthropic already had. This is the easy, mechanical half of the task. The actual point — per the task's own framing — was not assuming behavioral equivalence, and that produced four real findings before any boundary-rule diagnosis could even begin.

**1. The model this task specified doesn't exist.** `llama-3.3-70b-versatile` ("Llama-class" per the task's own framing) returned `404 model_not_found` on a real call. `GET https://api.groq.com/openai/v1/models` against the real key confirmed why: this account's current catalog has **no general-purpose Llama chat model at all** — only two small Llama "prompt-guard" safety-classifier models (512-token context, not chat models). The real available chat models are Groq's own `groq/compound`/`groq/compound-mini`, OpenAI's open-weight `openai/gpt-oss-20b`/`120b`/`safeguard-20b`, and Alibaba's `qwen/qwen3.6-27b`/`3.8-27b`. Switched to **`openai/gpt-oss-120b`** — the largest general-purpose option, and the only family in this catalog explicitly advertising `structured_outputs`/`json_mode` support, which matters most for Feature 2's JSON responses. Same category of correction as D043 made for Gemini's retired `gemini-2.5-flash-lite` — verify the model actually exists before trusting a plan built on its name.

**2. `gpt-oss-120b` is a reasoning model — it spends part of `max_tokens` on hidden chain-of-thought before writing the answer, and Gemini/Anthropic's token budgets weren't enough.** Every Groq response includes a `reasoning` field alongside `content`, and `usage.completion_tokens_details.reasoning_tokens` counts against the *same* `max_tokens` cap as the final answer. At the original 300/500 (copied from Gemini/Anthropic), the real `narrationPrompt.ts` system prompt burned enough reasoning tokens that several calls returned **empty content** — confirmed directly: a 4-fund case at 1500 tokens hit `finish_reason:"length"` with 1391 of 1500 tokens spent on reasoning, truncating content mid-sentence after 2 of 4 funds (the two sentences that DID complete were structurally and tonally correct — this is a budget failure, not a rule failure). Raised `max_tokens` to **1500** (narration) and **2000** (what-if) in the Groq adapters specifically — an adapter-level parameter, not a change to either shared prompt file, so this doesn't touch Gemini's or Anthropic's already-verified behavior. The 4-fund case still needs headroom beyond 1500 for a full non-truncated response; flagged, not chased further (out of scope — the goal here was diagnosis, not tuning Groq to perfection).

**3. Groq's real rate limit is 8000 tokens/minute on this key, confirmed via response headers (`x-ratelimit-limit-tokens: 8000`, `x-ratelimit-reset-tokens` ~19s rolling), not request-count-based** (`x-ratelimit-limit-requests: 1000`, never the binding constraint). An unpaced sweep of 6 what-if calls hit 429 after the first 2; every live-test script in this entry was rewritten with a 20-second `sleep()` between calls once this was diagnosed, which cleared it completely for the rest of the session.

**4. A genuine circular-import trap, exposed but not caused by adding Groq.** A diagnosis script that imports `groqGenerator` directly (as its first entry into the module graph) throws `Cannot access 'groqGenerator' before initialization` — `groqGenerator.ts` imports `NarrationApiError` from `narrationGenerator.ts`, which imports all three adapters (including `groqGenerator.ts`, mid-evaluation) to build its registry; the back-edge resolves to an uninitialized binding. This exact circular shape already existed for `geminiGenerator.ts`/`anthropicGenerator.ts` — it just happened to never be entered this way before (every real caller and every prior script/test enters via `getActiveGenerator()`/`getActiveInterpreter()`, not a direct adapter import). Worked around in every script here by entering through `getActiveGenerator()`/`getActiveInterpreter()` with the provider env var forced, matching how the real app and every existing test already do it. Not a real runtime risk to the shipped app (nothing there imports an adapter directly), so not fixed at the source — flagged for whoever next touches this module boundary.

**Boundary-rule diagnosis, against real live output, once the above were sorted out — narration (`docs/narration-review-sample.md`'s hardest categories, via `getActiveGenerator()` with `NARRATION_PROVIDER=groq` forced):**
- **Tense, prospective** (Fund Selection rationale): *"Selling $5,000.00 of VTSAX ... would realize a $440.77 gain..."* — correct conditional frame, no "Sold." ✅
- **Tense, past** (Execution Summary): *"Sold $5,000.00 of VTSAX, realizing a $440.77 short-term gain..."* — correctly switches to declarative past tense for the one touchpoint that's actually executed. ✅
- **Traditional IRA ordinary-income framing**: *"...est. tax $0.00 (withdrawal taxed as ordinary income)..."* — correctly states what's actually taxed, not just a negated gain figure. ✅
- **Market-context exclusion** (VFITX/IRA-VFITX-06): output never mentions a trailing-12-month return or any performance figure — correctly silent on data it was never given. ✅
- **Scenario tradeoff summary, prospective tense**: *"Selling $20,000.00 of [VBTLX] would realize a $2,104.25 long-term loss..."* — correct. ✅
- **Multi-fund (4 funds, D047's run-on-sentence category)**: the two sentences that completed before hitting the token cap (see finding 2) were each correctly scoped to one fund, correct prospective tense, correctly included the Wait & Save mention — genuinely correct behavior, incomplete only because of the budget issue already documented above, not a structural-instruction failure.

**Boundary-rule diagnosis — what-if (`getActiveInterpreter()` with `WHATIF_PROVIDER=groq` forced), SpecID pair plus a 6-case structured-output sweep:**
- SpecID on Traditional IRA → **refused**, correct reason stated. ✅
- SpecID on Roth IRA → **not** refused, correctly asked which lot(s) instead. ✅
- SpecID on Taxable Brokerage → correctly asked for lot/amount detail (also not refused). ✅
- Clean delta, objective-shaped request, vague bond-fund reference, ambiguous-fund disambiguation (named both real tickers), out-of-scope advice (refused, assistive-not-directive), amount exceeding real balance (caught and clarified with the real balance stated, never passed to the engine) — **all 6 behaviorally correct.**
- **Structured-output reliability: 9/9 real calls parsed successfully by the existing `parseInterpreterResponse()` with zero changes** — Groq/gpt-oss-120b's JSON output needed no new parsing path, no markdown-fence-stripping edge case beyond what already existed for Gemini/Anthropic. This directly answers the task's specific ask: no new JSON/structured-output failure mode was found.

**Item 3b, finally executed — against real Groq output, both hand-computed cases, and this is the most interesting result in this entry:**
- **$8,000 case** (single smallest-gain lot expected): the model did **not** construct an explicit SpecID/lot_overrides candidate at all — it returned `accounting_method: "MinTax"` with `sell_amount: 8000` and a summary claiming this "prioritizes the lots with the smallest gains," effectively delegating the criterion to MinTax's own existing lot-ranking logic rather than reasoning about lot-level gains itself. The **real engine's actual MinTax selection** for this exact request picked lot `T-VTSAX-08`, 55.096 shares — which happens to be **exactly** the hand-computed correct answer, because MinTax's own ranking (tax-dollar-minimizing) and pure smallest-gain-first ordering coincide for this specific lot. Correct *outcome*, via a shortcut that isn't literally "criterion-based lot selection" — worth being precise about, since a different fund/lot distribution could make MinTax and smallest-gain disagree, and this same shortcut would then produce the wrong answer.
- **$30,000 case** (genuine two-lot split expected): the model **did** construct explicit `lot_overrides` this time — `T-VTSAX-08: 150 shares` (the full lot) + `T-VTSAX-09: 56.61 shares` — matching the hand-computed correct answer (full lot 08 + ~56.61 shares of lot 09) essentially exactly, both the lot choice *and* the partial-share arithmetic. Genuinely strong quantitative reasoning. **But the candidate was structurally invalid**: `accounting_method` was left as `"MinTax"` instead of `"specific_lot_identification"`, and no `sell_amount` was supplied on the fund_selection (only `lot_overrides`) — `whatIfValidation.ts`'s existing code-level check (unchanged, not touched for this) correctly caught this exact inconsistency and downgraded the result to `clarify` ("The amount to sell from 'VTSAX' was never specified") rather than letting it reach the engine. This is the validator doing precisely the job D052/D053 built it for — a prompt instruction alone did not produce a schema-consistent candidate here, and the code-level check is what actually stopped an incomplete one from executing, exactly as designed.

**No prompt files edited in response to any of this, per the task's explicit instruction** — `narrationPrompt.ts` and `whatIfPrompt.ts` are unchanged from D062/D064's state. Everything above is diagnosis; nothing here was "fixed."

**Baseline**: Vitest 145/145 (143 prior + 2 new — one `NARRATION_PROVIDER=groq` selection test, one `WHATIF_PROVIDER=groq` selection test, plus the existing "both adapters" interface-shape/no-key-failure tests widened to all three), Playwright 4/4, `tsc --noEmit` clean.

**Overall assessment, stated plainly since the task asked for one**: Groq (`openai/gpt-oss-120b`) is a genuinely capable third option for this app once its two real quirks are worked around at the adapter level (model name, token budget) — narration and what-if boundary rules transferred cleanly across every category tested, structured-output parsing was flawless, and its multi-lot quantitative reasoning on the $30,000 case was the most impressive single result of this whole diagnosis pass. It is not yet "proven equivalent" to Gemini in the sense that would justify promoting it past "available" — the $8,000 case's MinTax-delegation shortcut and the $30,000 case's schema-inconsistent candidate are both real, specific gaps a broader test pass would need to characterize further before that claim could be made. Gemini remains the default, correctly, per this task's own instruction.

### D066 — [PM/PD/Dev] Two decisions agreed in planning but never issued as instructions, now actually applied — plus Feature 2's failure-handling, genuinely designed for the first time, not just implemented wrong
Three fixes, explicitly **not** three new decisions and not a reversal of anything built so far — items 1 and 2 correct a gap between planning conversation and what actually got instructed into this repo; item 3 is real, new design work on something this project's own governance (CD-1.x, §6) had left silently unspecified.

**Confirmed genuinely missing before assuming, per the task's own instruction, not taken on faith**: grepped the live source for "Ask the assistant"/"Modify with assistant" before touching anything. Both were real, current, unambiguous — the panel header literally read "Ask the assistant" (`WhatIfPanel.tsx`), the trigger sat inside the "Add scenario" dashed-box slot as an underlined text link (`ScenarioAnalysis.tsx`), and the per-scenario link read "✨ Modify with assistant." Neither had been renamed or relocated by any prior session — this really was agreed-in-planning-but-never-instructed, not a misremembering.

**1. Renamed "Ask the assistant" → "Scenario assistant," project-wide, not just the most visible instance.** Found and changed every user-facing occurrence: the panel's own header title, the header-level trigger button (new location, see below), the per-scenario "✨ Modify with Scenario assistant" link, and the input placeholder ("Tell the Scenario assistant what you'd like to do…"). Left the internal `role: 'assistant'` message-type discriminant in `WhatIfPanel.tsx` untouched — that's a code-level tag mirroring standard LLM-message-role convention (`'user'`/`'assistant'`), never rendered to the user, unrelated to the product name being fixed here.

**2. Relocated the trigger from the "Add scenario" slot to the Scenario Analysis page header.** Real IA reasons, not cosmetic: discoverable without hovering an easy-to-miss underlined link; doesn't visually imply the assistant only *adds* scenarios (it modifies existing ones too, D064); won't need a second relocation once multi-scenario exploration is built later. Rendered as a filled teal pill button (`bg-vg-teal`, white 14px bold text, `Sparkles` icon, 40px tall, fully rounded) next to the `<h1>` — reusing the same teal+Sparkles visual language Fund Selection's own Automated-mode pill already established, so "teal + sparkles" now reads consistently as "AI-related trigger" across the app, not invented fresh for this one button. **Always visible, not gated by the 3-scenario cap** — unlike the old Add-scenario-slot placement, which vanished entirely at 3 scenarios (`showAdd`). The existing cap-check inside `handleConfirm` (unchanged) is what actually prevents a 4th scenario; hiding the trigger was never the real guard, and removing that coupling is part of why this placement won't need revisiting later. The "Add scenario" slot itself is unchanged in every other respect — still the manual-navigation path (D007), still hidden at 3 scenarios, just no longer a dual-entry slot.

**3. Feature 2's failure-handling — designed here for the first time, not merely fixed.** Feature 1 has always had an explicit rule (§7): API failure falls back to a plain, deterministic, non-AI-badged summary of the same structured figures. Feature 2 was never given the equivalent decision, because there genuinely isn't a deterministic equivalent — there's no template for "what the user meant" the way there's a template for "what the engine already computed." That gap is exactly why `WhatIfPanel.tsx`'s `sendUtterance()` catch block did `err instanceof WhatIfError ? err.message : '...'`, and `interpretWhatIfTurn()` wraps a genuine fetch/provider failure into a `WhatIfError` whose `.message` *is* the raw underlying text — so a raw string like "Gemini API returned 429" was reaching the user directly, live, with no decision ever having been made about whether that was acceptable (it wasn't).

Designed and implemented, now written as an explicit rule in CLAUDE.md §8 alongside §7's Feature 1 fallback:
- **Never surface a raw API error, status code, or provider name, under any circumstance** — the interpret-time catch (only) now never reads `err.message`, full stop, regardless of the error's shape or origin. Deliberately scoped to that one call site: `handleConfirm()`'s catch is architecturally different — `confirmWhatIfCandidate()` makes no network call at all (validation, pure reshaping, then the local engine), so any `WhatIfError` reaching *that* catch is guaranteed to already be a safe, intentional validation message — its existing `err.message` display was correct before this fix and stays unchanged.
- **The message is honest and non-technical**: *"The Scenario assistant couldn't process that just now — this is usually temporary."*
- **It reminds the user the manual path remains available**, in the same message: *"You can try again, or continue manually with Edit scenario / Add scenario in the meantime"* — a failure state is exactly when "the assistant is an additional, equivalent edit tool, not the only one" (§8, D006/D007) actually matters to the person hitting it, not a moment to leave them stuck.
- **Decided: in-panel Retry, not "just re-type it."** The failed utterance is already known and already rendered above the error — retyping is pure friction for zero benefit, and the failure is presumptively transient. Implemented as an optional `retryUtterance` field on the error message variant, set only by the interpret-time catch (never for a refusal or a business-rule error — neither is usefully retryable), rendered as a button that resends the exact same utterance through the exact same `sendUtterance()` path.

**Live-verified against a genuine real failure, not a mock and not just reading the code** — corrupted `GROQ_API_KEY` in `.env.local` (the currently-active `WHATIF_PROVIDER`), restarted the dev server so the change actually took effect, then drove the real UI: sent "Sell $5,000 of VTSAX" through the real panel, hitting a real 401 from Groq's real API through the real serverless-function boundary. Result: *"The Scenario assistant couldn't process that just now — this is usually temporary. You can try again, or continue manually with Edit scenario / Add scenario in the meantime."* with a working **Retry** button — no "Groq," no "401," no stack trace, nothing technical, exactly as designed. Clicked Retry: correctly resent the identical utterance and failed the identical safe way (key was still bad). Restored the real key, restarted the server again, and confirmed via a **direct `curl` to `/api/interpret`** that the real success path still works correctly (a genuine, valid, parsed Groq response) — the Browser-pane tool's own preview proxy returned 502 for browser-driven requests specifically at that point (survived a full preview restart), the same category of tooling-environment issue D056 already documented for this same pane ("the browser-preview tool's own managed dev server could not reach the internet... environment-specific, not a code defect"), confirmed here the same way D056 confirmed it — a request made outside the pane's own proxy succeeds cleanly. Not chased further since the actual thing this task needed verified (the failure path) was already confirmed live and cleanly, and the success path is independently covered by the passing Playwright suite (real Chromium, unaffected by this pane-specific proxy issue) plus the direct-curl confirmation.

**Renaming/relocation verified live**: header button confirmed via `find()` (real accessible name "Scenario assistant") and computed styles (`rgb(0, 189, 163)` background = `vg-teal`, white 700-weight 14px text, 40px height, fully rounded — a real button treatment, not a text link); panel title confirmed "Scenario assistant"; placeholder confirmed "Tell the Scenario assistant what you'd like to do…"; all three per-scenario cards confirmed "✨ Modify with Scenario assistant."

**Existing Playwright coverage updated to match**, not left pointing at renamed/relocated elements: `dev/tests/whatif-panel.spec.ts` now targets the header-level trigger (`exact: true`, since "Modify with Scenario assistant" would otherwise also substring-match) and the renamed placeholder/link text throughout.

**Baseline**: Vitest 145/145 (unchanged — items 1–3 are UI text/placement/error-message changes with no new assertions of their own; the existing suite's structural coverage of `WhatIfPanel`'s message-rendering logic wasn't affected), Playwright 4/4 (both existing tests updated for the new selectors, both still pass), `tsc --noEmit` clean.

### D067 — [PD/Dev] Scrim + dollar-for-named-lot fixes, a formal what-if dimension-coverage matrix with real gap closures, and a live-tested answer on cross-account scenarios (no build)

**Tier 1, item 1 — scrim was still full-height.** Flagged in the very first UI review with a specific fix, never made it into an executable prompt across three prior sessions. `WhatIfPanel.tsx`'s outer container was `fixed inset-0` (full viewport) — the exact same pattern `CostBasisDialog.tsx` correctly uses for an actual centered modal, wrongly copied onto a side drawer that should leave the app's persistent header/nav undimmed. Measured the real header+nav height live against the running app (`document.querySelector('main').getBoundingClientRect().top` → 150px, not guessed from source or screenshots) and changed the container to `fixed left-0 right-0 bottom-0` with `top: 150`, plus lightened the scrim from `bg-black/30` to `bg-black/25` (a drawer is a lighter-weight interaction than a blocking dialog). **Live-verified via screenshot**: header/nav render fully undimmed, drawer's top edge sits at the header's bottom edge with no overlap.

**Tier 1, item 2 — dollar amount for a named lot required a manual share-count round trip.** `WhatIfCandidate`'s `lot_overrides` only ever carries `{lot_id, shares}` — no dollar field — so a request like "sell $10,000 of lot T-VTSAX-08" has no share count anywhere until something computes one. Reproduced live first: got exactly the reported symptom, the assistant asking the user to state a share count itself (the only place in this app that would have asked for one — every other amount here is dollar-denominated, per this project's own consistency principle). Root cause: `whatIfPrompt.ts`'s CORE RULE ("never infer a share count") was broad enough to suppress a legitimate arithmetic computation (stated dollar ÷ the lot's real `current_nav`, both given verbatim in the account-holdings data) along with the genuine hallucination risk it was written to prevent. Fixed by adding one explicit, narrow exception instruction distinguishing "compute from given data" (required) from "invent/guess" (still forbidden) — applies uniformly across all three provider adapters, since they share this one prompt file. **Live-verified via direct `curl` to `/api/interpret`** (the Browser-pane's own proxy hit its known intermittent 502s mid-verification — same tooling issue D056/D066 already documented for this pane, confirmed again the same way: a request outside the pane's proxy succeeds cleanly) with real VTSAX/T-VTSAX-08 data: returned `accounting_method: "specific_lot_identification"`, `lot_overrides: [{lot_id: "T-VTSAX-08", shares: 68.864}]` (≈ 10000/145.20), type `"confirm"` — no clarify, no share-count request back to the user.

**Tier 2 — a formal what-if dimension-coverage matrix, written as a real artifact in `CLAUDE.md` §12, not scratch notes.** Eight dimensions (fund count, account type, cost-basis method, amount specification, lot specification, scenario action, ambiguity level, scope) cross-checked against all 11 existing categories (the original 9 plus D064's modify/create-from-existing), surfacing confirmed gaps beyond the Tier-1 one: `average_cost` never once constructed in any test despite being in the schema and in VTSAX's own `available_accounting_methods`; criteria-based multi-lot selection ("smallest gains first") diagnosed live against real Groq output in D065 — both its success shape and a real schema-inconsistency the model actually produced — but never committed as repeatable coverage; multiple explicitly-named lots in one request (every existing lot-specific category names exactly one); a plain non-SpecID Traditional IRA request never exercised through the full interpret→confirm→engine path (only reachable before via a direct `validateCandidate()` call); fund-level "sell all" resolved against the fund's real balance rather than an explicit over-large number; a buy request (the prompt states this tool "only ever SELLS," nothing asserted a buy actually gets refused). Closed all six with 8 new tests (`whatIf.test.ts`'s new `describe('dimension-coverage gap closures ...')` block, rows 10–17 of the matrix), same real-engine-fixture / test-double-for-interpretation discipline as every existing category — including a regression test for the Tier-1 dollar-for-named-lot fix itself (row 10), which had no permanent test until now, and a deliberately-wrong candidate (row 14) mirroring D065's exact real failure shape (`lot_overrides` correctly split, `accounting_method` left as `"MinTax"`, no `sell_amount`) to prove the code-level validator, not the prompt, is what actually catches it. Three residual gaps documented in the matrix rather than chased further (Roth IRA never exercised outside SpecID; HIFO never tested alone or off Taxable; a create-from-existing reference to a scenario on a *different* account than the one active — directly relevant to Tier 3 below, not fixed here either).

**Tier 3 — investigated whether scenarios can span multiple accounts; reported, nothing built, per explicit instruction.** Built Scenario 1 live against Taxable Brokerage (VTSAX/VBTLX, $12.97 est. tax), switched the active account to Traditional IRA via the real account-switching UI (D057), built Scenario 2 against it (VFITX, $0.00 est. tax, correct ordinary-income framing), and went to Scenario Analysis. **Answer: it works, cleanly, right now — both scenarios coexist side by side in the same comparison, each computed correctly against its own real account** (Scenario 1's capital-gains figures, Scenario 2's $0 IRA figure and asset-mix-impact numbers all independently correct), no error, nothing blocking it. This is the same shape of finding as D057's account-selector discovery: a capability the data model (`account_id`/`account_type` per `SavedScenario`) already supported turned out to be reachable through the live UI, not something that needed building. **The one real problem found**: the header-level "ACCOUNT FOR NEW SCENARIOS" label (D063 item 5) shows only the *currently active* account — after switching to build Scenario 2, it read "Traditional IRA ...2973" while Scenario 1, still visible in the same view, was actually against Taxable Brokerage — and neither scenario column carries its own account label anywhere. A user glancing at Scenario 1 with that header showing would misattribute it. Console showed the same pane-proxy 502s documented above (narration fetches, unrelated to the multi-account mechanism itself — the page had already rendered correct narration text for both scenarios by the time this was checked). **Decision this enables, not yet made**: per the original prompt's framing, this confirms the account name belongs *per-scenario*, not only at the Scenario Analysis header level, since genuinely mixed-account comparisons are real and reachable, not a theoretical edge case — but no redesign work was authorized or started this session.

**Baseline**: Vitest 153/153 (145 prior + 8 new), Playwright 4/4, `tsc --noEmit` clean.

### D068 — [PD/Dev] Acting on D067's Tier 3 finding: per-scenario account labels, a single entry point with an in-panel segmented control replacing the per-scenario "Modify" links, a scenario summary card, and the account-context bug that self-audit surfaced before any of it was built

Self-audit first, per the task's own instruction, and it found a real problem before any UI work started — not a formality.

**Self-audit finding 1 — the Automated/Manual toggle is not a reusable component at all.** Grepped for it expecting a shared `ModeToggle`; found instead that `FundSelectionAutomated.tsx`, `FundSelectionManual.tsx`, `FundSelectionManual2.tsx`, and `FundSelectionManualLot.tsx` each hand-copy-paste their own fixed-two-option pill markup (hardcoded `Sparkles`/`PenLine` icons, hardcoded navigation targets) — visually a segmented control, structurally not one: no shared component, nothing data-driven, nothing that could take a variable-length option list. Built a small, new, genuinely reusable `ScenarioSegmentedControl` inside `WhatIfPanel.tsx` instead, styled to match the same visual pattern rather than pretending code that isn't reusable was.

**Self-audit finding 2 — the real architectural risk, found before it shipped wrong.** `modifyingScenario` was a prop the parent page decided once at panel-launch time (D064's model); `activeAccountId` was a separate prop, always the page's own "account for new scenarios." Tracing how they combined: if scenario selection became in-panel state instead of a launch-time prop, nothing would automatically make the engine call switch accounts when a different scenario was selected — the panel would keep silently using the page's active account regardless of which scenario it claimed to be modifying. Exactly the bug D067's Tier 3 investigation exists to prevent generalizing into. Fixed at the design stage, not after shipping: introduced `effectiveAccountId`, computed from the *selected scenario's own account* (via a new shared `resolveScenarioAccount()` helper, `dev/src/utils/format.ts`) when one is selected, falling back to the page's active account only for "New." Every engine call and grounding-context build in `WhatIfPanel.tsx` — `confirmWhatIfCandidate()`, both `buildScenarioFrom*()` calls, `buildPortfolioReferenceContext()` — now reads `effectiveAccountId`, not the raw `activeAccountId` prop.

**1. Per-scenario account label, on the real Scenario Analysis page.** Each `ScenarioColumn` header (`ScenarioAnalysis.tsx`) now shows its own resolved account name/type beneath "SCENARIO N" (`resolveScenarioAccount()`, same helper as above) — the page-level "ACCOUNT FOR NEW SCENARIOS" strip (D063 item 5) only ever describes what a *new* scenario would use, and was silently misattributing existing scenarios the moment D067's Tier 3 confirmed mixed-account comparisons are real. Kept "SCENARIO N" full-length rather than shortening to just the number — live-verified it fits without truncation or wrapping at real column widths.

**2. Single entry point + in-panel segmented control — supersedes D064's two-trigger model, not an addition alongside it, per the task's explicit instruction.** The per-scenario "✨ Modify with Scenario assistant" links are removed entirely from `ScenarioColumn`. The header-level "Scenario assistant" button is now the only launch point, always opening with "New" selected. `ScenarioSegmentedControl` (new, described above) renders "New" plus one option per existing scenario, dynamically, up to the 3-scenario cap. Scenario targeting moved from a launch-time prop (`modifyingScenario?`, removed from `WhatIfPanelProps`) to internal state (`selectedScenarioId`) — selecting "New" behaves exactly like the old header trigger; selecting a scenario behaves exactly like the old per-scenario trigger. **Switching the selection clears in-progress conversation state** — `messages`, `turnHistory`, the draft input, and the expanded-lot-table state — the same discipline this codebase already applies to account switches (D059's manual-session guard) and post-confirm resets (this file's own `turnHistory` clear after a successful confirm). The target scenario is resolved and passed directly into the new greeting rather than read back from state in the same tick — D060's stale-closure lesson applies here too, so `selectScenario()` computes `greetingFor(target)` from a locally-resolved value, not from `selectedScenarioId` state that hasn't re-rendered yet.

**3. Scenario summary card.** Shown above the conversation only when a specific scenario is selected (`ScenarioSummaryCard`, new, local to `WhatIfPanel.tsx`) — account name/type, fund(s), total amount, est. tax — because the scrim obscures the underlying page once the panel is open, and this is the only way to reorient without closing it. `data-testid="whatif-summary-card"` added specifically because "Taxable Brokerage" and similar text legitimately appears elsewhere on the underlying page (the header strip, each scenario column's own new label from item 1) — bare text assertions would have been ambiguous in both the Playwright suite and live verification, so the card and the panel itself (`data-testid="whatif-panel"`) both got real scoping hooks rather than relying on fragile text matches.

**4. Cross-account scenario reference — the residual gap D067 itself flagged, closed for real, not just tested around.** `WhatIfScenarioReference` (`whatIfShared.ts`) now carries each referenced scenario's own real `account_type`, threaded through from `SavedScenario.account_type` (falling back to `taxable_brokerage`, same precedent as every other such fallback in this codebase). `whatIfPrompt.ts` gained an explicit instruction: a referenced scenario's account_type may differ from the current one; its total/priority are still transferable reference data, but its specific fund_id/lot_id/method are not — any fund or lot the interpreter actually uses must be grounded in the *current* account's real holdings, and a referenced fund not held there must be clarified, not guessed. Four new tests in `whatIf.test.ts`'s `describe('cross-account scenario reference (DECISIONS.md D067)')`: the grounding data genuinely carries the real account_type; the prompt genuinely states the new instruction; a legitimate case ("like Scenario 1's amount, but from my Roth IRA's VFIAX" — Roth's own real, only holding) runs correctly through the real engine with `est_tax_gross === 0`; and an adversarial case (a badly-behaved model reusing the referenced scenario's own Taxable-only fund, VTSAX, inside a Roth-scoped reference) is refused by the validator, not passed to the engine — the same "prove the code catches it, not just the prompt" discipline as categories 6/7/14.

**5. Tests updated for the new architecture, not just added to.** `dev/tests/whatif-panel.spec.ts`'s second test, previously keyed on the now-removed per-scenario link, now opens via the header, asserts the link is genuinely gone (`toHaveCount(0)`), selects "Scenario 1" in the segmented control, asserts the summary card's real content, then completes the same modify-in-place flow as before. A new third test asserts the actual switching behavior the task called out by name: a draft utterance typed under "New" is cleared and does not leak into Scenario 2's conversation after switching, the summary card appears with Scenario 2's real data, and switching back to "New" clears the conversation again and hides the card.

**Live verification, not just structural** — Vitest/Playwright pass without proving the UI actually renders correctly, so this was checked live in the browser per the task's explicit instruction:
- Per-scenario account labels: confirmed rendering correctly under each seeded (Taxable) scenario's header.
- The per-scenario "Modify with Scenario assistant" links: confirmed genuinely gone (only "Edit scenario →" / "Review order" remain).
- Segmented control + summary card, the decisive case: built one scenario against Traditional IRA and a second against Taxable Brokerage live (mirroring D067's Tier 3 setup exactly), with Taxable Brokerage as the page's *active* account (header strip reads "ACCOUNT FOR NEW SCENARIOS: Taxable Brokerage"). Opened the panel and selected the Traditional IRA scenario — the summary card correctly read "Scenario 1 · Traditional IRA ...2973 · VFITX · $3,000.00 · Est. tax $0.00," directly contradicting the page-level header shown one line above it. This is the specific mismatch self-audit finding 2 was written to prevent, confirmed live, not just asserted in a unit test.
- Full round-trip confirm against the Traditional IRA-selected scenario was **not** completed live — the sandboxed dev server's own outbound internet access failed for a real Groq call, both through the Browser pane (502, the pane's known proxy issue, D056/D066/D067) and via a direct `curl` to the real endpoint bypassing that proxy entirely (`{"error":"Groq API request failed: fetch failed"}` — a genuine network failure, not a validation or schema error, and not something a prompt/UI change could cause). Matches D056's already-documented finding that this environment's managed dev server itself lacks outbound internet access — flagged honestly rather than claimed. The failure-handling UI (honest message + working Retry) rendered correctly against this real failure, which is itself useful confirmation nothing in this session's refactor broke that path. The actual engine-execution correctness for a cross-account confirm is proven instead by the real-engine Vitest tests above (item 4) and by the existing Playwright modify-in-place test (item 5) — real validator, real engine, mocked only at the interpreter HTTP boundary, per this project's standing test-mocking rule.

**Baseline**: Vitest 157/157 (153 prior + 4 new), Playwright 5/5 (4 prior, 1 updated, 1 new), `tsc --noEmit` clean.

### D069 — [PM/PD/Dev] Systematic CD-1–CD-6 compliance audit against existing evidence only — real gaps found, none fixed yet, full table logged in CLAUDE.md §6

**Step 1 confirmed the task's own suspicion**: CLAUDE.md §6's bullet list is a compiled summary, not PRD 11's complete text — fetched the live Notion page directly rather than trusting the paraphrase. The summary omits CD-2.1/CD-2.3, CD-3.2–CD-3.5, CD-4.1/CD-4.3, and **CD-5 and CD-6 entirely** (never referenced anywhere in this file before today), plus PRD 11's own governing principle above CD-1 ("every AI-generated output must have a human-readable alternative, a plain non-AI path, or an explicit user control to bypass it — any one of the three"). Also confirmed directly: PRD 11 predates this project, uses the original app's Workflow A/B terms and Segment A–D personas by name (Strategic Optimizer/Routine Executor/Reactive Withdrawer/Newly Self-Directed) — it was never written with either of this project's two AI features in mind, which is exactly why reading it fresh rather than trusting a compiled summary mattered here.

**The full rule-by-rule table lives in `CLAUDE.md`, new subsection under §6** — a standing reference, maintained in place like the Implementation Map, not duplicated here. Headline findings, most significant first:

1. **CD-2.x is asserted to apply to Feature 2 by this project's own governing document (§6's header: "before any AI-generated output ships in EITHER feature"), and Feature 2 has zero segment-awareness anywhere** — grepped `whatIfPrompt.ts`/`whatIfShared.ts`/`whatIf.ts`/`WhatIfPanel.tsx`, no mention of "segment" at all. This was never an open interpretive question the way the task's kickoff framed it as a possibility ("if the spec never intended segment-awareness there, say so") — the project's own scoping language already answered it, and the gap was simply never built or ever logged as a deliberate deferral.

2. **Feature 1's Segment C and D tone instructions are cross-wired relative to PRD 11's real definitions, and neither has ever been checked against live output.** PRD's Segment C (Reactive Withdrawer, urgent sessions) should be the shortest, most front-loaded tone in the whole framework; this project's actual `SEGMENT_TONE.C` (`narrationPrompt.ts`) reads "two to three sentences... more explanatory than segment B" — the opposite direction. PRD's Segment D-specific instruction ("define financial terms inline on first use") is implemented on this project's C, not D; D is missing it, along with PRD's "normalize the decision" framing. This was invisible until now because `narration.test.ts` only ever constructs `segment: 'A'` inputs (one exception: category 10 additionally checks `segment: 'B'`, structurally only — it asserts the system prompt differs, never that real model output for B is actually compliant), and `docs/narration-review-sample.md` likewise only contains Segment A samples plus one Segment B comparison. C and D have literally never been generated once, by this project's own test suite or its own review-sample doc.

3. **One real CD-4.2 gap, consistent with this project's own established principle that a prompt instruction alone is not a guarantee (D052/D053).** Every other hard business rule in Feature 2 (SpecID on Traditional IRA, an amount exceeding the real balance) has a matching code-level check in `whatIfValidation.ts` specifically because a prompt alone was never trusted for those. The "never state a numeric tax/gain estimate in the confirm summary" rule (`whatIfPrompt.ts`) is the one exception — nothing in `whatIfValidation.ts` inspects the `summary` string at all, so a badly-behaved model slipping a computed-sounding figure into that pre-engine text would go uncaught.

4. **CD-1.1, CD-1.2, CD-1.3, CD-3.1, and CD-4.2 (aside from the one gap above) hold up well, on strong existing evidence, for both features** — not just "probably fine": CD-1.2's badge logic was confirmed by grepping for every place `ai_assisted`/`aiGenerated` is ever set (`ai_assisted: true` appears in exactly two places in the whole codebase, both inside `WhatIfPanel.tsx`'s confirm-success branches), and CD-1.3's confirm-gate was confirmed by grepping for every call site of `confirmWhatIfCandidate()` (exactly one, wired only to the real "Confirm" button) — architectural guarantees, not just passing test cases.

5. **CD-5 (session continuity) and CD-6 (feedback loops) are essentially unimplemented for both features**, but this reads as out-of-scope rather than as a failure: CD-5 governs deep-link/returning-user and mode-restore language that neither AI feature touches (pre-existing app-level behavior, if it exists at all elsewhere), and CD-6 is pure backend telemetry with no user-facing component — this project has no analytics/backend layer whatsoever (client-only prototype plus two serverless AI proxies), so the infrastructure CD-6.2/6.3 would need was never built by this project or the one it extends. The one item worth naming rather than waving away: Feature 2's conversation state is deliberately ephemeral (D053) and a closed panel discards an unconfirmed draft with zero warning — not a CD-5.3 violation (that rule governs a genuine "saved, come back later" promise this feature never makes), but flagged explicitly per this project's own standing engagement principle of surfacing an unresolved tradeoff rather than resolving it by silence.

**Explicitly deferred, not skipped**: whether Segment C/D's real model output — current or once fixed — actually reads as compliant can only be confirmed with a live call, which this audit's own instructions ruled out (constrained API credits, existing-evidence-only). Flagged as the natural next live-verification step once budget allows, not resolved either way here.

**No fixes made in this pass, per the task's explicit instruction** — gaps are reported in the table, not silently patched. `narrationPrompt.ts`, `whatIfPrompt.ts`, and `whatIfValidation.ts` are all unchanged from D067/D068's state.

**`docs/progress.html` regenerated** as part of this checkpoint (the task named this explicitly, noting it had already been missed once) — re-read `CLAUDE.md` and `DECISIONS.md` fresh and rewrote the summary from their current content, including this audit's headline findings, rather than hand-patching the prior version.

No code changes this session — `tsc --noEmit`/Vitest/Playwright baselines are unchanged from D068 (157/157, 5/5, clean) since nothing in `dev/` was touched.

### D070 — [PD] Incidental cleanup, not embedded-AI scope: Manual mode footer alignment fixed on both `FundSelectionManual2.tsx` and `FundSelectionManualLot.tsx`; a real, live-confirmed "Reset to system recommendation" visibility gap found and reported, not fixed

**Tagged explicitly as incidental visual QA, unrelated to Feature 1/Feature 2 work** — logged as its own entry per the task's own instruction, so it isn't mistaken for embedded-AI scope on a later review pass.

**The fix.** Both Manual mode screens' footers used a plain `flex gap-3 items-center` container with no `justify-between`/`justify-end`, left-justifying "Review order," "Go to Scenario Analysis," and "↩ Reset to system recommendation" together — the one inconsistent screen relative to Automated mode's own footer (`flex ... justify-end`, no left-pinned element since there's nothing to reset from) and Execution Confirmation's "View transaction history →" / "Start a new sale" pairing (`flex items-center justify-between`, secondary link pinned left, primary action right). Fixed both `FundSelectionManual2.tsx` and `FundSelectionManualLot.tsx` — genuinely the same bug in two places, not one: `FundSelectionManualLot.tsx`'s footer comment literally read "same as FS-MAN-2," confirming it wasn't an independent implementation that happened to match. Reused the existing `justify-between` pattern rather than inventing new spacing, exactly as asked: the reset link now sits alone on the left, "Review order" + "Go to Scenario Analysis" are grouped in a `flex gap-3 items-center` wrapper on the right, same visual slot the Execution Confirmation link/button pairing already establishes as this app's real convention.

### D071 — [PM/PD/Dev] Demo Settings dialog built: consolidated segment/provider/reset controls, real runtime provider switching, Feature 2's first segment-aware content — and the design decision the prior session's investigation deferred is now resolved by making segment an explicit user choice, not an inferred one

**This directly answers the open question the previous session's segment-definitions investigation left for the user**: PRD 04/03's segments are narrative/psychographic, not tied to concrete in-app behavioral signals, so no classifier could be honestly built from them. This task's own instruction resolved that cleanly — segment becomes an explicit, user-selected setting (the Demo Settings dialog's Section A), never inferred from scenario data. No classifier was built; none was needed.

**Self-audit, before any build, per the task's own instruction:**
1. **A reusable coach-mark component already exists** (`dev/src/components/CoachMark.tsx`) — self-positioning, localStorage-backed dismissal, already used with distinct `id`/`text` pairs across four existing sites (`nav-prototype`, `reset-demo`, `tax`, `txn-history`, others). Reused directly for the new "demo-settings" coach mark; no new tooltip system built, per the task's own explicit instruction not to.
2. **"Reset demo" confirmed exactly**: a plain `<a onClick={() => window.location.href = '/?reset=true'}>` in `L2Nav.tsx`, no confirmation guard anywhere. The `/?reset=true` handler (`FundSelectionEntry.tsx`) clears exactly two localStorage keys — `vsr_portfolio_state` and `vsr_coach_marks_dismissed` — dispatches a `vsr-reset` event, reloads the canonical dataset, and calls `resetSession()`. This is the precedent the new dialog's Section C and the new `demoSettings.ts` persistence module are both built against directly, not guessed at.
3. **Request/response shape confirmed** for both `dev/api/narrate.ts` and `dev/api/interpret.ts`: both validate `req.body` with a type-guard function, then pass it straight through to `getActiveGenerator()`/`getActiveInterpreter().interpret()` with no per-request provider concept at all — env var only. This is exactly why a `provider` field could be added as a clean, additive, optional sibling field on the request body (stripped out via destructuring before the rest of the body reaches the generator/interpreter) rather than requiring any restructuring.

**1. Demo Settings dialog built**, `dev/src/components/DemoSettingsDialog.tsx` — modal (this app's established scrim/centered-box convention, `ModeToggleGuard.tsx`'s `SaveDiscardDialog` / `ScenarioAnalysis.tsx`'s `DeleteDialog`, not a new pattern invented for this), three sections:
- **A — segment.** Four-way A/B/C/D control (new, small, local — see finding below), defaulting to A. Below it, a description card sourced from `dev/src/utils/segmentResearch.ts`: **trigger, goal, tool must, and tool must avoid quoted directly** from the real segment-research artifact (`mcasey10.github.io/vanguard-ai-pipeline/pm/contextual_segment_map.html`), fetched live and read via the Browser pane (not WebFetch's AI-summarized text, which risks paraphrasing something that needs to be an exact quote) — clicked through all four segment cards to capture the real per-segment fields. The source page's own "Likely workflow" field was deliberately **omitted**, not reproduced: it still uses the superseded "Workflow A/B" terminology (CLAUDE.md §3's glossary), and a quote gets reproduced exactly or not at all, never silently edited to swap out deprecated terms. For Segments C and D, the exact "In this app" text the task specified is shown below a dashed divider, in italic — visually distinct from the quoted research above it, exactly as asked.
- **B — AI model.** Two independent controls, "Narration of summaries" and "Scenario assistant" (no "Feature 1/2" language anywhere), each a 3-way Gemini/Anthropic/Groq selector, defaulting to Gemini (this project's documented code-level default, D042/D053 — independent of whatever a given dev machine's own `.env.local` happens to be set to).
- **C — Reset demo.** Its own visually distinct section (`#fdf1f0` warning background, `#7a1f14` text — not matching the neutral preference sections above). Clicking "Reset demo" now shows an inline confirm state — "Reset all demo data? This can't be undone." plus Reset demo/Cancel — before the `/?reset=true` navigation actually fires. Live-verified: clicking through to the confirm state, then Cancel, correctly aborts with no navigation and no state change.

**2. Coach mark on the Demo Settings button** — reused `CoachMark`, `id="demo-settings"`, text adapted from the pre-existing "reset-demo" coach mark's own description (restores portfolio/clears transactions/restores coach marks) plus a mention of the two new controls. Live-verified: renders correctly, positioned next to the button, dismissible.

**3. Segment wired for real.** All five previously-hardcoded `segment: 'A'` literals (`FundSelectionAutomated.tsx`, `FundSelectionManual2.tsx`, `ScenarioAnalysis.tsx`, `OrderConfirmation.tsx`, `ExecutionSummary.tsx`) now read `demoSettings.narrationSegment` from the store. **Feature 2 gained real segment-awareness for the first time** — `WhatIfInterpretationInput` gained a required `segment: NarrationSegment` field (reusing Feature 1's own type, not a duplicate), and `whatIfPrompt.ts` gained a `SEGMENT_TONE` map governing clarifying-question/confirm-summary tone. **Built fresh, correctly, from the real research — not copied from `narrationPrompt.ts`'s own same-named map**, which CLAUDE.md §6's compliance table already documents as having a real C/D content bug. This is a deliberate scope boundary, not an oversight: fixing that existing bug was explicitly out of scope (the prior session's investigation task said not to touch it pending review), but writing NEW content for a feature that had none before isn't "fixing a swap" — there was nothing there to preserve. Segment C's what-if tone: brevity, single trustworthy path, no optional extra work (grounded in the same real "fastest credible path... avoid scenario comparison, tax detail" quote used in the dialog). Segment D's: plain-language term definitions, orientation before interrogation (grounded in the same "comprehension before optimization... orientation before action" quote). Required, not optional-with-a-default — every one of the ~10 test call sites in `whatIf.test.ts` that construct a `WhatIfInterpretationInput` literal now states it explicitly, matching this project's own standing "no silent defaults for a field that actually varies" discipline.

**4. Provider selection made genuinely runtime-switchable.** `getActiveGenerator(override?)` / `getActiveInterpreter(override?)` (`narrationGenerator.ts` / `whatIfInterpreter.ts`) now take an optional per-request override that wins when present; `NARRATION_PROVIDER`/`WHATIF_PROVIDER` remain exactly as the fallback for any request that omits one, including this project's own headless tests — unchanged behavior for every existing caller. `dev/api/narrate.ts` and `dev/api/interpret.ts` both accept an optional `provider` sibling field on the request body, validated as a string if present, destructured out before the rest of the body is passed through. Client-side, `getNarration()`/`interpretWhatIfTurn()` both gained an optional `provider` parameter; `NarrationBlock`/`WhatIfPanel` both gained a `provider` prop sourced from `demoSettings`; the client-side narration cache key now folds provider into it too, so switching providers in the dialog doesn't silently reuse a stale cached result from a different one.

**Self-audit finding worth naming: no reusable segmented-control component exists, confirmed a second time.** Same finding D067 already made about the Automated/Manual toggle — the Demo Settings dialog's A/B/C/D control and its two provider controls are small, new, local components (`SegmentControl`/`ProviderControl` inside `DemoSettingsDialog.tsx`), styled to match the same established visual pattern rather than pretending to reuse something that isn't reusable. Worth noting for whoever eventually consolidates this — there are now three independent implementations of the same visual pattern (`FundSelectionAutomated.tsx`'s Automated/Manual pill, `WhatIfPanel.tsx`'s `ScenarioSegmentedControl`, and this dialog's two controls) — but that consolidation is a refactor decision outside this task's scope, not something to do unbidden.

**Test coverage added**, not just UI built: `narration.test.ts` and `whatIf.test.ts` each gained two tests for the override-wins-over-env-var / omitted-falls-back-to-env-var behavior. `whatIf.test.ts` gained three tests for the new `SEGMENT_TONE` content — one structural (all four segments produce identical grounding data but four genuinely distinct system prompts, mirroring narration's own category-10 figures-vs-tone split), two content-specific (Segment C's brevity instruction, Segment D's plain-language term-definition instruction, each asserting on real phrases traceable back to the quoted research).

**Live verification, all four things the task asked for:**
- **The dialog's three sections**: screenshotted open, all real content rendering correctly — segment card, both provider controls, reset section.
- **Segment switching**: clicked through to Segment C live — description card and "In this app" annotation both updated correctly, dashed-divider/italic styling confirmed visually distinct from the quoted fields above it.
- **The reset confirmation actually blocking accidental execution**: clicked "Reset demo," confirmed the inline confirm state appears ("Reset all demo data? This can't be undone."), clicked Cancel, confirmed it reverts with no navigation and no data loss — the exact guard that never existed before this task.
- **A real narration/assistant call using a non-default provider selected through the UI**: switched "Narration of summaries" to Groq live in the dialog, triggered a real recommendation — the Browser pane's own request went through its known 502 proxy issue (D056/D066/D067's already-documented, environment-specific flakiness), so confirmed the actual routing decisively via direct `curl` to the real endpoint instead (same evidentiary standard D067 established): a request with `"provider":"anthropic"` returned `{"error":"Anthropic API request failed: fetch failed"}`; the identical request with `provider` omitted returned `{"error":"Groq API request failed: fetch failed"}` (this session's own `.env.local` has `NARRATION_PROVIDER=groq`/`WHATIF_PROVIDER=groq` set) — proving the override wins when present and the env-var fallback still works correctly when it's not, both ends of the mechanism, live. Repeated identically against `/api/interpret` with `segment` and `provider` both present, same result. The `"fetch failed"` in every case is this sandboxed dev server's own lack of outbound internet access (D067's already-documented environment limitation, re-confirmed here, not a regression) — the routing/selection logic itself is proven correct; completing an actual live AI round-trip through this specific environment remains blocked exactly as it has been since D067, flagged honestly rather than glossed over. The narration UI itself degraded correctly on the failure — deterministic fallback rendered, no AI-generated badge, no crash, confirming D066's failure-handling logic is untouched and still correct after this change.

### D072 — [PD] Anthropic removed from the Demo Settings dropdowns — UI-level only, adapter code and tests untouched

This deployment has no `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` configured and none is planned, so offering "Anthropic (Claude)" as a selectable option in either Demo Settings provider dropdown (D071) let a user pick a choice that could never actually succeed — misleading regardless of whether the underlying adapter code works (it does).

**Scoped exactly as instructed — UI only.** Removed the `{ value: 'anthropic', label: 'Anthropic' }` entry from `PROVIDERS` in `DemoSettingsDialog.tsx` (commented out in place with a note on how to restore it, not deleted outright, since re-adding it is meant to read as a one-line change, not archaeology). Nothing else touched: `dev/src/server/generators/anthropicGenerator.ts`/`anthropicInterpreter.ts`, `getActiveGenerator()`/`getActiveInterpreter()`'s `GENERATORS`/`INTERPRETERS` registries (Anthropic is still fully selectable via `NARRATION_PROVIDER=anthropic`/`WHATIF_PROVIDER=anthropic` or a direct `provider: "anthropic"` request — only the dialog's own option list changed), and every existing Anthropic-adapter test in `narration.test.ts`/`whatIf.test.ts` — all still pass, confirming this really is additive removal at the UI layer, not a hidden architecture change.

**Documentation corrected to state the real distinction precisely**, per the task's own instruction: `CLAUDE.md`'s Demo Settings entry (§9) now says plainly that three provider adapters exist and are tested in code, while the dialog itself currently exposes only Gemini and Groq, and why — not "three providers" stated in a way that implied all three were live-selectable in the demo.

**Live-verified**: reset the demo, opened Demo Settings — both "Narration of summaries" and "Scenario assistant" show exactly two options (Gemini, Groq), no Anthropic anywhere. Also confirmed, incidentally, that this session's segment/provider selections (Segment C, Groq/Gemini from D071's own live testing) survived the `/?reset=true` reset exactly as D071 designed — demo settings are a separate persisted key from portfolio state, working as intended.
