### D073 — [Dev] Provider dropdowns fixed: diagnosed as a genuine logic gap (not styling), the client-side "default" was never actually resolved from the real env var, a stale persisted choice could become permanently orphaned, and — found while fixing it — two of the five narration touchpoints never got segment/provider wired in D071 at all

**Diagnosis, per the task's explicit instruction to determine which before touching any styling: this is entirely a logic gap, not a rendering gap.** `ProviderControl`'s active-state class logic (`value === p.value ? 'bg-vg-teal...' : ...`) is structurally identical to `SegmentControl`'s and was never broken — confirmed live: it correctly highlighted Gemini on a fresh load and correctly highlighted a valid persisted Groq choice, both *before* any fix in this entry. The real problem was upstream, in what value the component was ever being asked to render as active, and it was actually two compounding bugs, not one:

1. **No real default resolution ever existed.** `DEFAULTS.narrationProvider`/`whatifProvider` in `demoSettings.ts` was a hardcoded `'gemini'` guess — correct only by coincidence for a deployment where the real env var happens to also be Gemini. This session's own `.env.local` has `NARRATION_PROVIDER=groq`/`WHATIF_PROVIDER=groq` set, so the dialog was showing "Gemini" as active while the real, actually-in-effect default was Groq the whole time — confirmed by directly reading `getActiveGenerator().name`'s real resolution logic, not inferred.
2. **A persisted choice could become permanently orphaned.** D072 removed `'anthropic'` from the dropdown's own option list but never touched `loadDemoSettings()`'s validation, which still accepted any of the three `DemoProvider` values as "valid" — so a value saved before D072 (or written directly) stayed exactly as `'anthropic'` forever, matching neither of the two rendered buttons. **This second bug is what actually produces the reported symptom** ("neither Gemini nor Groq shows active") — bug 1 alone would show the *wrong* one highlighted (Gemini instead of the real Groq default), not *neither* highlighted; it took the orphaned-value case to reproduce "neither" exactly, and it was reproduced live before any fix: cleared just the `vsr_demo_settings` key (not a full reset) with a hand-set `narrationProvider:"anthropic"`, reloaded, opened the dialog — neither button lit up, exactly as reported.

**Fixed, per the task's own instruction that the client needs to know what the server-side default actually resolved to, not guess at it:**
- `DemoSettings.narrationProvider`/`whatifProvider` are now `DemoProvider | null` — `null` means "no explicit choice," a real, distinct state from "chose Gemini."
- New endpoint, `dev/api/demo-config.ts` (`GET`, dev-server parity wired into `vite.config.ts` alongside the other two) — reports `getActiveGenerator().name`/`getActiveInterpreter().name`, the *exact* real resolution function every live narrate/interpret request already uses (no network call, no calculation — both functions just pick an adapter object synchronously). Fetched once per dialog open via `fetchServerProviderDefaults()`, used only when no explicit choice exists.
- `OFFERED_PROVIDERS` (`demoSettings.ts`) is now the single shared list both `DemoSettingsDialog.tsx`'s own dropdown options and `loadDemoSettings()`'s reconciliation check use — a stored value that isn't currently offered (the orphaned-`'anthropic'` case, or any future removal) now reconciles to `null` on load instead of persisting as an unmatchable string forever. Closes the actual root cause, not just this one instance of it.
- **Styling reused exactly as asked** — no new "selected" treatment invented; `ProviderControl` uses the identical `bg-vg-teal`/border-fill classes `SegmentControl` already had, unchanged.
- **Reset behavior corrected, per the task's explicit step 5 requirement** — this is a deliberate, narrower correction of D071's original "nothing in Demo Settings resets" stance, not a full reversal: segment still survives "Reset demo" (D071's reasoning for *that* still stands — it's a UI-tone preference), but providers now explicitly revert to `null` on a full reset, via the store's own `setDemoSettings({ narrationProvider: null, whatifProvider: null })` call in `FundSelectionEntry.tsx`'s reset effect — not a standalone `localStorage` write, since that reset flow uses client-side `navigate()`, not a real page reload, so only a store-level update is guaranteed to be picked up immediately (the same reasoning `setPortfolio(loadPortfolio())` already established for portfolio state in that same effect).

**Found while fixing this, not part of the original ask, fixed anyway as directly in-scope**: grepping every call site for `demoSettings` while wiring the provider fix turned up that **two of the five narration touchpoints never actually got segment/provider wired in D071 at all** — `OrderConfirmation.tsx` (still hardcoded `segment: 'A'`, no `provider` prop, `demoSettings` never even destructured from the store) and `FundSelectionManual2.tsx`'s `ActiveFundRow` (same gap — its `NarrationBlock` call had no `provider`, and the `narrationInput` builder one level up was still `segment: 'A'`). Both fixed here: `demoSettings` added to each page's store destructure, `segment`/`provider` wired through exactly like the other three touchpoints already were. `ActiveFundRow` gained a new `narrationProvider?: string` prop threaded from its one real call site. This is a real, direct consequence of this task's own instruction to trace the actual data flow rather than patch symptoms — tracing "does every provider-consuming site actually get a real value" surfaced it.

**Test coverage added**: new file `dev/src/utils/demoSettings.test.ts` (10 tests) — `loadDemoSettings()` covering the empty-storage default, a valid round-trip, **the actual orphaned-value bug directly** (a stored `'anthropic'` choice reconciles to `null`), corrupted JSON, and an invalid segment value; `fetchServerProviderDefaults()` covering a successful resolution, a resolved-but-not-offered value mapping to `null`, a network failure, and a non-OK HTTP response — all via a minimal in-memory `localStorage` mock (this Vitest suite runs in a Node environment with no real browser storage; the mock is test infrastructure only, not something the real module needs).

**Live-verified, all three scenarios the task specified, in order:**
1. **Fresh page load, no prior choice** (cleared `vsr_demo_settings` entirely, normal reload — not `/?reset=true`): both rows correctly highlighted **Groq** — the real, actual `.env.local` default in this deployment — not the old hardcoded "Gemini" guess. This is the single most decisive piece of live evidence: the old code would have shown Gemini here, wrongly.
2. **Explicit choice persists across reload**: switched "Narration of summaries" to Gemini (the non-default option here, since Groq already was the default — the meaningful persistence test in this environment is overriding *away* from the real default, not toward it), reloaded the page fresh: Gemini correctly still showed active, "Scenario assistant" correctly remained at Groq (untouched, independent per-row state confirmed).
3. **Full reset reverts to the env default, not the persisted choice**: triggered Reset demo's confirm flow from inside the dialog, confirmed: "Narration of summaries" reverted from the just-set Gemini back to **Groq**, matching the real env var again. Confirmed at the data layer too, not just visually — `localStorage.getItem('vsr_demo_settings')` read back `{"narrationSegment":"A","narrationProvider":null,"whatifProvider":null}` immediately after reset.

**Baseline**: Vitest 174/174 (164 prior + 10 new), Playwright 5/5, `tsc --noEmit` clean.

### D074 — [Dev] Feature 1's Segment C/D tone-instruction swap fixed, using segmentResearch.ts's own real quoted fields as the literal shared source, not a re-derived paraphrase

The original, never-fixed bug D069's compliance audit found: `narrationPrompt.ts`'s `SEGMENT_TONE.C` carried Segment D's real trait (define jargon for a less-familiar audience) while `SEGMENT_TONE.D` was missing that and its own real "orientation before action" framing; PRD's actual Segment C (Reactive Withdrawer) should be the *shortest, most urgent* tone in the whole framework, and the pre-fix instruction had it backwards ("two to three sentences... more explanatory than segment B").

**Fixed by importing `SEGMENT_PROFILES` from `segmentResearch.ts` directly into `narrationPrompt.ts`** — the same module built for Feature 2's own segment-aware content (D071) — and interpolating its real quoted `goal`/`toolMustAvoid` fields straight into the corrected `SEGMENT_TONE.C`/`.D` strings, rather than re-typing the research a third time. This is the literal reading of "one source of truth for this content across both features," not just a conceptual consistency: `narrationPrompt.ts` and `whatIfPrompt.ts` now both read from the exact same exported constant, so the two features' segment content cannot drift apart the way it apparently already had once. Segments A and B were left untouched — the task and the original D069 finding both name only the C/D swap, and A/B were never flagged as wrong.

- **C, corrected**: shortest possible phrasing, one front-loaded sentence, no subordinate clauses, no suggestion to pause/compare/reconsider — grounded in the real quoted goal ("Fastest credible path to executing a specific dollar amount") and "tool must avoid" ("Scenario comparison, tax detail, anything that reads as optional extra work").
- **D, corrected**: reassuring plain-language tone, jargon defined inline on first use (the real "cost basis" worked example, moved here from where it was misplaced on C), outcome acknowledged before explained — grounded in the real quoted goal ("Comprehension before optimization — understand what is happening") and "tool must avoid" ("Leading with a recommendation before establishing comprehension").

**Test coverage added** (this suite had never exercised C or D at all before now — exactly how the swap went unnoticed for as long as it did): `narration.test.ts` gained a structural test (all four segments produce identical figures, four genuinely distinct system prompts) and two content tests per corrected segment, each asserting both a real phrase (e.g. "shortest possible phrasing," "cost basis") and a literal `toContain(SEGMENT_PROFILES.X.goal)` check — proving the prompt text is actually built from the shared constant, not just coincidentally similar to it.

**Not live-verified against a real model call** — this environment has no outbound internet access (established repeatedly this session, most recently D073), so whether the corrected C/D prompts actually produce compliant real output is explicitly deferred, exactly as D069's own audit already flagged it would need to be. The fix itself — correct content, correctly sourced, structurally tested — is complete; the live-output confirmation is the next step once API access allows, not resolved here.

**Baseline**: Vitest 177/177 (174 prior + 3 new), Playwright 5/5, `tsc --noEmit` clean.

### D075 — [Dev] The missing CD-4.2 validator added for Feature 2's confirm-summary rule — two real design mistakes found and fixed by this file's own tests before anything shipped, not live

D069's audit found the one real inconsistency in Feature 2's validation architecture: every other hard rule (SpecID/Traditional-IRA, amount-exceeds-balance) has a code-level validator backing its prompt instruction; "never a numeric tax or gain estimate in the confirm summary" (`whatIfPrompt.ts`) had none. Added `validateSummary()` to `whatIfValidation.ts`, called from `whatIf.ts`'s `downgradeIfInvalid()` alongside `validateCandidate()` for every "confirm" result — before it's ever shown to the user, same as every other check in this file.

**Two designs were tried and rejected in this same session, both caught by this file's own first test runs, not found live:**
1. **Ban the words "tax"/"gain"/etc. outright, via `.includes()`.** Broke immediately on the suite's own category-1 fixture — "MinTax" (a real, expected cost-basis method name in nearly every summary this feature produces) contains the literal substring "tax."
2. **Ban the words with proper `\b` word-boundary matching.** Fixed the MinTax false positive, but then broke category 3's real fixture — "Raise $20,000.00 optimizing for the lowest tax impact" legitimately names "tax" as the user's *stated objective* (an automated, tax-first request), not a computed estimate, and got refused anyway.

**What actually ships**: a dollar figure is flagged only when it is BOTH (a) not among the candidate's own real, legitimate amounts (`targetSaleAmount`, each fund's `sell_amount` — the actual figures the user or the structured candidate already state) AND (b) sitting within a short proximity window of one of the forbidden words. Neither signal alone is sufficient — a bare word ban false-positives on legitimate objective language; a bare unrecognized-figure check would need no keyword at all and could false-positive on any restated total (e.g. a lot's real dollar value) that just isn't literally present on the top-level candidate. The combination is what an actual pre-computed tax/gain estimate looks like: a number nobody gave the model, stated right next to the word for the thing it isn't allowed to compute yet.

**Test coverage**: `whatIf.test.ts` gained a dedicated `describe('validateSummary — D075...)` block — genuine figures-free summaries accepted, the real objective-shaped false positive from design 2 above kept as a permanent regression test, all five forbidden terms tested via `test.each`, the refusal reason confirmed generic/non-technical (never echoes which word matched), and an adversarial `interpretWhatIfTurnWith` test proving the orchestration layer — not just the validator function in isolation — actually downgrades a leaking confirm to `refuse` before it would ever reach the UI.

**Baseline**: Vitest 187/187 (177 prior + 10 new), Playwright 5/5, `tsc --noEmit` clean.

### D076 — [PD/Dev] Confirmation added before discarding an unconfirmed Scenario assistant conversation on close

D069's audit named this specifically: closing the panel with an unconfirmed, in-progress conversation discarded it silently, no warning, ever. Added `hasUnconfirmedProgress()` to `WhatIfPanel.tsx` — true when a user message (or an unsent draft in the input box) exists *since the last successfully-confirmed result*, not "any message ever": a panel re-opened after already confirming once, just to look around, has nothing left to lose by closing, and shouldn't be interrupted by a prompt about work that's already safely in Scenario Analysis. Both close triggers (the × button and the scrim click) now route through `handleCloseAttempt()`, which shows a new confirm dialog — "Discard this conversation? This request hasn't been confirmed yet. Closing now will discard it — nothing has been added to Scenario Analysis." / Keep editing / Discard — only when that's genuinely true; otherwise the panel still closes immediately, exactly as before. Same centered-modal convention as this app's other confirm dialogs (`ScenarioAnalysis.tsx`'s `DeleteDialog`, `ModeToggleGuard.tsx`'s `SaveDiscardDialog`), not a new pattern invented for this.

**Live-verified, all three states**: closing a freshly-opened panel (nothing typed) closed immediately, no prompt. Typing a draft and attempting to close correctly showed the prompt; "Keep editing" correctly aborted the close with the draft still intact in the input box. Attempting to close again and clicking "Discard" correctly closed the panel with no scenario added.

**Test coverage**: new Playwright test in `whatif-panel.spec.ts` covering the same three states — no-prompt-when-nothing-typed, prompt-when-a-draft-exists, Keep-editing aborts and preserves the draft, Discard actually closes.

**Baseline**: Vitest 187/187 (unchanged — pure UI-state logic, no backend behavior to add unit coverage for beyond what the new Playwright test already covers end-to-end), Playwright 6/6 (5 prior + 1 new), `tsc --noEmit` clean.

### D077 — [PD] Manual-mode footer alignment (D070) confirmed still shipped and correct — no fix needed

Checked whether D070's fix had actually persisted through the several sessions of subsequent work in this same area (D071–D076 all touched adjacent `WhatIfPanel.tsx`/store code) before assuming it needed redoing. It had: `FundSelectionManual2.tsx` and `FundSelectionManualLot.tsx` both still use the `flex items-center justify-between` layout from D070 (reset link pinned left, "Review order"/"Go to Scenario Analysis" grouped right), confirmed by reading the source directly, then confirmed live — navigated to `/manual-2`, screenshotted the footer, matches the intended layout exactly. ### D078 — [PD/Dev] Retirement-age tax assumption and filing-status assumption both made explicit in the dataset — two previously-unstated qualifying conditions, closed together

A prior investigation had confirmed by direct search that neither "Roth IRA distributions are tax-free" nor "no early-withdrawal penalty applies" was ever connected to *why* those hold for this specific investor — both were asserted flatly in `sample-dataset.json`'s existing `note_tax_treatment` fields. Fixed with a new sibling field, `dataset_metadata.note_retirement_status`, following the exact documentation style of the existing `note_investor_age`/`note_tax_treatment`/`note_specific_lot_id` fields: it states plainly that the investor's age (73, per `note_investor_age`) is what makes both conditions valid — age 59½+ satisfies the "qualified distribution" test for Roth (exceeded by 13+ years) and the account's 20+ years of accumulation satisfies the 5-year holding requirement; the same age threshold is what makes the 10% early-withdrawal penalty inapplicable. Explicitly flagged as an assumption that does not carry over automatically — if the dataset's investor age is ever changed, both conditions need re-verification, not silent inheritance.

**A second, related gap found during this same item, not left sitting unaddressed next to the first**: the dataset specifies no filing status anywhere, and the new tax-bracket dialog (D079) needs one to determine which of its three tables applies. Confirmed via direct grep (zero matches for `filing_status`/`marital`) that nothing in the dataset states or implies one, and no co-holder/spouse data exists to infer one from. Resolved as an explicit decision — Single — documented the same way as the retirement-age assumption, in a new `note_filing_status` field: reasoned from (a) a single named investor with no co-holder anywhere, and (b) consistency with the app's own pre-existing default rates (24% ST / 15% LT), which are a real, valid Single-filer combination for taxable income $105,701–$201,775 in the real 2026 bracket data — not a coincidence to silently rely on, but reasoning made explicit so a future change to either the default rates or the assumed filing status is deliberate.

**Baseline**: unchanged (JSON dataset edit only, no code touched) — Vitest 206/206, Playwright 6/6, `tsc --noEmit` clean.

### D079 — [Dev] Tax bracket "Change" control built for the first time — confirmed completely inert everywhere first, then built as a unified filing-status/income-band picker, not free-text entry or two independent table lookups

Self-audit before building (per the task's own explicit instruction not to assume) confirmed the "Change" link next to the header's TAX BRACKET figure had zero `onClick` handler in all five locations it appears (`ScenarioAnalysis.tsx`, `FundSelectionAutomated.tsx`, `FundSelectionManual.tsx`, `FundSelectionManual2.tsx`, `FundSelectionManualLot.tsx`) — genuinely non-functional everywhere, not partially wired. Fixed identically in all five, including `FundSelectionManual.tsx` even though the same audit found that route registered but unreachable from live navigation (fixed anyway for consistency).

**Design reasoning, not just "add a dropdown"**: the federal ordinary-income brackets and the long-term capital-gains brackets don't share threshold boundaries — Single's 12% ordinary bracket ($12,401–$50,400) straddles the LT table's $49,000 0%/15% boundary, and the 35% bracket straddles the $541,350 15%/20% boundary (with analogous splits in the MFJ and Head of Household tables). Picking a row from either table independently would be genuinely ambiguous for anyone in a straddling bracket — free-form numeric income entry was also rejected, since the two-tables problem exists regardless of how income is captured. Built instead as a single unified list per filing status (`dev/src/data/taxBrackets2026.ts`), merging both real tables into one ordered set of sub-ranges where both rates are simultaneously constant, so one selection determines both `st_rate` and `lt_rate` at once, shown for confirmation before Apply. `validateTaxBracketTable()` enforces bounds on the static table itself (contiguous from $0, open-ended top row, rates in `[0,1]`, no gaps/overlaps) at module load — this is data-structure validation, not user-input validation, since the UI has no free-text field to validate.

**Two real data gaps in the source material, both resolved explicitly rather than worked around**: (1) Married Filing Separately has no sourced 2026 LT capital-gains data — scoped out of the dialog's filing-status options entirely (3 offered, not 4, not guessed); (2) the dataset specifies no filing status at all — resolved as Single and documented in D078/`note_filing_status`, not left as a second unstated assumption sitting next to the first.

**Why this is sequenced before the Traditional IRA ordinary-income tax calculation, which it otherwise looks unrelated to**: once this dialog works, changing the rate live and watching the IRA tax figure respond becomes a real, interactive way to verify that calculation once it's built, rather than needing to hand-edit the dataset to test different rate scenarios. This dialog is a prerequisite tool for validating the next task, not a detour from it.

**Live-verified past a hard-navigation false start**: an initial verification attempt hit `activeTaxRates` resetting after a hard `navigate()` call (pre-existing, expected in-memory-only behavior, not a bug — confirmed against the same non-persistence already established for this state in an earlier session) — re-verified correctly within a single page load instead: on `/automated`, selected Married Filing Jointly → $100,801–$211,400 (22% ordinary / 15% LT) → Apply, confirmed the header updated to "22% ST / 15% LT," then ran a live calculation and confirmed VTSAX's `EST. TAX` figure computed to exactly $58.18 for a $264.46 ST gain (`264.46 × 0.22`) — proving the new rate flows into a real recalculation, not just the header display.

**Test coverage**: 19 new tests, `dev/src/data/taxBrackets2026.test.ts` — all three tables' bounds validation and contiguity, MFS's deliberate absence, adversarial coverage of `validateTaxBracketTable` itself (empty/not-$0-start/not-open-ended/bad-rate/gap/overlap/valid), both named ambiguous splits asserted as literal row values, the current app default (24%/15%) confirmed to match the real Single $105,701–$201,775 row, and `formatIncomeRange` for both bounded and open-ended ranges.

**Baseline**: Vitest 206/206 (187 prior + 19 new), Playwright 6/6, `tsc --noEmit` clean.

### D080 — [PD] Three conditional-formatting rules traced and documented ahead of the IRA banner redesign — investigation and documentation only, no behavior changed

The upcoming Traditional IRA banner redesign will touch displays that already use conditional coloring, and conflating what looked like three similar rules risked a real regression. Traced each to its actual implementation rather than assuming from visual similarity, per this file's own standing "verify, don't assume" rule — documented as new §13 in `CLAUDE.md`.

**Rule 1 (gain/loss figures) confirmed as hypothesized**: sign-based, three states — positive green, negative red, exactly zero neutral (not a plain binary) — identical across all four real locations (`ScenarioAnalysis.tsx`, `FundSelectionManualLot.tsx` ×2 call sites, `FundSelectionManual2.tsx`).

**Rule 2 (per-fund allocation IMPACT badge) refuted the stated hypothesis, not confirmed it.** It is not toward/away-from-target coloring. Traced `impact_pct`'s actual computation (`buildFundResult()`, `dev/src/engine/index.ts`) and found it is simply `afterPct − beforePct` for that asset class — no target percentage enters the calculation at all. The color rule (`impact_pct <= 0 ? green : red`, identical across `FundSelectionAutomated.tsx`, `FundSelectionManual2.tsx`, `FundSelectionManualLot.tsx`) is a fixed sign convention — reducing a fund's own asset-class share is always green, increasing it is always red — regardless of whether that asset class is actually over- or under-target. Worth having checked rather than assumed: a fund that's currently underweight would still render red for a sale that moves it further toward target, which a toward/away framing would get backwards.

**Rule 3 ("Gap to target" rows, Scenario Analysis) confirmed as hypothesized**: magnitude-based via one shared `diffColor()` function, independent of sign — `Math.abs(diff) >= 0.5` red, otherwise neutral black. No green state exists in this rule at all, unlike Rules 1 and 2.

All three are genuinely independent rules with different semantics (reward positive vs. negative; reward decrease regardless of target; reward smallness of gap regardless of sign) — documented as a register in `CLAUDE.md` §13 specifically so the IRA banner work picks deliberately rather than copying whichever existing convention looks closest.

**Baseline**: unchanged (documentation only, no source touched) — Vitest 206/206, Playwright 6/6, `tsc --noEmit` clean.

### D081 — [Dev] Tax bracket dialog's selection now persists across close/reopen, via the same demoSettings.ts mechanism already storing the other Demo Settings

The dialog previously reopened with no memory of the prior selection — every open started from a blank filing-status/income-band state even though `activeTaxRates` itself was still in effect. Fixed by extending `DemoSettings` (`demoSettings.ts`) with two new fields, `taxBracketFilingStatus`/`taxBracketIncomeMin`, persisted to the same `vsr_demo_settings` localStorage key already used for segment/provider choices — reused, not a bespoke second persistence path, per the task's own explicit "for consistency" instruction.

**Stores the real row identifiers, not just the resulting rates**, per the task's own stated reasoning: `minIncome` uniquely identifies a row within its filing status's table, where the two resulting rates alone could not — nothing in `taxBrackets2026.ts`'s real data actually produces a duplicate rate pair today, but the persistence is correct by construction rather than by coincidence of today's data.

**Pairing validation, following D073's own precedent for orphaned values**: `loadDemoSettings()` treats filing status and income-band as one atomic pair, not two independently-reconciled fields — a stored filing status with no matching row in that status's real table (e.g. after a future bracket-table edit) reconciles *both* fields to null, never a half-state where a filing-status pill would show selected with no income-band row to highlight.

**Reset behavior, a deliberate call not specified by the task**: "Reset demo" (`FundSelectionEntry.tsx`'s `/?reset=true` handler) now also clears both new fields to null, extending D073's existing provider-clearing call rather than leaving them out. Reasoning: that reset flow begins with a genuine hard navigation, so the Zustand store's `activeTaxRates` has already reverted to its 24%/15% default by the time this effect runs — leaving a stale persisted filing-status/band pair around would reopen the dialog showing a selection that no longer matches the actual (reset) rate, the same category of staleness bug D073 fixed for providers. Segment is not affected by this change — it remains a UI-tone preference that survives reset (D071's original reasoning, unchanged); this is a narrower, tax-bracket-specific addition to the "does this survive reset" list.

**Test coverage**: 3 new tests in `demoSettings.test.ts` — a valid persisted pair round-trips correctly; an unrecognized filing status (e.g. `'married_filing_separately'`) reconciles both fields to null, not just the invalid one; a valid filing status paired with a `minIncome` matching no real row also reconciles both to null. All prior tests in that file updated for the two new fields.

**Live-verified**: applied Married Filing Jointly / $100,801–$211,400 (22% ST / 15% LT) on `/automated`, confirmed `vsr_demo_settings` in localStorage held `{"taxBracketFilingStatus":"married_filing_jointly","taxBracketIncomeMin":100801,...}`, then reopened the dialog in the same page load and confirmed both the "Married Filing Jointly" pill and the $100,801–$211,400 row rendered as selected — not just that the header still showed the applied rate.

**Baseline**: Vitest 209/209 (206 prior + 3 new), Playwright 6/6, `tsc --noEmit` clean.

### D082 — [PD] Per-fund Impact badge's misleading-display gap documented as a known, pre-existing issue — not fixed

D080 traced the badge's actual color logic and refuted the "toward/away from target" hypothesis: `impact_pct <= 0 ? green : red`, where `impact_pct` is a raw `afterPct − beforePct` delta with no `target_allocation` in the computation at all. This session's task made the consequence explicit and put it on record: a Manual-mode sale that makes an already-underweight asset class *more* underweight can still render green, because the badge has no way to know the move was harmful — it was never told what the target is. A user who reasonably reads green as "this helped my allocation" can be misled in exactly the case where it didn't.

Documented in `CLAUDE.md` §5's Business logic constraints, in the same "flagged, not fixed" style and physical location as the existing D052 SpecID/Traditional-IRA gap — this project's established home for a confirmed-but-deliberately-unfixed defect — plus a cross-reference added to §13 Rule 2 pointing back to it, so either entry point (constraints list or formatting-rules register) leads to the other. Explicitly noted as a pre-existing characteristic of the original app's badge logic, not something introduced by any AI feature or by recent work in this project.

**Not fixed, by the task's own explicit instruction** — this is on record specifically so the upcoming Traditional IRA banner redesign (which will reuse conditional-coloring conventions per §13) doesn't get built assuming this badge already means what it visually implies.

**Baseline**: unchanged (documentation only, no source touched) — Vitest 209/209, Playwright 6/6, `tsc --noEmit` clean.

### D083 — [Dev] Traditional IRA ordinary-income tax computed for real at the portfolio level; per-fund gross tax now an explicit not-applicable sentinel, never a numeric $0, for both IRA types

Calculation and types only, deliberately narrow — no new summary banner cards, no narration changes, no early-withdrawal-penalty UI. The full banner redesign is an explicit follow-up, not part of this entry. This makes `EST. NET TAX` (the one existing display surface already reading `Recommendation.est_net_tax` on Automated mode's Fund Selection page) correctly reflect Traditional IRA's real ordinary-income tax for the first time — previously hardcoded to `$0` by the same `taxable_brokerage`-only gate that correctly zeroes Roth IRA.

**Self-audit confirmed the three named call sites first** (`buildFundResult()`, the automated-mode per-fund override, the automated-mode portfolio-level net-tax computation, all `engine/index.ts`) before touching any of them, per the task's own instruction — matching the earlier investigation's description exactly, not assumed.

**Item 1 — per-fund sites return `'not_applicable'`, never `0`, for IRA holdings.** New shared type `TaxFigureOrNA = number | 'not_applicable'` (`types/index.ts`), applied to `FundSaleResult.est_tax_gross` and `ScenarioFundSelection.est_tax_gross`. `buildFundResult()` now initializes `estTaxGross` to `'not_applicable'` and only overwrites it with a real number inside the `taxable_brokerage` branch — no separate "is this IRA" check, the exclusion is the absence of any other branch, made explicit with a comment at each of the (now four, not two) sites that gate on `taxable_brokerage` — the automated and manual per-fund overrides in `engine/index.ts` each got a short comment stating the exclusion is deliberate, referencing the fact that ordinary-income tax is a portfolio-level figure, not a per-fund one, so a future reader doesn't "fix" this by adding per-fund logic that was intentionally excluded.

**Single source of truth propagated to every real "EST. TAX" per-fund display, not just the engine's own touchpoints — five real render sites, not four**: `FundSelectionAutomated.tsx`, `FundSelectionManual2.tsx`, `FundSelectionManualLot.tsx`'s collapsed-fund banner, `ScenarioAnalysis.tsx`'s saved-scenario table, and `OrderConfirmation.tsx`'s own per-fund summary table (found while tracing the type change — a fifth real render site this task's self-audit hadn't enumerated, since it's a locally-typed `ConfirmData.funds[].estTaxGross`, not a direct read of `FundSaleResult`). Two new shared helpers in `utils/format.ts` — `formatTaxFigure()` (→ `"N/A"` for display) and `taxFigureToNumber()` (→ `0`, for the handful of arithmetic/aggregation sites — `verify.ts`, `engine.test.ts`, one `narration.test.ts` sum — that don't care about the distinction and previously summed a real `0` there anyway). **Deliberately NOT touched**: `FundSelectionManualLot.tsx`'s separate, locally-recomputed live banner tax figure (~line 1037, computes its own gross tax from `bannerData` rather than reading `fr.est_tax_gross` at all) — outside this task's named scope, flagged below as a related gap rather than silently fixed.

**Item 2 — the portfolio-level branch, explicit three-way, not a two-way gate**: `taxable_brokerage` (existing ST/LT netting formula, byte-for-byte unchanged) / `traditional_IRA` (**new**: `totalSaleAmount × st_rate`, `totalSaleAmount` reordered to compute once, before the branch, as the real sum of every fund's `sell_amount` in this transaction — not a per-fund figure summed after the fact incorrectly, confirmed correct for a real 2-fund case below) / `roth_IRA` and anything else (`0`, its own branch, no longer a shared fallthrough with `traditional_IRA` now that it has real logic, and no longer sharing D057's old "everything non-taxable" gate). The `st_rate` choice is commented at the exact line: short-term capital gains are taxed at the taxpayer's ordinary marginal rate under US tax law — there is no separate ST schedule — so `st_rate` already **is** this investor's ordinary income rate, not a coincidence of the sample data.

**Item 3 — early-withdrawal-penalty groundwork, engine/types only**: new `Recommendation.est_early_withdrawal_penalty: TaxFigureOrNA`, always `'not_applicable'` — no calculation performed, no UI reads it anywhere. Placed on `Recommendation`, not `FundSaleResult`, because a withdrawal penalty (like ordinary-income tax) is a portfolio/withdrawal-level concept, not a per-fund one; not added to `ManualConfiguration` (which has no portfolio-level tax figure at all today) — my call, noted here rather than left implicit, since the task said "on whatever result structure makes sense." Comment references `sample-dataset.json`'s `note_retirement_status` (D078) for why this is always inapplicable for this dataset's single, age-73 investor.

**Item 4 — real engine calls, new `describe` block in `engine.test.ts`** (8 new tests): Traditional IRA single-fund ($10,000 → exactly $2,400 net tax = 10,000 × 24%, confirmed against real account `ACCT-TRAD-IRA-001`/VFITX); Traditional IRA multi-fund ($150,000 forces both VBTLX + VFITX to sell, confirmed ≥2 funds actually sold, net tax = the real summed `sell_amount` total × st_rate — the specific regression this task's self-audit flagged, not just a single-fund sanity check); Roth IRA regression (still exactly `$0`, now that it no longer shares a branch with Traditional IRA); taxable brokerage regression (`$64.85` on the existing $25,000 fixture, byte-identical to before the rewrite); and a dedicated assertion that per-fund `est_tax_gross` is `'not_applicable'` for every fund in both IRA fixtures even though the Traditional IRA portfolio total is genuinely nonzero — locking in item 1's design decision as checked behavior, not just a comment. Three pre-existing raw-engine-value assertions in `whatIf.test.ts` (checking `fr.est_tax_gross` directly, not narration's coerced view of it) updated from `.toBe(0)` to `.toBe('not_applicable')` for two Roth IRA cases and one Traditional IRA case — genuine behavior changes at the type level, not incidental churn.

**Item 5 — live-verified past a real UX snag, not glossed over**: switched to Traditional IRA on `/automated`, sold $10,000 of VFITX — `EST. NET TAX` showed a real `$2,400.00` (24% default) and the per-fund `EST. TAX` correctly showed `N/A`. Opened the tax bracket dialog (D081, now persisting), applied Single / Over $640,600 (37% ST / 20% LT) — the header rate updated immediately, but `EST. NET TAX` stayed at the stale `$2,400.00` even after clicking "Recalculate." Diagnosed before assuming a bug: `FundSelectionAutomated.tsx`'s Recalculate button is `disabled={inputDollars <= 0 || (!!recommendation && !isDirty)}`, and `isDirty` only tracks whether the **dollar amount** has changed since the last calculation — a tax-bracket change alone doesn't dirty it, by pre-existing design (`activeTaxRates` is deliberately excluded from the mount/account-change `useEffect`'s dependency array per D060's own reasoning, and Recalculate is the only other path). This is correct-as-designed, not a regression from this task's changes — retyping the identical `10000` correctly left `isDirty` false for the same reason. Changed the amount to `$12,000` and recalculated: `EST. NET TAX` showed `$4,440.00` (= 12,000 × 37%) with a `37.00% effective rate`, and per-fund `EST. TAX` still `N/A` — confirms the new rate genuinely flows through a fresh calculation, which is what item 5 asked to verify.

**Related gaps found and deliberately NOT fixed, flagged instead** (same "confirmed but out of scope" discipline as D052's SpecID gap and D082's Impact badge gap): (1) Manual mode has no equivalent portfolio-level ordinary-income-tax fix — `scenarioBuilder.ts`'s `buildScenarioFromFundResults()` and `OrderConfirmation.tsx`'s manual-mode branch (lines ~116–126) each independently reimplement the same `taxable_brokerage`-only ST/LT netting gate this task fixed in exactly one place (`engine/index.ts`'s automated branch) — a Manual-mode Traditional IRA sale or scenario will still show `$0` net tax after this task. Not touched: this task's self-audit and item 2 both explicitly scoped "the portfolio-level site" to the one named in `engine/index.ts`, and OrderConfirmation's automated path already inherits the real fix for free by reading `rec.est_net_tax` directly. (2) `FundSelectionManualLot.tsx`'s locally-recomputed live banner tax figure (~line 1037) still produces a literal `0` (not `N/A`) for the currently-expanded IRA fund's own display, since it never reads `fr.est_tax_gross` at all — a third duplicate of the same per-fund gate, outside item 1's named "two per-fund sites." Both are natural candidates for the follow-up banner-redesign task, not silently absorbed into this one.

**Baseline**: `tsc --noEmit` clean, Vitest 217/217 (209 prior + 8 new), Playwright 6/6.

### D084 — [Dev] Manual mode's IRA tax gap closed by consolidation, not duplication — one shared `computeNetTax()` now backs every portfolio-level tax site, six real call sites found and fixed, not the two D083 flagged

Self-audit (item 0) confirmed the premise exactly: `scenarioBuilder.ts`'s `buildScenarioFromFundResults()` had the identical two-way `taxable_brokerage ? computed : 0` gate `engine/index.ts` had before D083, never updated when Traditional IRA's ordinary-income tax was added. Answered the consolidation question directly: **yes, genuinely low-risk** — both code paths already operate on the same `FundSaleResult[]` shape (`est_st_gain_loss`/`est_lt_gain_loss`/`sell_amount`), so no adapter layer was needed. Extracted D083's three-way branch verbatim into a new exported `computeNetTax(fundResults, accountType, taxRates)` in `engine/index.ts` (placed there, not a new module, since it's the project's one designated calculation-engine home per CLAUDE.md §2) — `runOptimization()`'s automated branch now calls it instead of inlining the branch itself, so there is exactly one implementation of "how does this account type tax a withdrawal," not two kept in sync by hand.

**Consolidation surfaced far more duplication than D083's two named gaps, precisely because live-verifying (item 4) actually exercised the pages, not just the functions.** Six real call sites in total needed the fix, not two:
1. `scenarioBuilder.ts`'s `buildScenarioFromFundResults()` — the task's own named gap.
2. `OrderConfirmation.tsx`'s manual-mode branch (~line 120) — the task's other named gap.
3. **`FundSelectionManual2.tsx`'s own live Summary Banner** (~line 785) — found live: switched to Traditional IRA, sold $8,000 of VFITX, and the on-screen `EST. NET TAX` stayed `$0.00` despite D083 already having shipped, because this page had its own third hand-copied gate, never audited before this task.
4–6. **`FundSelectionManualLot.tsx` had four separate copies** of the same gate — one backing its real, rendered `combinedBanner.estNetTax` (the page's actual `EST. NET TAX` header figure, also confirmed live-broken the same way as #3 before the fix), and three backing `bannerData.estNetTax`/`effRate`, which turned out to be **dead fields never rendered anywhere in this component** (grepped every `bannerData.` reference to confirm) — fixed anyway rather than left as duplicated-and-wrong formulas waiting to be wired up later, per this file's own standing principle about surfacing rather than silently carrying technical debt forward.

A repo-wide grep for the literal formula shape (`const taxST` / `netGain = Math.max(0, ...)`) after all six fixes returned zero remaining matches — this is now confirmed to be the *only* implementation left, not "the ones we happened to find."

**Item 2 — `FundSelectionManualLot.tsx`'s per-fund `EST. TAX` banner (~line 1051, the one D083 explicitly named)**: given the not-applicable sentinel, `TaxFigureOrNA`. Considered reading from the shared `FundSaleResult.est_tax_gross` instead of recomputing locally, per the task's own suggestion — **rejected**: this banner's neighboring `EST. ST/LT GAINS` figures deliberately read live, currently-typed `bannerData` rather than the last-committed `manualConfig.fund_results`, specifically so the banner reflects what the user just selected before a recompute catches up; sourcing the tax figure from `manualConfig` instead would reintroduce exactly that staleness for one figure while its neighbors stayed live. Kept separate, sentinel logic applied directly, reasoning commented in place — matching the task's explicit fallback instruction for this case.

Also corrected, mid-task, a mistaken premise from D083 itself: that entry assumed `FundSelectionManualLot.tsx`'s `taxableAcct` variable was a fixed "the Taxable Brokerage account" lookup (making its `taxable_brokerage` gate a tautology / effectively dead code for IRA). Tracing it for real this time: `taxableAcct = portfolio?.accounts.find(a => a.account_id === activeAccountId)` — it's the **active** account, misleadingly named, not a fixed one. Every gate on this page was live-reachable for Traditional IRA all along; D083's characterization of this file's gap as lower-stakes than it turned out to be is superseded here, not silently corrected.

**Test coverage**: new `dev/src/utils/scenarioBuilder.test.ts` (8 tests, real `runOptimization()` + `buildScenarioFromFundResults()` calls, no reconstructed fixtures, same discipline as D083) — Traditional IRA single-fund ($8,000 → $1,920 = 24%), Traditional IRA multi-fund (VBTLX $5,000 + VFITX $6,000 → $2,640 on the real $11,000 total, the actual multi-fund-summation regression this fix targets), Roth IRA regression (still exactly $0), taxable brokerage regression ($105.78, byte-identical to the known real VTSAX $5,000 MinTax figure narration.test.ts already established).

### D085 — [PD/Dev] IRA banner redesign — Traditional IRA and Roth IRA content adapted across all six real touchpoints, display/layout only, no calculation changed

Self-audit (§0) confirmed the six touchpoints do NOT share one summary-banner component, as the task's own instruction warned might be true — three genuinely different structures exist: a 7-card grid (`FundSelectionAutomated.tsx`, `FundSelectionManual2.tsx`, `FundSelectionManualLot.tsx`'s `combinedBanner`, near-identical to each other but each with its own local state/JSX); a thin horizontal tax strip plus a per-scenario vertical `TaxRow` list (`ScenarioAnalysis.tsx` — the strip governs "new scenarios," the per-scenario block reads that scenario's *own* real account, which can differ from the page's active one, per D067); and a `TaxRow`-based "Estimated tax impact" block with a separate "Estimated proceeds" column (`OrderConfirmation.tsx`, post-transaction but pre-commit) and a near-identical but not-quite-matching `TaxRow` block plus a "Portfolio Impact" card (`ExecutionSummary.tsx`, genuinely post-transaction, its own structure exactly as the task predicted it might need).

**Single source of truth for the *decision*, not a shared component for the *layout*** — new `dev/src/utils/accountBanner.ts`, exporting pure functions each touchpoint calls with its own real `account_type`: `bannerRateDisplay()` (TAX BRACKET → ORDINARY INCOME RATE, dropping the LT split, or → TAX STATUS "Tax-free (qualified)" with no "Change" link for Roth), `bannerShowYtdRealized()` (false for either IRA type), `bannerShowSeparateGainLoss()` (false for either IRA type — governs whether ST/LT gain/loss render as two figures or consolidate into one), `CONSOLIDATED_GAIN_LOSS_LABEL`/`_TITLECASE` (ALL-CAPS card vs. Title-Case TaxRow variants), `bannerNetTaxLabel()`/`bannerRelabelForIra()` (Traditional IRA only — "EST. ORDINARY INCOME TAX" etc.; Roth deliberately keeps the generic "EST. NET TAX" label, per the task's own scope), `bannerShowEarlyWithdrawalPenalty()` (true for either IRA type) plus the shared N/A value and note text. Each of the six touchpoints kept its own exact JSX/CSS, branching content via these functions — matching this project's established pattern (narration, `computeNetTax()`) of one spec applied at many call sites rather than one shared component forced onto genuinely different layouts.

**Item 1/2 content, exactly as specified**: Traditional IRA gets ORDINARY INCOME RATE (st_rate only) with "Change" retained (the rate still matters); Roth gets TAX STATUS "Tax-free (qualified)" with no "Change" link (nothing to change). Both drop YTD REALIZED. Both consolidate ST/LT gain/loss into one "Est. gain/loss (no tax impact)" figure — for the 7-card banners this is `estSTGains + estLTGains`, a display-layer sum of two already-computed figures, not a new calculation; for `ScenarioAnalysis`/`OrderConfirmation`/`ExecutionSummary`'s TaxRow blocks it's the already-computed `net_taxable_gain`/`netTaxableGain` (literally the same sum, already stored) — **Rule 1** (CLAUDE.md §13, confirmed by reading the real register, not memory) governs its color in every location, verified live in both green (positive) and red (negative) cases with real screenshots. The existing net-tax figure is untouched in value, only relabeled for Traditional IRA (Roth keeps its label, since "ordinary income tax" describing an always-$0 figure would be a strange thing to call it — the task's own reasoning). New "Early Withdrawal Penalty" card/row added to both IRA types, value "N/A", noting the investor's age — this is a **display-layer constant**, not a new field threaded through `Recommendation`/`ManualConfiguration`/`SavedScenario`: the underlying fact (age 73, past 59½) is dataset-level and identical regardless of transaction, so a second data field would compute nothing this one constant doesn't already say correctly; `Recommendation.est_early_withdrawal_penalty` (D083) remains real engine-level groundwork for an actual future calculation, not superseded by this display text. **Rule 2's known gap (D080/D082, Impact badge) is untouched** — confirmed by inspection, no IMPACT rendering code was touched on any page.

**Item 3 (taxable regression) and item 4 (every touchpoint individually)**: confirmed, not assumed — each of the six files' `showSeparateGainLoss`/`showYtd`/label functions were traced and wired independently (no shared component to silently propagate a fix or a bug).

**Live-verified, 17 of 18 account-type × touchpoint combinations directly, with screenshots**: all 3 account types on `FundSelectionAutomated.tsx`, `FundSelectionManual2.tsx`, and `ScenarioAnalysis.tsx` (top strip and per-scenario column both checked, including Rule 1 green/red and Rule 3 gap-to-target red/black confirmed unchanged by direct screenshot); Traditional IRA and Roth IRA on `FundSelectionManualLot.tsx`, `OrderConfirmation.tsx`, and `ExecutionSummary.tsx` (submitted real orders for both, through to a real Execution Summary page, for both account types). The one cell not independently re-screenshotted — taxable brokerage on `ManualLot`/`OrderConfirmation`/`ExecutionSummary` specifically — relies on the same shared `accountBanner.ts` functions already confirmed correct for taxable in the other four touchpoints and confirmed correct for both IRA types on these exact three touchpoints; not a gap in the underlying logic, a scope call on live-verification exhaustiveness given real, repeated browser-automation flakiness encountered this session (intermittent click failures on this app's account-switch rows and mode toggle, root-caused mid-session to a coordinate/ref mismatch — `find`-returned refs use real DOM coordinates while screenshots are scaled, and mixing the two silently clicked the wrong element; `form_input` by ref and re-screenshotting before every coordinate click resolved it, but only after several wasted attempts).

**Baseline**: `tsc --noEmit` clean, Vitest 225/225 (unchanged — display/layout only, no new calculation logic to unit-test), Playwright 6/6.

### D086 — [PD/Dev] FundSelectionManual2.tsx's fund-row narration overlap — a real, pre-existing layout bug, found live and fixed

Reported live by the user with two side-by-side screenshots: Manual mode's fund rows showed the "AI-generated" badge and narration text overlapping the SELL AMOUNT field above and colliding with the next fund row / "Lot details" trigger below, cutting sentences off mid-word — the identical narration content rendered cleanly in Automated mode on the same page.

**Root cause, traced not assumed**: `ActiveFundRow`'s "Details Row" (`FundSelectionManual2.tsx`) was a fixed `h-8` (32px) container with `items-center`, sized back when it only ever held `taxData.rationale`'s short one-line deterministic fallback string. `NarrationBlock` (`components/NarrationBlock.tsx`) renders a badge `div` stacked above a `<p>` — inherently multi-line once real narration text wraps, which routinely exceeds 32px. With `items-center` on a fixed-height flex container, the oversized child centers on the 32px band and overflows symmetrically above and below it, visually colliding with sibling rows in normal document flow. `FundSelectionAutomated.tsx`'s equivalent narration container has never had a fixed height (a plain `flex items-center px-4 py-2` div, single child, grows freely) — which is exactly why the identical narration content never showed this problem there, confirming the two touchpoints' divergent history (Automated's container predates or was never touched by whatever set Manual2's height, most likely inherited unchanged from an early Figma-spec comment — "Main Row: 64px · Details Row: 32px · Total: 96px" — that was only ever accurate before live AI narration replaced the placeholder rationale text).

**Fix**: `min-h-[32px] items-start py-2` (was `h-8 items-center`) on the Details Row, and `items-start` (was `items-center`) on its inner rationale/badge/Wait-&-Save wrapper — the row now has a floor height matching the old short-text case but grows to fit real narration of any length, and top-aligns its children so a short "Lot details" trigger sits sensibly against the first line of a taller narration block rather than centering into its middle. `FundSelectionManualLot.tsx` was checked for the same pattern and confirmed unaffected — it never renders `NarrationBlock` at all (only the plain short rationale string, grepped to confirm zero references), so no equivalent fix was needed there.

**Live-verified**: reproduced the user's exact scenario (Manual mode, Taxable Brokerage, $15,000 VTSAX + $10,000 VBTLX) — both fund rows now render their narration fully, without overlapping the input field above, the next fund row, or the "Lot details" trigger below.

**Baseline**: `tsc --noEmit` clean, Vitest 225/225 (unchanged — pure layout fix, no logic touched), Playwright 6/6.

### D087 — [PD/Dev] IRA narration updates — states the real ordinary-income tax figure, omits the early-withdrawal penalty by default, and fixes a real gap the self-audit found along the way

Self-audit (§0) confirmed the premise: `narrationPrompt.ts`'s Traditional IRA instruction only ever stated the qualitative rule ("taxed as ordinary income regardless of this gain/loss figure"), never the dollar amount — a real limitation before D083 made `est_net_tax` an actual nonzero number for this account type, not something to fix until now. Tracing where `est_net_tax`/`est_early_withdrawal_penalty` would need to flow surfaced a real, separate, pre-existing gap: `FundSelectionManual2.tsx`'s per-fund narration call never passed `est_net_tax`/`effective_rate` at all (unlike `FundSelectionAutomated.tsx`, which always has) — so Manual mode's narration could never state an aggregate net tax figure for *any* account type, AI-generated or fallback, confirmed by comparing real generated text side by side: Automated mode's output ends with "Estimated net tax across this transaction: $X.XX."; Manual mode's the same session simply stopped after the market-context sentence. Fixed as part of this task, not deferred — without it, item 1's actual goal (state the real number) would silently fail to work in Manual mode specifically, the same "found a needed fix while implementing the actual ask" pattern as D084's `computeNetTax()` consolidation.

**Item 1**: `narrationPrompt.ts`'s Traditional IRA instruction now requires stating `est_net_tax`'s real dollar figure alongside the qualitative rule, not instead of it — example phrasing added: "...taxed as ordinary income, an estimated $2,400.00 in this case." `FundSelectionManual2.tsx` now computes the same real aggregate `computeNetTax()` figure at component scope (previously only computed locally inside the Summary Banner's own IIFE, invisible to the narration call site) and passes it through — Automated mode already had this wired via `rec?.est_net_tax`, now also passing `rec?.est_early_withdrawal_penalty` for item 2.

**Item 2 — the omit-vs-state design decision, made explicitly, not defaulted into**: the early-withdrawal-penalty figure is omitted from all narration (AI-generated and deterministic fallback alike) unless it is ever a real, nonzero value — today, given the fixed retirement-age profile (`note_retirement_status`), it never is. Implemented as a coercion at the `narrationBuilders.ts` boundary, `meaningfulPenalty()`: the `'not_applicable'` sentinel AND a genuine `$0` both collapse to `undefined` before the figure ever reaches `NarrationInput` — the field is never passed through and then instructed away; it's simply absent. New prompt instruction makes the omission explicit and defends against the model filling the silence on its own initiative: "If it is absent, say NOTHING about penalties, retirement age, or whether one applies — do not add a reassuring aside... to fill the silence." Deliberately **not** threaded through `ManualConfiguration`/`SavedScenario`/`TransactionRecord` — only `Recommendation` carries the real field (D083/D085's own scope decision) — so Manual/Scenario/Execution-sourced narration never has the field to omit in the first place, producing the identical correct behavior without adding plumbing three more result types don't otherwise need. The chosen alternative (state it every time as a fixed disclaimer) was rejected for the reason the task itself gave: it would clutter every single narration today for a fact that's true 100% of the time in this dataset, and the omit-by-default design means the day this assumption changes (a real, computed, nonzero penalty), narration starts surfacing it automatically — no flag to remember to flip.

**Item 3 — two new categories added to CLAUDE.md §12's narration list** (14 total, not 12): **13. Traditional IRA with a real computed ordinary income tax figure** (real `automatedRun(TRAD_IRA, 10000)` fixture — $10,000 × 24% = $2,400 — confirms the fallback states both the qualitative rule and the real figure, and that the prompt instructs the model to do the same) and **14. Traditional IRA narration correctly omits the early withdrawal penalty when not applicable** (confirms the real engine genuinely emits the `'not_applicable'` sentinel, that it's coerced to `undefined` rather than passed through, that a genuine `$0` is *also* omitted — only a real nonzero figure is ever stated — that a hypothetical real nonzero figure does flow through and get stated, and a self-audit guard proving the sentinel never leaks as literal text and never produces "NaN"/"undefined" in output). 11 new tests total, all against real engine output, no hand-typed fixtures.

**Item 4 — live-verified with a genuine model call, not just the deterministic fallback**: attempting this through the app's own UI hit the same `/api/narrate` 502 this session had already seen during D085/D086's verification — but this time traced further rather than accepted as a repeat of the "no outbound internet access" finding from D067/D073/D078: a direct `fetch` from the Bash tool to Gemini's API succeeded (`GET /v1beta/models` → 200), proving that finding was specific to the **Browser pane's** network sandbox, not this session generally. Built the real `NarrationInput` from a real `automatedRun(TRAD_IRA, 10000)` call, built the real prompt via `buildNarrationPrompt()`, and called Gemini's real `/v1beta/interactions` endpoint directly (same request shape `geminiGenerator.ts` builds — importing the adapter module itself hit an unrelated circular-import ordering issue specific to running it standalone via `tsx`, not a production bug, so the request was replicated by hand instead) — a genuine, one-off live call, temporary script deleted immediately after, per this project's established pattern for narration review samples. Real output, read cold: *"Selling $10,000.00 of Vanguard Intermediate-Term Treasury Index Fund Admiral Shares using MinTax would result in a shift in portfolio allocation of -0.78%, realizing a long-term loss of -$322.58 in an account where the withdrawal amount is taxed as ordinary income, an estimated $2,400.00 in this case, while the fund has returned 5.48% over the trailing 12 months."* Checked against every item-4 requirement: the real dollar figure appears correctly ($2,400.00, matching the fixture exactly); the penalty is genuinely absent — no "penalty," "N/A," "not applicable," "age," or "retirement" anywhere; **one honest caveat, not glossed over**: the sentence is a single dense run-on cramming allocation-impact, loss, ordinary-income framing, the tax figure, and market context together — technically compliant with segment A's "one sentence for a single fund" rule and every content instruction, but denser than the segment-A example sentences in the prompt itself, which are shorter and use semicolons to separate clauses rather than one long relative-clause chain. Not a correctness defect and not a scope item for this task (segment-A phrasing quality generally, not IRA-specific), but worth a future look if segment-A output density becomes its own concern.

**Environment finding worth remembering**: outbound network access is available from this session's Bash tool but not from the Browser pane's own sandbox (confirmed by the `/api/narrate` 502 vs. the direct Bash `fetch` 200 to the identical Gemini endpoint) — the earlier "no outbound internet access" conclusion (D067/D073/D078) was accurate for what those sessions actually tried (the Browser pane / the app's own proxy) but should not be generalized to "this environment has no outbound access at all" without checking which surface is being asked.

**Baseline**: `tsc --noEmit` clean, Vitest 236/236 (225 prior + 11 new), Playwright 6/6.

### D088 — [PD/Dev] Provider-failure indicator in Demo Settings — self-audited 429-is-the-only-reliable-signal, per-provider not per-feature, own persistence key that survives Reset demo

**Self-audit (§0), done before any code was written**: is a genuine quota/rate-limit failure actually distinguishable from a bad key, a network issue, or a malformed request? Traced all six provider adapter files (`geminiGenerator/Interpreter.ts`, `anthropicGenerator/Interpreter.ts`, `groqGenerator/Interpreter.ts`) — every one follows the identical `if (!res.ok) throw new XApiError(...)` pattern against a real HTTP response. A genuine 429 is real, already-observed evidence in this project specifically (D065's live Groq 429 with real `x-ratelimit-*` headers; D066's live Gemini 429) — not a hopeful assumption. It's cleanly distinguishable from the other three failure modes the task named: a bad key returns its own distinct status (401/403), a malformed request returns 400, and a network failure never produces an HTTP response at all (no status to check). Conclusion: 429 is safe to treat as "likely a usage limit"; anything else must not make that claim, per the task's own explicit "if a failure isn't clearly a limit, don't guess" instruction. This governs the whole design below.

**Architecture**: `dev/api/narrate.ts` and `dev/api/interpret.ts` were both restructured to resolve the active generator/interpreter in their own try/catch (returning an early 502 on an unrecognized provider string, rather than crashing uncaught — a real regression I introduced in an early draft and caught in the same turn, see below), then wrap the actual `generate()`/`interpret()` call in a second try/catch that now includes `providerName`/`providerStatus` in the JSON body on both success (200) and failure (502). This is the only way the client can learn *what actually happened* server-side, since both routes already always returned a flat 502 regardless of cause — without these fields there'd be nothing to build an indicator from. `NarrationApiError`/`WhatIfInterpreterApiError` (`narrationGenerator.ts`/`whatIfInterpreter.ts`) both gained a `status?: number` field, set by every adapter at its one `throw` site. These fields are internal-use-only wire fields, never rendered as raw text to an end user anywhere — the Demo Settings dialog showing a provider's own name is a pre-existing, deliberate design choice (D071) distinct from the user-facing narration/chat surfaces D066's "never surface a raw API error, status code, or provider name" rule protects; that rule is unchanged and unaffected by this work.

**Item 1 — shared per-provider, not per-feature**: `dev/src/utils/providerFailureTracker.ts` (new file) keys its state purely by provider name string (`gemini`/`anthropic`/`groq`), with no per-feature dimension at all. Both `narration.ts` and `whatIf.ts` call the exact same `recordProviderFailure()`/`recordProviderSuccess()` functions against the exact same `localStorage` key. Chosen because narration and the Scenario assistant genuinely share the same underlying provider quota when pointed at the same provider (a very likely demo configuration, and in fact this environment's actual current state — both `NARRATION_PROVIDER` and `WHATIF_PROVIDER` are set to `groq`) — a failure on one is real evidence about the other, not an independent fact.

**Item 2 — Demo Settings display, "don't guess" honored**: `ProviderFailureNote` (new component in `DemoSettingsDialog.tsx`) renders beneath each of the two provider rows (Narration of summaries / Scenario assistant), reading `getProviderFailureDisplay(effectiveProvider)` — `null` renders nothing. A confirmed-429 failure reads `"Gemini — last failed 7:46 AM, likely a usage limit"`; any other real failure (bad key, network failure, anything non-429) reads the generic `"Groq — last failed 7:46 AM"` with no claim about cause, per the self-audit's conclusion above.

**Item 3 — clears on success or day-rollover, success wins**: `recordProviderSuccess()` deletes the record outright the moment any real call to that provider succeeds — stronger, more direct evidence the limit has lifted than time alone, so it's checked and applied before the day-rollover fallback would ever matter. `getProviderFailureDisplay()`'s fallback compares `Date.toDateString()` (calendar-date equality), deliberately not a rolling 24-hours-since-failure window — a free-tier quota limit resetting daily means an 11:58pm failure should read stale by 12:01am, not 24 real hours later; this was a deliberate implementation choice, not the more obvious rolling-window default.

**Item 4 — persistence location, decided explicitly, not defaulted**: state lives in its own dedicated `localStorage` key, `vsr_provider_failures`, kept deliberately separate from `demoSettings.ts`'s `vsr_demo_settings` key, and is deliberately **not** cleared by "Reset demo." Reasoning: this is operational/observed-fact state (what a real API call actually did, and when), not a user preference like the segment or provider selection `demoSettings` holds — commingling the two would conflate "what the user wants" with "what the environment just told us," and a demo reset (which restores fictional portfolio data) has zero bearing on a real provider's real quota state. Clearing this record on reset would actively cause the same confusion to resurface the next time that provider genuinely failed, with no memory of the pattern. This reasoning is also written into the file's own header comment, so it isn't reasoning that exists only here.

**Bug found and fixed while restructuring `dev/api/narrate.ts`/`interpret.ts` (self-caught, no live impact)**: an early draft moved `getActiveGenerator(provider)`'s resolution outside any try/catch, which meant an unrecognized `provider` string in the request body would throw uncaught and crash the handler with a raw 500, instead of the pre-existing, correct behavior of returning a clean 502 JSON error. Caught and fixed in the same turn by wrapping generator/interpreter resolution in its own try/catch with an early 502 return, before ever reaching the second try/catch around the real `generate()`/`interpret()` call.

**Live verification — a real failure, not a simulated one**: corrupted `dev/.env.local`'s `GROQ_API_KEY`, restarted the dev server (required — `vite.config.ts`'s `loadEnv()` only loads env vars once at server startup), and triggered a real recommendation-generation flow through the Browser pane. `read_network_requests` confirmed `/api/narrate` returned `502` with body `{"error":"Groq API request failed: fetch failed","providerName":"groq"}` — a genuine network-level failure (the `fetch` itself threw; no HTTP response, hence no status, ever came back), because the Browser pane's own sandbox cannot reach the internet in this session at all (same environment characteristic D067/D073/D078 found, and the same one D087 clarified is specific to the Browser pane surface, not this session's Bash tool). This is arguably a *better* real test than the task's suggested "corrupted key" technique, since it live-exercises the no-status-at-all path, not just a non-429 HTTP status. Opened Demo Settings and screenshotted both provider rows (both pointed at Groq) showing the identical note `"Groq — last failed 7:46 AM"` — correctly generic, no "likely a usage limit" claim, since no real HTTP status was ever returned to check against 429.

Restored the real `GROQ_API_KEY`. Since the Browser pane can't complete a real network round-trip regardless of key validity, the success-clears-immediately path was verified by using `javascript_tool` to dynamically `import()` the real bundled `providerFailureTracker.ts` module directly in the live page's JS context and calling the actual production `recordProviderSuccess('groq')` — confirmed via the module's own `getProviderFailureDisplay('groq')`: `{"before":"Groq — last failed 7:46 AM","after":null,"rawStorage":"{}"}`. Reopened Demo Settings and confirmed via screenshot that both rows now show no failure note. Confirmed via `preview_list` that only one clean dev-server process remained running afterward (no orphaned duplicates from the restart).

**Tests**: `dev/src/utils/providerFailureTracker.test.ts` (new, 12 tests) — 429-produces-quota-wording, non-429-produces-generic-wording, no-status-produces-generic-wording, providers tracked independently, success clears its own provider only, success on a never-failed provider is a safe no-op, same-calendar-day failure still shows, previous-calendar-day failure is stale even if <24 real hours passed, several-days-stale, and corrupted-localStorage-JSON never throws and degrades to "nothing recorded." Same in-memory `localStorage` mock pattern as `demoSettings.test.ts`.

**Baseline**: `tsc --noEmit` clean, Vitest 248/248 (236 prior + 12 new), Playwright 6/6.

### D089 — [PD/Dev] Provider-failure indicator display gains a date; the day-rollover invariant it relies on was checked, not assumed, and one narrow real gap was found and documented rather than silently patched

**The display bug**: `getProviderFailureDisplay()` (`providerFailureTracker.ts`) rendered only a time — `"Groq — last failed 8:05 AM"` — with no date. Fixed to include one: `failedAt.toLocaleDateString('en-US', { month: 'short', day: 'numeric' })` prepended to the existing time string, for both the 429 and generic wording branches — `"Groq — last failed Sep 15, 8:05 AM"` / `"...Sep 15, 8:05 AM, likely a usage limit"`. Live-verified against a real recorded failure in the running dev server (via direct module invocation, then confirmed on-screen in the actual Demo Settings dialog, screenshotted showing both provider rows correctly reading `"Groq — last failed Sep 2, 8:29 AM, likely a usage limit"`), then cleaned up immediately with a real `recordProviderSuccess()` call.

**The invariant check (the task's real ask, not just the display fix)**: is the day-rollover clearing logic actually guaranteed to run before a stale message could ever render on a later day? Traced every real path that can produce this text, not assumed from the design intent:
- `getProviderFailureDisplay()` is not a "clear" that runs on a timer or writes anything to storage on rollover — it's a pure read-time staleness check, comparing `failedAt.toDateString()` against `new Date().toDateString()` fresh, every single time it's called. There is no separate "clearing" step for the day-rollover case to race against; the record itself stays in `localStorage` untouched (only a real success deletes it) — staleness is entirely a property of *when this function is called*, not of stored state.
- **Fresh page load**: a brand-new render tree, calls the function fresh against real current time. Safe by construction.
- **Dialog reopen**: `L2Nav.tsx` renders `DemoSettingsDialog` conditionally — `{settingsOpen && <DemoSettingsDialog .../>}` — so React fully unmounts it on close and mounts a genuinely new component instance on reopen, which calls `getProviderFailureDisplay()` fresh against real current time on that new mount. Confirmed by reading `L2Nav.tsx` directly, not assumed from the component's name. Safe by construction.
- **Grepped the whole codebase for every place this display function or its underlying record is ever read**: exactly one call site exists, `ProviderFailureNote` inside `DemoSettingsDialog.tsx` — no other surface renders this text.
- **The one real gap found**: if a `DemoSettingsDialog` instance stays mounted continuously — opened and simply left open, untouched — across a real calendar-day boundary, the previously-rendered stale text stays on screen. React does not re-invoke a mounted function component just because wall-clock time passed; nothing in this component polls or subscribes to time, and its only `useEffect` (fetching server provider defaults) runs once on mount with an empty dependency array. So a message computed correctly at, say, 11:58pm would not go stale on screen until *some other* state change inside the dialog (changing the segment, changing a provider, opening the reset-confirmation step) happens to trigger a re-render after midnight.

**Not fixed — reported and documented, per the task's own instruction and this project's standing practice of surfacing an unresolved tradeoff rather than resolving it silently in whichever direction is easiest.** Judged not worth adding polling/interval infrastructure for: this is a demo settings modal a user opens, glances at, and closes — leaving it open and untouched, unattended, specifically across a midnight boundary is a narrow, low-consequence edge case, and the fix (a `setInterval`-driven re-render, or a `visibilitychange`/focus-triggered refresh) would add real complexity for a case this project's own demo-tool context makes unlikely to matter. Added to CLAUDE.md's implementation note and to `docs/progress.html`'s "What's not built yet" list, so it's visible rather than quietly known only here.

**Baseline**: `tsc --noEmit` clean, Vitest 249/249 (248 prior + 1 new — the date-format regression test), Playwright 6/6.

### D090 — [PM/PD] Two documentation additions to docs/progress.html: an AI-approaches landscape (table + spectrum diagram) and a "Design lessons" section distinct from engineering highlights

Both additions restate this project's own stated primary purpose (§1: learning to design AI-*enabled* applications, not just build two features) directly on the human-facing status page, rather than leaving that framing implicit in `CLAUDE.md` alone.

**AI approaches landscape**: a 13-row reference table (data-to-text/NLG, function calling, slot filling, HITL, RAG, classification/routing, recommendation systems, anomaly/fraud detection, multi-step autonomous agents, multi-agent systems, fine-tuned models, cross-session memory, predictive ML) plus a horizontal spectrum diagram, both added as a new section directly after "Feature status." Three status categories, color-coded consistently between the table and the diagram (green dot = built, amber = considered and explicitly rejected, grey = not needed for this project's scope): five approaches are genuinely built here (NLG, classification/routing, function calling, slot filling, HITL); three were actively considered and rejected on the record elsewhere in this project (anomaly/fraud detection — the wash-sale-flagging candidate; multi-step autonomous agents; predictive/forecasting ML); the remaining five were simply never in scope (RAG, recommendation systems, multi-agent systems, fine-tuned models, cross-session memory).

**Diagram ordering — a deliberate design choice, stated honestly as one**: the task asked for approaches ordered "roughly by sophistication/autonomy," which is a genuinely fuzzy axis (fine-tuning, say, is more a training-technique dimension than an autonomy one). Rather than force a single rigorous ranking, the five *built* approaches were grouped contiguously at the low-autonomy end inside a visually distinct dashed "This project's actual footprint" band, with the remaining eight ordered afterward in a rough autonomy/consequence progression ending at multi-step autonomous agents — prioritizing the story the task actually asked for ("here's the full space, here's what was deliberately chosen") over strict axis precision. A caption under the diagram says plainly that the ordering is approximate, not rigorous — avoiding the failure mode of a confident-looking diagram implying more precision than the underlying judgment call actually has. The diagram also explicitly cross-references the roadmap's item 5 (illustrative-only "autonomous execution" idea) as the concrete line this project's footprint stays on the correct side of — tying the landscape section to content already on the page rather than existing in isolation.

**"Design lessons" section — deliberately separate from "Highlights worth knowing at a glance"**: the existing highlights section is engineering-focused (bugs found, code-level guarantees built). The new section holds the eight lessons given, each rewritten in this page's own voice rather than copied verbatim, and each checked against what it's paired with in the existing page to avoid restating an engineering bug as if it were the lesson itself — e.g. the "confidently-reasoned hypothesis needs verification" lesson names the Impact-badge coloring assumption (D080) as its example but frames the lesson as "check reasoning about existing code before building on it," not as a restatement of the badge bug. Visually distinguished with a blue left border (`--blue`, a new CSS variable) against the engineering highlights' maroon, so the two sections read as related-but-different at a glance, not as one undifferentiated list. Placed directly after the engineering highlights section, before "What's not built yet."

**Regeneration discipline**: per the standing rule, the whole page was rewritten fresh from current `CLAUDE.md`/`DECISIONS.md` content (including this session's own D088/D089 additions), not hand-patched — the Vitest count, the provider-failure-indicator highlight text, and the new "What's not built yet" entry for D089's narrow gap all reflect this session's actual latest state, not the prior checkpoint's.

**Baseline**: `tsc --noEmit` clean, Vitest 249/249, Playwright 6/6 (no source code touched by this entry beyond the docs page itself — same baseline as D089's).

### D091 — [PM/PD] "What's not built yet" categorized by real next-step, not a generic status label — verified against each item's actual documented status, not guessed

Added a 6-category tag (Ready to build / Needs a decision first / Known, accepted limitation / Roadmap idea / Requires manual action / Needs user verification) to each of the six items in `docs/progress.html`'s "What's not built yet" list, plus a compact legend explaining what each category means. Each tag was assigned only after re-checking the item's real status in `CLAUDE.md`/`DECISIONS.md` — not assigned generically off the item's title alone, per the task's explicit instruction.
