**Result — heavily skewed toward "Known, accepted limitation" (4 of 6), and that skew is itself the honest finding, not a sign the categorization wasn't done carefully**: this project's own standing practice (§0's "surface an unresolved tradeoff explicitly... rather than resolving it silently") means most open items in this list were already deliberately closed out as accepted tradeoffs at the time they were written, not left open as unscheduled work. Specific reasoning per item:
- **Segment C/D live verification** → **Needs user verification**. The clearest, most literal match — CLAUDE.md §6 states plainly the gap is a live model call the Browser pane's sandbox can't make (no outbound internet), which is exactly this category's own description ("usually networking-related").
- **Provider-failure indicator's midnight gap (D089)** → **Known, accepted limitation**. D089's own text says explicitly "judged not worth adding polling infrastructure for" — an accepted tradeoff, not a queued task.
- **What-if residual test gaps** (Roth non-SpecID, HIFO alone) → **Known, accepted limitation**, not "Ready to build," even though writing the two missing test cases is genuinely mechanical with no design question attached. CLAUDE.md's own heading calls these "residual gaps, documented rather than chased further" — an explicit stopping point, not a backlog item; tagging it "Ready to build" would have misrepresented a deliberate decision to stop as an oversight waiting on a prompt.
- **Allocation-impact indicator wording mismatch** → **Known, accepted limitation**. CLAUDE.md §11 calls it "flagged for whoever eventually touches that indicator" — no current plan, pre-existing app behavior outside either AI feature's scope.
- **No dedicated "how was this calculated?" control** → **Needs a decision first**, not "Known, accepted limitation" or "Roadmap idea." Unlike the other gaps, this one has a real open design question attached — CLAUDE.md notes the existing always-visible figures "substantively serve the same intent" the named control would, so building it first requires deciding whether a literal control is even warranted, and if so what it would show that isn't already visible. Not itemized in the roadmap table, so "Roadmap idea" (which explicitly points to that table) would have been a false cross-reference.
- **CD-6 feedback loops** → **Known, accepted limitation**, not "Roadmap idea," for the same false-cross-reference reason — the roadmap table has no feedback-loop row. CLAUDE.md states this project "has no backend/analytics layer at all," a settled scope boundary from §1, not a live-but-unscheduled idea.

No item was tagged "Ready to build" or "Requires manual action" — neither category has a genuine match in the current list; forcing one into either would have been the generic guessing the task explicitly warned against.

**Baseline**: `tsc --noEmit` clean, Vitest 249/249, Playwright 6/6 (docs-only change).

### D092 — [PM] Design Lessons replaced verbatim with the eight concrete versions given — the prior entries were real but too abstracted from the events they described to be traceable

The eight `docs/progress.html` "Design lessons" cards were replaced with the exact text supplied, unparaphrased, per the task's explicit instruction. The previous versions (written in this session's own D090) were not fabricated, but had been generalized far enough from the real underlying events (specific dollar amounts, specific features, specific research documents) that a reader couldn't trace a lesson back to what actually happened — e.g. the old "a passing test can still fail to prove what it claims" gave no indication this was about the Scenario assistant's multi-lot selection feature, an $8,000 test case, or a $30,000 case that actually forced the real proof. The new versions name the actual mechanism (multi-lot selection), actual dollar figures ($8,000 / $30,000), actual research source (SEC guidance, this project's own user-research documentation), and the actual feature the lesson was caught while building (the IRA summary banner design, for the Impact-badge lesson) — all specific enough to cross-reference against `DECISIONS.md`'s own entries.

**Presentation choice**: each card kept the existing bordered `.highlight.design-lesson` visual treatment (blue left border, from D090) but the `.h-title` line was changed to a neutral "Lesson 1" through "Lesson 8" instead of an editorial summary phrase. Deliberate: inventing a new descriptive title risked reintroducing exactly the paraphrasing-drift problem this replacement exists to fix, since a title is itself a compressed (and therefore lossy) restatement of the body. Numbering preserves the task's own ordering and gives the section scannability without adding any interpretive text beyond what was supplied verbatim.

**Baseline**: `tsc --noEmit` clean, Vitest 249/249, Playwright 6/6 (docs-only change).

### D093 — [Dev] "Clear all coach marks" added to Demo Settings — a storage-level sentinel, not an ID list, so it covers every coach mark that exists now or gets added later without maintenance; Reset demo's own behavior is untouched

**The real design problem, found before writing any code**: coach marks (`CoachMark.tsx`) are scattered across many pages (`FundSelectionEntry.tsx`, `FundSelectionAutomated.tsx`, `FundSelectionManual2.tsx`, `FundSelectionManualLot.tsx`, `ExecutionSummary.tsx`, plus two always-mounted ones in `L2Nav.tsx` — `nav-prototype` and the recently-added `demo-settings`, D071) — and only whichever page is the current route has its coach marks actually mounted in React at any moment (`PortalShell.tsx` swaps page content via `<Outlet/>`; `L2Nav`'s two are the only ones always present). A "clear all" that snapshotted currently-mounted coach mark IDs at click time could only ever dismiss the 2–5 marks on whatever page the user happened to be viewing when they clicked — not the marks on every other page, which is what "clear all" has to mean. A hardcoded ID list avoids that specific problem but creates a maintenance trap the task explicitly flagged: every future `<CoachMark id="...">` added anywhere would need a matching update to that list, silently going stale otherwise.

**The fix**: `vsr_coach_marks_dismissed` (the existing per-ID dismissed-array localStorage key, unchanged in format for individual dismissals) now also accepts a single sentinel string, `'ALL'`, written by a new exported `clearAllCoachMarks()` (`CoachMark.tsx`). `isDismissed(id)` (renamed from the former `getDismissed().includes(id)` call pattern) checks for the sentinel before ever attempting `JSON.parse` on the stored value, returning `true` unconditionally when it's present — which means every coach mark, on every page, present today or added in a future code change, reads as dismissed the moment it next mounts, with zero ID enumeration anywhere in the clear-all path. `clearAllCoachMarks()` reuses the existing `'vsr-reset'` window event (already dispatched by the full Reset-demo flow and already listened to by every mounted `CoachMark` to re-check its own dismissed state) rather than inventing a second event — the semantics ("something external changed dismissed-state, recheck") are identical for both callers.

**Reset demo's behavior is provably unchanged**: its flow (`FundSelectionEntry.tsx`'s `/?reset=true` handler) still does `localStorage.removeItem('vsr_coach_marks_dismissed')` unconditionally — removing the key entirely regardless of whether it held a per-ID array or the new sentinel string, which is exactly what it already did before this task. No line in that handler was touched. `clearAllCoachMarks()` only ever *sets* the key, never removes it, so the two actions can't interfere with each other in either direction.

**One honest scope boundary, not silently glossed over**: the sentinel persists in `localStorage` until either an individual dismiss overwrites it (which can't happen while it's active — see below) or Reset demo removes it. This means a coach mark added to the codebase *after* a user has clicked "Clear all" would also read as pre-dismissed the first time it ever mounts, even though that specific user has never actually seen it. Judged an acceptable tradeoff for a demo application (not a real multi-user product with a real onboarding-completeness requirement), and it's the direct, necessary consequence of satisfying "covers coach marks added later" without any ID list to selectively exclude a not-yet-existing one from a past clear-all click — there is no version of an ID-agnostic mechanism that avoids this specific edge case.

**`addDismissed()` never needs to handle the sentinel case specially, by construction**: a `CoachMark` whose `dismissed` state is `true` renders `null` before the "Got it" button ever exists in the DOM (`if (dismissed) return null`), so `addDismissed()` — which only runs from that button's click handler — can never fire while the sentinel is active. Confirmed by tracing the component's render logic, not assumed.

**Live-verified end to end, exactly as the task's validation step asked**: with the dev server running, cleared `vsr_coach_marks_dismissed` to a known baseline, then confirmed 3 real coach-mark beacons on the entry page (`nav-prototype`, `demo-settings`, `mode-toggle`) via `find`. Opened Demo Settings, clicked "Clear all coach marks," confirmed the new "Cleared" inline label appeared and `localStorage` held exactly `"ALL"`. Confirmed all 3 beacons vanished from the entry page. **Navigated to `FundSelectionAutomated.tsx` — a page never mounted this session, carrying 5 of its own coach marks (`mode-toggle`, `opt-priority`, `tax`, `ytd`, `scenario-analysis`) — and confirmed zero beacons rendered there either**, the direct proof that the sentinel mechanism covers pages beyond whichever one was active at click time, not just a snapshot of what happened to be on screen. Then opened Demo Settings again and triggered the full Reset demo flow (two-step confirm, "Reset all demo data? This can't be undone." → confirm) — confirmed `localStorage.getItem('vsr_coach_marks_dismissed')` returned `null` (key fully removed, not merely cleared to an empty array) and all 3 entry-page beacons were visible again, matching the original pre-test baseline exactly.

**New test coverage**: `dev/src/components/CoachMark.test.ts` (new file, 4 tests) — `clearAllCoachMarks()` stores the sentinel rather than a per-ID array; it dispatches `'vsr-reset'` exactly once; it overwrites (not merges with) a prior per-ID dismissed array; it never removes the key (only Reset demo's own flow does that). This project's Vitest environment is `node`, not `jsdom` (`vite.config.ts`) — no real `window` exists there, so the test stubs one with Node's built-in `EventTarget`, sufficient for dispatch/listener assertions without pulling in a full DOM environment for one module.

### D094 — [PD] AI Approaches Landscape diagram restacked vertically — the horizontal-scroll layout required a second axis of navigation for no real benefit on a page that already scrolls vertically

The spectrum diagram (`docs/progress.html`, D090) previously laid its 13 approach nodes out left-to-right inside a `min-width: 1180px` flex row, requiring a horizontal scrollbar independent of the page's own vertical scroll — a genuine usability issue on a document meant to be read top-to-bottom like the rest of the page. Restacked to a single vertical column: `.spectrum-track` is now `flex-direction: column`, each node is a full-width row (dot + label + status side by side, not stacked), and the "This project's actual footprint" dashed band wraps its five built nodes as a vertical sub-stack rather than a horizontal one. The `spectrum-axis-labels` two-line top/bottom pair became two single labels, `↑ Lower autonomy / narrower scope` above the track and `↓ Higher autonomy / broader scope` below it — arrows added since the axis direction is no longer conveyed by left/right positioning. Same three status colors (green/amber/grey), same footprint band, same legend and caveat note — this was a layout change, not a content or data change; the low-to-high autonomy ordering established in D090 is unchanged, just read top-to-bottom instead of left-to-right. `overflow-x: auto`/`min-width` were removed from `.spectrum-outer`/`.spectrum-track` entirely, since nothing in the new layout can overflow horizontally at any real viewport width.

**Live-verified**: opened the regenerated page, scrolled to the section using the page's normal vertical scroll only (no horizontal scroll interaction at any point), and confirmed all 13 nodes, the footprint band, both axis labels, the legend, and the note render correctly in sequence.

### D095 — [PD/Dev] Account type now stated on Order Confirmation and Execution Summary — both screens had the same hardcoded-"Brokerage" bug, not a missing label on one of them

**Checked both screens, as the task asked, rather than assuming the fix from one applied to the other — findings genuinely differed.** `OrderConfirmation.tsx`'s Section 1 ("ORDER SUMMARY") already correctly displayed the real account type (`data.accountName`, from `accountTypeLabel(acct?.account_type)`) alongside the masked number — that part was never broken. But Section 4's DESTINATION field hardcoded the literal string `"Brokerage {data.accountMasked}"` regardless of the real account type — so a Traditional IRA or Roth IRA transaction showed "Traditional IRA ...2973" in one section and "Brokerage ...2973" in another section of the *same page*, an internal contradiction that actively undermines the correct information sitting right above it. `ExecutionSummary.tsx` was worse: it had **no account-type display anywhere on the page at all** — the only account mention was its own success-banner settlement line, which carried the identical hardcoded `"Brokerage {masked}"` bug, so a real IRA transaction's receipt never stated its own account type once, correctly or incorrectly.

**Fix**: `OrderConfirmation.tsx`'s DESTINATION field now reads `{data.accountName} {data.accountMasked}`, reusing the exact value Section 1 already computes correctly — a one-line fix, since the correct data was already present in scope. `ExecutionSummary.tsx` gained a new `accountName` constant (`accountTypeLabel(acct?.account_type)`, `acct` already resolved correctly per D057) used in two places: the success banner's settlement line (`"Proceeds to {accountName} {masked} settlement fund"`, replacing the hardcoded word), and a new addition to the "Transaction summary" card's header — previously just the text "Transaction summary" with nothing else, now also showing `{accountName} {masked}` on the same row, mirroring `OrderConfirmation.tsx`'s Section 1 pattern so this page states its account context at least once the way the other one always did.

**Why this matters more than a typical missing-label bug (per the task's own framing, confirmed true)**: the IRA ordinary-income tax work (D083–D087) exists specifically to make account-type treatment visible and correct — different tax rules, different labels, different figures depending on account type. A transaction receipt that either omits the account entirely or, worse, actively states the wrong one ("Brokerage" on an IRA) undermines that work's whole purpose at the exact moment — a transaction receipt — where getting it right matters most.

**Live-verified end to end on a real Traditional IRA transaction**: switched the active account to Traditional IRA on `FundSelectionAutomated.tsx`, sold $4,000 of VFITX, and confirmed on `OrderConfirmation.tsx` that ORDER SUMMARY and DESTINATION both now read "Traditional IRA ...2973" (previously DESTINATION alone would have read "Brokerage ...2973"). Submitted the order and confirmed on `ExecutionSummary.tsx` that the success banner reads "...Proceeds to Traditional IRA ...2973 settlement fund" and the Transaction summary card header reads "Traditional IRA ...2973" — both real account-type mentions this page never had before. Demo state reset afterward via Demo Settings.

**Baseline**: `tsc --noEmit` clean, Vitest 253/253 (no test changes needed — this is a display-layer fix with no new logic to unit test; correctness verified live against a real committed transaction), Playwright 6/6.

### D096 — [PD] Sell-amount helper text's orphaned-word wrap — an arbitrary `max-w-[1111px]` on the paragraph itself, not a container constraint

**Checked the actual container/max-width rather than guessing, per the task's instruction.** Measured live in the browser (`getBoundingClientRect()`/`getComputedStyle()` on the real rendered paragraph): the parent container (`.px-8`) is a full 1440px wide — the app's fixed-width layout gives this paragraph roughly 1376px of real available space — but the paragraph itself carried `max-w-[1111px]`, an arbitrary cap well inside that available space, not a symptom of the container being too narrow. At that 1111px width, the sentence ("Enter the amount you'd like to raise from your portfolio. We'll generate a fund sell recommendation that minimizes your tax impact while keeping your portfolio on target.") wrapped with the single word "target." stranded alone on its own second line — confirmed visually before touching any code.

Grepped for the same `text-[14px] text-vg-ink-muted leading-normal` style used elsewhere in the codebase (`FundSelectionManual2.tsx`'s dialog copy) and found it carries no `max-w` constraint at all — confirming `FundSelectionEntry.tsx`'s `max-w-[1111px]` was a one-off outlier, not an established convention worth preserving. Fixed by deleting the class entirely, letting the paragraph use its natural container width like its sibling does. Live-verified: the full sentence now renders without the orphaned line break at the app's real 1440px width.

**Baseline**: `tsc --noEmit` clean, Vitest 253/253, Playwright 6/6 (a one-class CSS removal on a page with no test coverage for its exact wrap point — verified live instead, the only way to actually confirm a text-wrapping fix).

### D097 — [PD/Dev] Scenario assistant's missing account context for "New" — investigated first per the task's instruction: an original gap, never actually closed, not a regression from a later refactor; closed with an in-panel account picker

**4a — the investigation, done before any code was written.** Grepped the whole codebase for "ACCOUNT FOR NEW SCENARIOS": the label still exists, unchanged, at `ScenarioAnalysis.tsx`'s Row 2 tax strip, and is still correctly wired data-wise — its underlying `activeAccountId` is passed to `WhatIfPanel` as a prop and was already correctly used as `effectiveAccountId` whenever "New" is selected (`effectiveAccountId = modifyingScenario ? ... : activeAccountId`, unchanged by this task). So the account-resolution *logic* was never broken. The actual gap is a **visibility** gap, and `WhatIfPanel.tsx`'s own existing code comments already half-diagnosed it: the `ScenarioSummaryCard` component (D067, item 3) exists, in the panel's own words, "because the underlying scenario is obscured by the scrim once the panel is open — this is the only way to see which account/funds/figures are being modified without closing the panel." That exact same scrim obscures the page-level "ACCOUNT FOR NEW SCENARIOS" strip too — but no equivalent surfacing was ever built for the "New" case; the visual slot where the summary card renders was simply left empty when `!modifyingScenario`.

**Conclusion, stated explicitly per the task's requirement: this is an original gap, never actually closed — not a regression.** Two independent facts rule out "regression": (1) Scenario Analysis has never had an account picker of its own at all — D057's account switcher exists only on `FundSelectionAutomated.tsx` and `FundSelectionManual2.tsx` (confirmed against CLAUDE.md's own Account switching entry, which doesn't list `ScenarioAnalysis.tsx` among the pages it touched) — so there was never a version of this page where a user could change the target account for a new scenario without leaving it, panel or no panel. (2) The `ScenarioSummaryCard`'s own comment frames the scrim-obscures-the-page problem as something D067 solved *for the scenario-modification case specifically* — it was never claimed to solve, or even considered for, the "New" case, meaning the gap was present continuously from D056's original panel build through D067's segmented-control refactor and up to now, never addressed at any point in between, not broken by any one of those changes.

**4b — the fix.** New `AccountPicker` component (`WhatIfPanel.tsx`), rendered in the exact same visual slot `ScenarioSummaryCard` occupies — `{modifyingScenario ? <ScenarioSummaryCard .../> : <AccountPicker .../>}` — so there's always something in that slot now, never an empty gap. Shows one button per real account (`portfolio.accounts`, label via the shared `accountTypeLabel()`), highlighting whichever is currently effective. New panel-local state, `newScenarioAccountId` (`null` by default, meaning "no override — use the page's `activeAccountId`," identical to this panel's behavior before this task), overrides `effectiveAccountId` for the "New" branch only; the scenario-modification branch is untouched. Picking a different account resets the in-progress conversation the same way switching the segmented control already does (`selectNewScenarioAccount()`, mirroring `selectScenario()`) — necessary because `effectiveAccountId` feeds `buildPortfolioReferenceContext`'s `useMemo`, so an in-progress conversation grounded in the old account's holdings would otherwise silently go stale under the user. State is panel-lifetime only (resets to default on next open, per D053's established ephemeral-conversation-state pattern) — no new persistence was added.

**Deliberately not built, exactly as the task specified**: the assistant's own natural-language interpretation does not additionally recognize an account mentioned in the message text (e.g., "sell from my Roth IRA") as a second, competing way to set the target account. The picker alone fully resolves the ambiguity the task described; adding message-level account recognition on top would introduce a real, unforced conflict question (which one wins if they disagree?) with no clean answer, for no capability gain the picker doesn't already provide. Left as a stated, deliberate boundary, not silently absent.

**Live-verified**: opened the panel with "New" selected — the picker rendered immediately with "Taxable Brokerage ...4782" pre-highlighted (the real page-level active account, confirming the default-to-`activeAccountId` behavior). Clicked "Traditional IRA ...2973" — selection updated, conversation reset to a fresh greeting, and the page's own "ACCOUNT FOR NEW SCENARIOS" strip (visible behind the scrim before the panel opened) stayed unchanged at "Taxable Brokerage ...4782," confirming the picker is a genuine independent override, not a mirror of the page value. Sent a real utterance ("Sell $2,000 of VFITX," a Traditional IRA holding) — the request reached `/api/interpret` and failed with the expected sandboxed-environment 502 (this session's Browser pane has no outbound internet, a already-established, unrelated environment characteristic — D067/D073/D078/D087/D088), correctly triggering D066's generic failure message with a Retry option, not a crash or a leaked error. Switched to "Scenario 1" (summary card correctly replaced the picker) and back to "New" (picker correctly returned, still showing Traditional IRA selected, conversation freshly reset) — confirming the picker and summary card toggle correctly in both directions and the account choice persists across a round-trip through an existing scenario, as designed. Demo state reset afterward via Demo Settings.

**Baseline**: `tsc --noEmit` clean, Vitest 253/253 (no new unit tests — this is UI/state-wiring inside an existing, already-tested component; the existing Playwright D067 coverage for the segmented control's summary-card appear/disappear behavior continues to pass unmodified, confirming the picker's sibling-slot toggle didn't regress it), Playwright 6/6.

### D098 — [PM] "Improvements to the underlying application" added to docs/progress.html — a third retrospective section, verified against the actual decision log rather than trusted as given, and expanded well beyond the eight starting items

The task supplied 8 candidate items and explicitly asked for verification against `DECISIONS.md` rather than trusting the list as complete — "it's a starting point, not a final list." All 8 were confirmed real and correctly attributed: the account-type selector wiring and the tax bracket control (D057, D079) were each genuinely "never built before"; the Order Confirmation/Execution Summary account-display fixes matched this session's own D095 exactly; the footer alignment fix matched D070 (confirmed still shipped in D077); the engine budget-splitting gap and the Manual-mode stale-account-data bug both trace to D059, but to its *two separate* findings, not one — worth noting since the task's phrasing could have read as describing a single bug.

**Systematic scan for anything else fitting the same description turned up six more, genuinely distinct findings, added beyond the given 8:**
- **D057's own second half** — once the selector was wired up, ~10 call sites were found silently computing capital-gains tax on non-taxable accounts with no account-type gate, fabricating tax figures and a "you saved $X" banner on a Roth IRA that owes no capital-gains tax under any method. This is arguably more significant than the wiring fix itself and was not covered by the given list's phrasing (which named the selector, not what selecting revealed).
- **D058** — two layout-coherence bugs surfaced by the same account-switching work: a fund table that could render under the wrong account's heading, and an account list that visually reordered itself on every switch.
- **D060's first finding** — a third, distinct Manual-mode account-switching bug (a stale-closure trap causing the sell amount to vanish), separate from D059's stale-session-data bug the given list already named.
- **D061** — "Edit scenario" never restored which account a saved scenario belonged to, silently substituting a different account's freshly-computed recommendation for the actual saved data.
- **D083/D084/D085** — Traditional IRA withdrawals were never actually taxed as ordinary income anywhere in the app (silently treated as tax-free, identical to Roth IRA) before being implemented for real, consolidated into one shared calculation, and given a matching display redesign across all six real touchpoints. Judged worth including despite being adjacent to the AI-narration work that motivated it (narration needed a real figure to state) — the calculation and its correctness are a base-application concern independent of whether narration ever mentions the result.
- **D086** — a fixed-height container in Manual mode, sized for old short placeholder text, cut real content off mid-word once real content grew longer than the container had ever anticipated — a general "container doesn't accommodate variable-length content" layout bug, not narration-specific, even though narration's longer text is what exposed it.

**Excluded, and why, rather than silently left out**: D082 (Impact badge misleading-display gap) and D052 (SpecID/Traditional-IRA business rule gap) both fit the *topic* but not the *tense* — both are documented, not fixed, so neither is an "improvement... closed," per the task's own framing. D078 (retirement-age/filing-status assumptions made explicit in the dataset) was considered and left out as a softer fit — it's assumption documentation, not a functional fix or capability, a different kind of rigor than the rest of this list. D081 (tax bracket dialog selection persistence) was folded into the existing tax-bracket bullet rather than given its own entry, since it's a refinement of the same D079 feature, not a separately-discovered defect.

**Structure**: three grouped sub-lists (Account switching / Tax accuracy / Display correctness) inside the page's existing `.open-list` bordered-card pattern, with a new lightweight `.improvement-group-label` style (small, bold, uppercase, `--accent` color) for the three group headers — reused the existing `<strong>`/`<span class="dim">` item pattern already established by "What's not built yet" rather than inventing new markup. Placed directly after "Design lessons" and before "What's not built yet," completing a three-part retrospective run (engineering highlights → design lessons → base-application improvements) before the page turns forward-looking (open items, roadmap). Section intro states the framing explicitly, per the task's instruction: this is evidence AI-feature work also meaningfully improved the underlying application's quality, a real complementary outcome, not the project's primary objective.

**While writing this entry, found and fixed a real duplication bug in `DECISIONS.md` itself**: D097's own entry ended with two consecutive "**Baseline**" lines, the second a stray, mismatched duplicate (citing "249 prior + 4 new" — D093's coach-mark test count, not anything D097 actually added) left over from an editing mistake in the prior turn. Removed the stray line; D097's real baseline (no new tests, existing Playwright coverage unmodified) is unaffected and was already correctly stated in the line that remains.

**No application code touched** — this was documentation-only, per the task's own explicit statement ("No code changes needed for this task").

### D099 — [PD] AI Approaches Landscape diagram — the bottom axis label moved from a trailing caption to a header introducing the "not built" section, and the eight not-built approaches split into two columns by status, per direct user feedback on the rendered page

The user pointed at a live screenshot of D094's vertical diagram and flagged two things: the "↓ Higher autonomy / broader scope" label reading as oddly placed underneath the full list of eight not-built approaches rather than introducing anything, and a suggestion to split those eight into two columns separating "considered and rejected" from "not needed."

**Root cause of the placement complaint**: the label wasn't a rendering bug — it sat exactly where D094 put it, after `.spectrum-track` and before the legend — but structurally it read as a trailing footnote, not a header, since nothing about its position or styling distinguished it from a caption closing out the list versus one introducing anything after it. The opening `"↑ Lower autonomy"` label worked because it visibly introduces the footprint band directly below it; the bottom label had no equivalent role once the list beneath it — the legend — isn't part of the autonomy ordering at all.

**Fix, addressing both points together rather than separately**: moved the label to `.spectrum-axis-label.mid`, positioned between the footprint band and the not-built section, with its text changed to `"↓ Higher autonomy / broader scope — not built"` so it now reads as that section's actual header, mirroring the top label's role for the footprint band above it. The eight not-built nodes were then split into `.spectrum-columns`, two flex children side by side: a 3-item "Considered and explicitly rejected" column (amber) and a 5-item "Not needed for this project's scope" column (grey), each with its own `.spectrum-column-header` naming the status the column represents. This let the per-item `.spectrum-status` text ("Not used" / "Considered, rejected") be dropped from each row — redundant once the column header already states it — decluttering each node down to just a dot and a label. `.spectrum-track` (the now-unused single-column wrapper) was removed; `.footprint-band` and `.spectrum-columns` are direct children of `.spectrum-outer` instead. Added a `max-width: 640px` stacking breakpoint for the two columns, since the page's own responsive rules require wide content to degrade gracefully rather than only being designed for one fixed width.

**Live-verified**: opened the regenerated page, confirmed the mid-label now sits directly above the two-column section as its header (not trailing beneath the list), and confirmed both columns render with the correct items, correct counts (3 and 5), and correct color-coded headers.

**No application code touched** — `docs/progress.html` only, same as D094/D090.

### D100 — [PD/Dev] "Breakdown ▾"/"Why? ▾" expanders built and applied to all six real touchpoints — a shared expand-in-place component, a real engine extension to expose the actual blended-rate attribution (not a naive re-derivation), and a live-caught positioning bug in the shared trigger itself

**Item 0 — self-audit, done before any code was written.** Read `accountBanner.ts` in full: confirmed it's a content-decision layer (labels/booleans), not a layout component, and this task extends it (one new constant, `ROTH_QUALIFIED_DISTRIBUTION_NOTE`) rather than building a parallel mechanism. Checked `computeNetTax()`: it computed real intermediates internally (`totalSTGain`/`totalLTGain`/`taxableAtST`/`taxableAtLT`) but discarded all of them, returning only the final number — confirming the self-audit's predicted risk was real. Extended it rather than building a second calculation path: `computeNetTaxBreakdown()` (`engine/index.ts`) now does the real work and exposes every intermediate; `computeNetTax()` became a one-line wrapper returning `.total` — byte-identical behavior for all six existing call sites, confirmed by the full pre-existing test suite passing unchanged.

**The one subtlety that made this more than a mechanical extension**: `taxableAtST`/`taxableAtLT` (the blended-rate attribution the engine actually taxes) are **not** the same number as the raw `netSTGain`/`netLTGain` the moment one category is a loss the other category's gain absorbs. Worked the arithmetic by hand before writing any UI code: a $500 net ST loss fully absorbed by a $1,000 net LT gain produces a real total of $75 (500 taxable, all at the 15% LT rate) — a naive "netST × st_rate + netLT × lt_rate" line would show $30 instead ((−500×24%)+(1000×15%)), silently disagreeing with the number already on screen. The mirror case (ST gain outweighing an LT loss) diverges the same way in the other direction ($168 real vs. $195 naive). Both are covered as dedicated `engine.test.ts` cases with the naive-vs-real numbers asserted explicitly, not just the correct answer in isolation — and both were reproduced **live**, unprompted, by the app's own real $25,000 taxable-brokerage fixture (VTSAX +$1,322.31 ST / VBTLX −$1,052.12 LT), confirmed via the actual rendered popover showing `$270.19 × 24% = $64.85` rather than the wrong `$1,322.31 × 24%`.

**Item 1 — one shared component.** `ExpandableDetail.tsx` (new): a generic "Label ▾"/"Label ▴" trigger, visual style matching the existing "Lot details ▾" pattern's exact classes. Reuses `CoachMark.tsx`'s proven trigger+portal+`getBoundingClientRect()` technique rather than rendering the panel as a normal DOM child — several real call sites (the three Fund Selection pages' 7-card summary banner) are narrow `overflow-hidden` flex cards, where a normally-positioned panel wider than its own card would be silently clipped by that ancestor, exactly the failure mode a portal to `document.body` sidesteps. Used for both "Breakdown ▾" (tax figures, all three account types) and "Why? ▾" (early withdrawal penalty, both IRA types) — one implementation, not two.

**Item 2/3 — taxable brokerage and Traditional IRA breakdown content.** `taxBreakdownDisplay.ts` (new, pure logic — no JSX, independently testable): `formatNettingEquation()` builds the "$645.81 − $483.69 = +$162.12"-style equation only when there's a real netting story — 2+ contributing funds (nonzero in that specific category) with mixed signs; 0–1 contributors or same-sign contributors just show the plain net figure, per the task's own rule ("no real netting story to tell in that case"). `TaxBreakdownPanel.tsx` (new) renders the full panel: net ST/LT gain/loss lines, ST/LT tax lines (using `taxableAtST`/`taxableAtLT`, never the raw net gains — see the subtlety above), total, then a visually-separated "Effective rate (for reference)" line below its own divider. Traditional IRA gets the single-line "{withdrawal} × {rate}% = {tax}" plus effective rate, per the task's spec.

**Item 4 — "Why? ▾" reuses existing documentation, writes no new wording.** Both IRA types' Early Withdrawal Penalty card now hides `EARLY_WITHDRAWAL_PENALTY_NOTE` (unchanged text, already condensed from `sample-dataset.json`'s `note_retirement_status`) behind the same trigger, collapsed by default — previously shown unconditionally.

**Item 5 — Roth's new "Breakdown ▾," flagged explicitly as beyond the reviewed mockup.** New `ROTH_QUALIFIED_DISTRIBUTION_NOTE` constant (`accountBanner.ts`), reusing the *same underlying reasoning* as the penalty note (both condensed from `note_retirement_status`) but stating the fact actually relevant to a $0 *tax* figure (qualified distributions are tax-free) rather than the penalty-specific fact — not the same string copy-pasted into a second place. Read live after building, per the task's own "confirm it reads sensibly rather than assuming it does": *"Investor is 73 — well past the 59½ age and 5-year holding requirements for a qualified Roth distribution, so this withdrawal is entirely tax-free."* Reads sensibly, confirmed live in the actual popover.

**Item 6 — applied individually to all six touchpoints, confirmed not assumed.** `FundSelectionAutomated.tsx`, `FundSelectionManual2.tsx`, `FundSelectionManualLot.tsx` (all three share the 7-card summary-banner shape but are three separate components — each wired and each live-verified independently, not assumed identical once one worked); `ScenarioAnalysis.tsx`, `OrderConfirmation.tsx`, `ExecutionSummary.tsx` (the `TaxRow`-based vertical layouts). Every touchpoint's unconditional "effective rate" line and unconditional penalty-note caption were removed from the collapsed tile — confirmed by `tsc`'s `noUnusedLocals` catching every resulting dead local variable (`fmtPct2`/`fmtRate2`/`effectiveRate`, five separate now-orphaned helpers/fields across five files), each one individually traced and deleted rather than left as dead weight.

**A real, pre-existing data-loss bug found and fixed as a direct consequence of needing both figures correct.** `OrderConfirmation.tsx`'s local `ConfirmData.funds[]` shape only ever stored ONE of a fund's ST/LT gain-loss (`gainLoss`/`gainLossPeriod`, whichever was nonzero first) — silently dropping the other one for a fund with real nonzero gain/loss in *both* categories in the same transaction (a mixed-lot MinTax sale spanning both holding periods). This fed directly into `TransactionRecord.funds_sold[].st_gain_loss`/`lt_gain_loss` at submission time, meaning `ExecutionSummary.tsx`'s own breakdown would have inherited the same data loss. Fixed at the source: `ConfirmData.funds[]` gained `stGainLoss`/`ltGainLoss` fields (populated from the same `stg`/`ltg` locals every branch of `buildConfirmData()` already computes), and `handleSubmit()`'s `funds_sold` mapping now reads them directly instead of reconstructing from the lossy `gainLoss`/`gainLossPeriod` pair.

**A second, related fix for the same reason: `TransactionRecord` gained `st_rate`/`lt_rate` fields**, populated at submission time from the real `activeTaxRates` active then. `ExecutionSummary.tsx`'s breakdown recomputes `computeNetTaxBreakdown()` itself from the frozen `funds_sold` array — using the *store's current* `activeTaxRates` instead would have let the panel disagree with the receipt's own already-committed `est_tax_at_active_rate` the moment a user changed their tax bracket between submitting and viewing the receipt (a real, reachable gap given the tax bracket dialog exists and persists selections, D079/D081) — not a hypothetical, given how much of this project's own history is about exactly this kind of frozen-vs-live state mismatch (D059–D061). Both fields are optional, falling back to this app's default rates (24%/15%) for a transaction recorded before this change existed, same pattern as `account_id`/`account_type`'s own optional-with-fallback precedent (D057).

**A real bug found live, in the shared trigger component itself, not any one touchpoint.** First live check of the Traditional IRA breakdown on `FundSelectionAutomated.tsx` showed the panel's right edge clipped off-screen, text truncated mid-word. Traced via direct DOM inspection (`getBoundingClientRect()` on both the trigger and the rendered panel), not guessed: the trigger `<button>` had stretched to **196.5px wide** inside its `flex flex-col` parent card, because that card has no `items-start` override and Tailwind's `flex-col` default cross-axis alignment is `stretch` — meaning `triggerRect.right` itself already exceeded `window.innerWidth`, and `ExpandableDetail`'s own left-edge nudge (`left = triggerRect.right - panelWidth`) repositioned relative to an edge that was itself off-screen, still overflowing. **Two independent fixes, not one papering over the other**: `self-start w-fit` added to the trigger button (fixes the actual stretching, the root cause), plus a final `Math.min(left, window.innerWidth - panelWidth - 16)` clamp in `computePanelPos()` (defensive — guarantees the panel can never overflow regardless of what geometry a future call site's trigger happens to have, not just this one). Re-verified live after both fixes: the identical Traditional IRA breakdown rendered fully on-screen, no clipping.

**Live-verified across every account type and multiple touchpoints, including real cross-netting cases, not just the happy path**: taxable brokerage mixed-sign case on all three Fund Selection pages (Automated, Manual2, ManualLot) via the real $25,000/VTSAX+VBTLX fixture; Traditional IRA's single-line breakdown on Automated; Roth IRA's new reasoning-only breakdown and the "Why? ▾" penalty explainer on Automated; a second, different cross-netting case (ST gain outweighing an LT loss) on `ScenarioAnalysis.tsx`'s real Scenario 1; the full flow through `OrderConfirmation.tsx` and `ExecutionSummary.tsx` via a real submitted taxable-brokerage order, confirming the breakdown there matches the receipt's own frozen total. Every live-observed total matched the number already on the collapsed tile exactly, in every case checked, including the two genuinely tricky cross-netting scenarios.

**Test coverage**: 14 new `engine.test.ts` cases for `computeNetTaxBreakdown()` (pure gain, both directions of cross-category netting with the naive-vs-real comparison asserted explicitly, pure loss, single fund, 3-fund multi-sign netting, Traditional IRA single/multi-fund, Roth IRA, unresolved account type) plus a `computeNetTax()` regression assertion alongside each; 19 new `taxBreakdownDisplay.test.ts` cases for the netting-equation formatter (single contributor, zero contributors, same-sign pairs in both directions, the task's own literal example, 3-term equations, a zero-amount fund correctly excluded) and end-to-end `buildTaxBreakdownDisplay()` cases against real `computeNetTaxBreakdown()` output, including the cross-netting case's exact displayed strings.

**Baseline**: `tsc --noEmit` clean, Vitest 286/286 (253 prior + 33 new), Playwright 6/6.

### D101 — [Dev] Amount-rejection bug — investigated from the simplest case first, per the task's explicit instruction, and found to be TWO distinct real model-output inconsistencies, not the "history truncation" hypothesis originally suspected

**Investigated in the order the task specified, not the order the bug was originally reported.** Reproduced the single-turn "Sell $5,000 of VTSAX" rejection first — genuinely the simplest case (no history, no ambiguity) — rather than starting from the more complex multi-turn report. Inspected the actual outgoing request payload before touching anything else: built the real prompt via `buildWhatIfPrompt()` (the exact function the app calls) and confirmed "$5,000" was present, verbatim, correctly formatted, in every real request sent — ruling out an application-level mangling bug immediately. This meant the failure had to be on the model's *output* side, not the *input* side, and the "history truncation" hypothesis (the working theory going into this task) was already ruled out for the single-turn case by construction — there is no history to truncate.

**Root cause A, confirmed by making real live model calls, not by reasoning about the prompt alone**: for a fund-specific ("manual") request, `validateCandidate()` (`whatIfValidation.ts`) unconditionally required `candidate.targetSaleAmount` to be a positive finite number *before even checking `candidate.mode`* — but the model was found, live, to inconsistently *omit* this top-level field for a manual-mode candidate, even though the fund's own `sell_amount` and the summary text both correctly stated $5,000 every single time. Reproduced directly: 4 of 10 real single-turn calls (across both Groq and Gemini) omitted `targetSaleAmount` entirely; format variants ("$5,000", "$5,000.00", "5000", "5,000") all exhibited the same omission at a similar rate, ruling out a formatting-specific prompt-clarity issue (item 1's second investigation step) — this is a general model-reliability characteristic, not tied to one phrasing. Traced why this field is even checked: `whatIfCandidateToParams.ts` passes it through to `OptimizationParams`, but `runOptimization()`'s manual branch only ever reads it as a fallback divisor (`sel.sell_amount ?? (targetSaleAmount / count)`) for a fund selection that's missing its own `sell_amount` — and `validateCandidate()`'s own per-fund checks (already present, unchanged) already *require* every non-SpecID selection to state its own `sell_amount` before a candidate is considered valid. So for any candidate that reaches this point, `targetSaleAmount` is structurally redundant — genuinely unused information the assistant's own architecture never needed the model to restate.

**Root cause B, found while reproducing the ORIGINAL multi-turn report specifically (item 1's third investigation step)**: a second, independent, real inconsistency — `manualSelections` sometimes returned as a bare array of fund selections instead of the documented `{"fund_selections":[...]}` wrapper object. Reproduced live in a genuine two-turn sequence (ambiguous "I want to sell some VTSAX" → clarify → "$5,000" answer): in this exact shape, ~4 of 5 real multi-turn calls exhibited the bare-array malformation, versus **zero** occurrences across all 8 real single-turn calls — a real, evidence-backed correlation with conversation history being present, not a uniformly-random glitch. This produces a *different* symptom than root cause A: `candidate.manualSelections?.fund_selections` silently evaluates to `undefined` (arrays have no `.fund_selections` property) → `?? []` → empty array → `validateCandidate()`'s "A fund-specific request needs at least one named fund to sell" — which is precisely the shape of "amount correctly echoed back [in the model's own summary text], then later claimed missing [by the validator, for a reason unrelated to the amount at all]" the original multi-turn report described. **Conclusion, stated as the task required: two distinct real bugs, not one** — root cause A reproduces in both single- and multi-turn conversations; root cause B is specific to multi-turn and was only found by actually reproducing the original report rather than assuming it shared root cause A's mechanism.

**Fixes, one per root cause, at the file that actually owns each concern**: `whatIfValidation.ts`'s `validateCandidate()` now only requires `targetSaleAmount` for `mode: 'automated'` (where it remains the sole real input, unchanged) — manual mode relies entirely on its own already-present per-fund `sell_amount`/lot-override checks. `whatIfResponseParser.ts` normalizes a bare-array `manualSelections` into the documented wrapper at the structural-parsing boundary — still unambiguously "is this shaped like the known response type," the file's own stated job, not a business-rule decision. `whatIfCandidateToParams.ts`'s manual branch now derives `OptimizationParams.targetSaleAmount` as the real sum of the fund selections' own `sell_amount`s, rather than trusting a candidate field now known to be sometimes absent.

**Live-verified end to end, reproducing the exact reported sequence, with the actual fixed code — not a synthetic double.** Made 6 more real Gemini calls for the identical single-turn "Sell $5,000 of VTSAX" utterance and fed each real response through the real `parseInterpreterResponse()` → `validateCandidate()` pipeline with the fix applied: 4 of the 6 real responses exhibited the exact missing-`targetSaleAmount` shape, and all 6 (including the 4 that would previously have been falsely rejected) validated successfully. The parser's bare-array normalization was verified against the exact real candidate shape captured live from the multi-turn reproduction.

**Baseline**: `tsc --noEmit` clean, Vitest 293/293 (286 prior + 7 new), Playwright 6/6.

### D102 — [Dev] A ninth what-if coverage dimension added — candidate schema conformance, found only by making genuine live model calls, since a test double cannot surface a model malformation by construction

Per the task's explicit instruction not to assume "conversation depth" was the right new dimension in advance of D101's actual findings: it was not. D101's two real root causes are not about how deep a conversation goes — they're about whether the model's own JSON response conforms to the schema it was asked to produce, an axis none of the existing 8 dimensions in CLAUDE.md §12's matrix (fund count, account type, cost-basis method, amount specification, lot specification, scenario action, ambiguity level, scope) describe, because all 8 describe properties of the *user's utterance*, not the *model's response shape*.

**Why this dimension could only be found by making real calls, and why the existing 9-original-plus-many test suite couldn't have caught it on its own**: every category in `whatIf.test.ts` (by explicit, longstanding design, CLAUDE.md §7/§12) uses a hand-authored `WhatIfInterpreterLike` test double standing in for "what a correctly-behaving model would return" — the two existing "deliberately wrong" doubles (categories 6/7) simulate the model *ignoring a business rule*, never the model *emitting a structurally near-correct but non-conforming JSON shape*. A test double, by construction, always returns exactly the shape its author typed — it cannot spontaneously omit a field or use the wrong nesting the way a live model genuinely does. This gap was only visible by doing what this suite deliberately never does: making real provider calls and inspecting the literal response text.

**Added to CLAUDE.md §12** as a new prose entry alongside the existing 8-dimension matrix (not force-fit into the existing table's columns, since it describes a genuinely different axis) — states both real conformance failures found, their real observed frequencies (root cause A: ~4/10 single-turn calls across two providers; root cause B: ~4/5 multi-turn calls, 0/8 single-turn calls), and which fix addresses which.

**Test coverage — real reproduced shapes, not hypothetical ones, per the task's own instruction**: `whatIf.test.ts` gained a `describe` block using the *exact* candidate shape captured live from the reproduction (`manualSelections` present, `targetSaleAmount` absent entirely) — asserting it now validates successfully, that automated mode's own `targetSaleAmount` requirement is untouched (a manual-mode-specific fix, not a blanket removal of the check), that a genuinely invalid manual candidate still correctly fails its other checks, and an end-to-end case running the real engine against the live-reproduced shape. `parseInterpreterResponse`'s own test block gained two new cases for the bare-array-to-wrapper normalization, one confirming an already-correct shape is left untouched.

**Baseline**: unchanged from D101 (same commit of work) — `tsc --noEmit` clean, Vitest 293/293, Playwright 6/6.

### D103 — [PD] Segment C narration content rules tightened — two explicit content rules replace a style-only "be terse" instruction, since terseness alone did not stop tax detail and allocation-shift figures from appearing

**The gap, confirmed against real generated output before changing anything**: Segment C's prior prompt instruction was purely about *style* ("write the SHORTEST possible phrasing... no subordinate clauses, no explanation of reasoning") — it said nothing about which *content* to include or exclude. Real output honored the style instruction (a single short sentence, genuinely shorter than Segment D's) while still stating the full ST/LT gain-loss breakdown and all four per-asset-class allocation percentage shifts — both squarely "tax detail" and "optional extra work," exactly what this segment's own research (`segmentResearch.ts`'s `SEGMENT_PROFILES.C.toolMustAvoid`) says to avoid, regardless of how tersely they're phrased. Brevity and content-appropriateness are independent properties; optimizing only for the former left the latter unaddressed.

**Fix**: `narrationPrompt.ts`'s Segment C entry now states two explicit, numbered content rules alongside the existing style/brevity guidance and the segment's real research-goal framing: (1) never state the short-term/long-term breakdown separately — state only the single net tax figure; (2) never state per-asset-class allocation percentage shifts. The existing style instruction and the segment's real quoted goal/avoid-list (from `segmentResearch.ts`, unchanged, still the shared literal source with Feature 2's own `whatIfPrompt.ts` SEGMENT_TONE) are kept, not replaced wholesale — the fix adds the missing content layer rather than discarding the working style layer.

**Live-verified with real generated output, compared directly against the task's own target example**: called the real Gemini API three times for the real $25,000 taxable-brokerage automated fixture (VTSAX $15,000 / VBTLX $10,000, real net tax $64.85 — the exact figures the task's target example uses). All three real outputs correctly omit both forbidden content categories. One attempt read, nearly verbatim: *"Selling $15,000.00 of Vanguard Total Stock Market Index Fund Admiral Shares and $10,000.00 of Vanguard Total Bond Market Index Fund Admiral Shares comes to an estimated net tax of $64.85."* — matching the task's own target sentence structure almost exactly (full fund names instead of tickers, this project's existing narration convention, not a deviation). A third attempt additionally stated each fund's trailing-12-month return percentage (e.g. "29.41%") — confirmed this is not a violation of either new rule: those are fund *performance* percentages (an existing, separately-governed narration input), not per-asset-class *allocation-shift* percentages, so they were correctly left untouched by this fix's scope.

**Test coverage**: a new `narration.test.ts` case asserts both explicit content-rule sentences are present in the real system prompt sent to the model, alongside the pre-existing D074 test (still passing unchanged) confirming Segment C's style instruction and real research-goal grounding remain intact.

**Baseline**: `tsc --noEmit` clean, Vitest 294/294 (293 prior + 1 new), Playwright 6/6.

### D104 — [PD/Dev] Fund Selection assistant entry point — a muted banner on Automated/Manual2, reusing existing live form state for visibility, navigating into Scenario Analysis's existing panel via a one-time router-state signal

**Self-audit performed before writing any new logic, per the task's explicit instruction — confirmed both pieces of state to reuse, no new tracking added.** `FundSelectionAutomated.tsx`'s existing `inputDollars` local state (`useState(targetSaleAmount ?? 0)`) is the exact same value already gating "Calculate"/"Recalculate"'s `disabled` prop — reused directly (`inputDollars <= 0`) rather than duplicated into a second flag. `FundSelectionManual2.tsx`'s existing Zustand `manualActiveFundIds` array — mutated by the same `handleSell()`/`handleCancel()` calls that already govern which rows render as active/editable — is reused directly (`manualActiveFundIds.length === 0`). Both conditions are computed live every render from state the app already tracks for an unrelated purpose, so the banner has no independent "has it been shown" memory and is guaranteed to reappear the moment the underlying condition reverts — no separate case to get wrong.

**New shared component, not duplicated JSX**: `dev/src/components/FundSelectionAssistantEntry.tsx` — muted `text-vg-ink-muted` link-style button with a small `Sparkles` icon, deliberately not styled as a competing call-to-action against "Get recommendation." Rendered identically at the identical position (below the title/toggle row, not inside it) on both `FundSelectionAutomated.tsx` and `FundSelectionManual2.tsx`, each supplying its own visibility condition via a wrapping `{condition && <FundSelectionAssistantEntry />}` — the component itself holds no page-specific logic. Deliberately **not** added to `FundSelectionManualLot.tsx`, per the task's own reasoning: that page is only reachable once a fund is already active, so the "nothing specified yet" condition this exists for can never be true there.

**Navigation reuses an existing codebase pattern, not a new mechanism**: clicking calls `navigate('/scenarios', { state: { openAssistant: true } })` — the same one-time router-state-signal shape `FundSelectionManualLot.tsx` already uses for `navigate('/manual-lot', { state: { fund: ticker } })`. `ScenarioAnalysis.tsx` reads it via `useState(() => !!locationState?.openAssistant)` — a **lazy initializer**, deliberately, so the signal is consumed exactly once at mount rather than re-applied on every render; using `location.state` directly in the render body would have made the panel "sticky" open forever, which is wrong for something the user should be able to freely close afterward. This governs only the panel's *initial* value — the page's own pre-existing "Scenario assistant" header button (`onClick={() => setAssistantOpen(true)}`) was not touched at all, satisfying the task's explicit "do not change Scenario Analysis's own header button" constraint; confirmed both in source (only the state declaration changed) and live (the header button still renders and opens the panel independently after arriving via the new entry point).

**Live-verified all four required transitions plus the navigation check, in the Browser pane against the real running app:**
- Automated, empty→filled: banner visible at `$0`; entering `5000` via the amount field hid it and enabled Calculate. ✅
- Automated, filled→cleared: clearing the field back to empty (via `form_input` setting an empty string, after keyboard-based clearing proved unreliable in this sandbox's automation) made the banner reappear and Calculate grey out again. ✅
- Manual, inactive→active: with no fund row active, banner visible; clicking VTSAX's "Sell" button (activating the row, "Cancel" now shown) hid the banner. ✅
- Manual, active→canceled: clicking "Cancel" on the now-active VTSAX row (all rows back to showing "Sell") made the banner reappear. ✅
- Navigation: clicking the banner from Manual mode landed directly on Scenario Analysis with the Scenario assistant panel already open and defaulted to "New," with the account picker and conversation input immediately usable — no second click required to open the panel itself.

**Baseline**: `tsc --noEmit` clean, Vitest 294/294 (unchanged — no new automated test added; this task's own validation instruction specified live browser verification of the four transitions and the navigation, not new unit coverage), Playwright 6/6 (unchanged).

### D105 — [PD/Dev] fund-selection-assistant-entry-gap — D104's self-audit named the right button but instrumented the wrong page; a third, distinct landing page (`FundSelectionEntry.tsx`) had no banner at all, reproducibly, not intermittently

**The reported discrepancy, reproduced exactly, on the first genuinely fresh look at the true root route.** D104's own self-audit instruction asked: "what state currently governs 'Get recommendation''s enabled/disabled condition?" — but D104's investigation answered that question by pointing at `FundSelectionAutomated.tsx`'s "Calculate" button (`inputDollars <= 0`), a button with a **different label entirely**. The actual button named "Get recommendation" lives on a third, separate page never touched by D104: `FundSelectionEntry.tsx`, rendered at the app's root route (`<Route path="/" element={<FundSelectionEntry />} />`, `App.tsx`) — the real first-visit landing screen a user reaches by navigating to the bare app URL, distinct from `FundSelectionAutomated.tsx` (route `/automated`, reached only *after* a first amount has already been submitted from this entry page, or via direct/programmatic navigation). D104's own decision text even quotes "Get recommendation" verbatim while describing `FundSelectionAssistantEntry`'s styling goal — the language from the task's self-audit was carried through without anyone noticing it named a button that doesn't exist on either page actually instrumented.

**Root cause, confirmed by elimination against the task's own three named hypotheses, not assumed:**
- **Wiring bug — this is it, confirmed directly.** `FundSelectionEntry.tsx` never imported or rendered `FundSelectionAssistantEntry` anywhere; there was no condition to evaluate because the component was never referenced at all.
- **Condition-logic bug — ruled out.** N/A per the above; nothing was there to have a broken condition.
- **Stale build/cache — ruled out, with direct evidence, not just a reload.** Fetched the actual dev-server-served, esbuild-transformed source for `FundSelectionAutomated.tsx` (`fetch('/src/pages/FundSelectionAutomated.tsx')` from within the running page) and confirmed the real served bundle already contained `inputDollars <= 0 && jsxDEV(FundSelectionAssistantEntry, ...)` — the current code, correctly wired, was being served with no discrepancy from source. Cross-checked the dev server's own log (`preview_logs`): one `.env.local`-triggered restart at 7:47 AM, then continuous uptime with no further restarts through the HMR updates for `FundSelectionAutomated.tsx`/`FundSelectionManual2.tsx` at 7:05 PM (D104's own edits landing) and beyond — no restart or caching gap exists anywhere near the relevant window. The dev server and the browser were never the problem.

**Reproduced on a genuinely fresh, network-forced reload of the real root route** (`navigate({ url: 'http://localhost:54051/', force: true })`, confirmed via `location.href` and `get_page_text`): "Sell & Rebalance" / Automated-Manual toggle / "How much would you like to sell?" / grayed "Get recommendation" — with **zero occurrence of the banner text anywhere on the page**, deterministically, not intermittently. This is `FundSelectionEntry.tsx` exactly as the original discrepancy report described it, not a race condition or a caching artifact — it reproduces every time, because the component genuinely was never there.

**Reconciling with D104's "confirmed" report — not a false report, a scope miss.** Every one of D104's own live-verification steps explicitly navigated to `/automated` or was already sitting on a page reached via `/automated`, per its own recorded tool-call sequence — it never once loaded the bare root route in this session. D104's verification was accurate for the page it actually tested (`FundSelectionAutomated.tsx`'s own instrumentation is correct and remains correct, unchanged by this fix); the gap was in scope, not in execution — the self-audit misidentified which file the task's own quoted button name belonged to, and no later step cross-checked that assumption against the actual root route.

**Fix**: added the same import and the same `{!hasAmount && <FundSelectionAssistantEntry />}` gate to `FundSelectionEntry.tsx`, in the same position (below the title/toggle row) as the other two pages — reusing the exact same shared `FundSelectionAssistantEntry` component D104 already built, with no changes to that component itself. `FundSelectionEntry.tsx`'s Manual toggle immediately calls `navigate('/manual-2')` on click (no local "manual, inactive" state ever renders on this page), so no second gap exists here — only the Automated/empty-amount case needed wiring.

**Live-verified on this page specifically, fresh reload each time**: empty amount → banner visible (`get_page_text` and screenshot both confirm, alongside the correctly-grayed "Get recommendation" button); entering `5000` → banner hides; clearing back to empty → banner reappears; clicking the banner → lands on Scenario Analysis with the Scenario assistant panel already open on "New," same as D104's own navigation check.

**Baseline**: `tsc --noEmit` clean, Vitest 294/294 (unchanged), Playwright 6/6 (unchanged).

### D106 — [PD] fund-selection-assistant-entry-styling — the banner rebuilt as a bordered box matching `fund-selection-assistant-entry-mockup.html` exactly, replacing a bare hover-only text link

**The gap**: D104/D105's `FundSelectionAssistantEntry.tsx` shipped as a plain `text-vg-ink-muted` link-style button with only a hover-opacity affordance — no visible background, no border, no explicit "button" to notice at a glance. This didn't match the actual spec: `fund-selection-assistant-entry-mockup.html` (provided mid-task after an initial search of Downloads/Desktop/Documents/the repo turned up no match — the user then supplied the exact path directly) shows a full-width bordered box with a light mint background, the descriptive text on the left, and a distinctly-styled pill button, "Ask the Scenario assistant," on the right — a self-evidently interactive control, not a hover-discoverable link.

**Rebuilt to match the mockup's literal values, not the app's nearest named token.** The mockup's colors (`#f0fbf8` background, `#b7ece1` border, `#0f6e56` text, `#14b8a6` button border) are close to but distinct from this app's existing `vg-teal` token (`#00bda3`, `tailwind.config.js`) — used as inline Tailwind arbitrary values rather than forced onto `vg-teal`, following the same precedent already established elsewhere in this codebase for Figma/mockup-sourced one-off colors (e.g. `FundSelectionAutomated.tsx`'s `bg-[#e8f5f0]` summary strip is its own arbitrary value, not retrofitted onto a named token either). Copy also updated to match the mockup verbatim: left text "Prefer to describe what you'd like to do instead?", button text "Ask the Scenario assistant" (previously a single run-on sentence with no distinct button element at all).

**Applied to all three pages this component renders on** (`FundSelectionEntry.tsx`, `FundSelectionAutomated.tsx`, `FundSelectionManual2.tsx`) via the one shared component — no per-page divergence, since all three already rendered `<FundSelectionAssistantEntry />` identically and only the component itself needed to change.

**Live-verified on all three pages, fresh reload each time**: full-width mint box with visible border, sparkle + text on the left, distinct pill button on the right, confirmed via screenshot on `FundSelectionEntry.tsx` (route `/`), `FundSelectionAutomated.tsx` (route `/automated`), and `FundSelectionManual2.tsx` (Manual mode, no fund active) — same visual treatment on all three, matching the mockup.

**Baseline**: `tsc --noEmit` clean, Vitest 294/294 (unchanged — no test asserts on styling), Playwright 6/6 (unchanged).

### D107 — [Dev] scenario-auto-reseed-bug — a pre-existing bug in `ScenarioAnalysis.tsx`'s own mount effect, not something the new Fund Selection entry point introduced; the new entry point only newly exposed a path that already reached it

**Reported**: after explicitly deleting all scenarios (reaching the "No scenarios to compare" empty state), following the new Fund Selection entry point to Scenario Analysis caused 3 scenarios to reappear automatically, contradicting the explicit deletion.

**Investigated per the task's explicit instruction — determined which of the two named hypotheses it actually was, before writing any fix.** Read `ScenarioAnalysis.tsx`'s mount effect directly: `useEffect(() => { if (scenarios.length === 0 && activeScenarioId === null) { setScenarios(seedCanonicalScenarios(portfolio)) } }, [])` — this pre-existing effect (predates this session's Fund Selection entry point work entirely) re-seeds the 3 canonical demo scenarios on **every mount** of the route whenever `scenarios.length === 0`, with no way to distinguish "app just booted, seed demo data" from "user genuinely emptied it out, leave it empty." Checked the new entry point's own navigation call (`navigate('/scenarios', { state: { openAssistant: true } })`) — it passes only `openAssistant`, which the seeding effect never reads; the new code does nothing beyond opening the panel.

**Reproduced the exact discrepancy through the ENTIRELY PRE-EXISTING "Go to Scenario Analysis" button first, deliberately not through any new code, to isolate cause**: seeded scenarios → deleted all 3 via the UI → reached "No scenarios to compare" → navigated to `/automated` → clicked the pre-existing `handleGoToScenarios` button (predates this session, `REQ-A5-004`) → **the 3 canonical scenarios reappeared**, with zero new code involved anywhere in that path. This is conclusive: **the bug is pre-existing in `ScenarioAnalysis.tsx`'s own mount effect, not introduced by the new Fund Selection entry point.** The new entry point is one more of several pre-existing paths (this button, the header "Scenario assistant" button, browser back/forward, any client-side `navigate('/scenarios')`) that were always capable of triggering this — it just happened to be the first one a person used in a delete-then-return sequence with no intervening full reload, since nothing about this app's own scenario deletion flow previously had a reason to be followed by a fresh mount of this specific route in the same session.

**Why this was never noticed before**: `scenarios` (`useAppStore.ts`) has no `persist()` wrapper — it's pure in-memory state, reset only by a genuine hard page reload. A hard reload landing on `/scenarios` correctly re-seeding demo data was always the intended, correct behavior (first real load of a session). The bug only manifests on a client-side SPA remount of the route *within the same session*, after the store had already been emptied by explicit deletion — a narrower window than "any load of this page."

**Fix — distinguish "never seeded this session" from "empty right now," not "seed less eagerly."** Added `scenariosSeeded: boolean` to `useAppStore.ts` (default `false`, in-memory like `scenarios` itself — resets correctly on a genuine hard reload, deliberately untouched by `resetSession()`/"Reset demo," matching the store's own existing comment that scenarios are never cleared by a demo reset) and a new `seedScenariosOnce(scenarios)` action that is a no-op once `scenariosSeeded` is already `true`, regardless of the array's current length. `ScenarioAnalysis.tsx`'s mount effect now calls `seedScenariosOnce(seedCanonicalScenarios(portfolio))` unconditionally (still gated on `activeScenarioId === null`, the original mid-edit guard, unchanged) instead of checking `scenarios.length === 0` itself — the length check is gone from the component entirely; the store now owns "has this already happened."

**Live-verified by reproducing the exact originally-reported sequence with the actual new entry point, not the pre-existing button used for isolation above**: fresh hard reload of `/scenarios` (3 canonical scenarios correctly seed, confirming the fix doesn't break first-load behavior) → deleted all 3 via the UI → "No scenarios to compare" confirmed → navigated to `/` (`FundSelectionEntry.tsx`) → clicked the new "Ask the Scenario assistant" banner button → landed on Scenario Analysis with the panel open on "New" and **"No scenarios to compare" still showing underneath**, confirmed via both `get_page_text` and a screenshot — no scenarios reappeared unless genuinely created.

**Baseline**: `tsc --noEmit` clean (required removing `setScenarios` from `ScenarioAnalysis.tsx`'s store destructure — it became unused once seeding moved fully into `seedScenariosOnce`), Vitest 294/294 (unchanged), Playwright 6/6 (unchanged — no existing test exercised the delete-all-then-return sequence).

### D108 — [Dev] scenario-seeding-hmr-regression — **corrects D107**, doesn't extend it: D107's `scenariosSeeded` flag was correct the whole time; the actual bug was Vite HMR silently resetting the whole store on every further edit, mistaken for a navigation-triggered reseed

**Reported**: "The D107 fix made this worse, not better — scenarios now populate to 3 regardless of navigation path, not just in the one originally-reported sequence." Per the task's explicit instruction, this was traced by direct state inspection, not another assumption-based patch.

**Step 1 — confirmed the actual scope first, not assumed.** A temporary `window.__appStore` debug hook (`useAppStore.ts`, removed after use) exposed the real store to direct inspection from the Browser pane. A genuinely fresh session (hard reload) correctly seeded 3 scenarios on first `/scenarios` visit. Deleting all 3 via real in-app clicks, then navigating away and back via real in-app clicks (never the `navigate` tool's URL-based hard reload, which — confirmed directly — resets the entire store, correctly, since that's a genuine fresh session) reproduced **zero** reseeding: `scenariosCount: 0, scenariosSeeded: true` held at every step. D107's flag and effect were not the bug.

**Step 2 — traced `scenariosSeeded`'s actual real-time value through its full lifecycle, per the task's explicit demand.** Confirmed via direct `getState()` reads at every step of the original repro (fresh session → first seed → delete 1 → delete 2 → delete 3 → navigate away → navigate back): the flag correctly flips to `true` on first seed and correctly survives real in-app navigation. It is genuinely in-memory (no `persist()`, matching `scenarios` itself) and does **not** reset on ordinary component mount — that hypothesis from the task's own list didn't hold up under direct inspection. What actually resets it: **any further code edit to `useAppStore.ts` (or, transitively, anything importing it) while a session is in progress**, confirmed by triggering a real edit mid-sequence and watching the flag flip back to a fresh-default state. The dev-server log showed why: `useAppStore.ts` had no HMR accept boundary of its own, so Vite propagated its invalidation up through **every page that imports the store** — the log line `hot updated: /src/pages/ScenarioAnalysis.tsx` (and seven other pages) fired together on a single store-only edit, and React Fast Refresh remounted each of them as part of that propagation, re-firing `ScenarioAnalysis.tsx`'s own mount effect. Nothing in D107's own code was wrong; the entire store silently reset to its defaults out from under it, with no `page reload` line in the log (unlike a genuine hard reload), making it look exactly like "regardless of navigation" — because navigation had nothing to do with it. This is why the original "verified live" claim didn't match an independent look: D107's own verification happened in a stretch with no further edits landing, while the person's own testing pass most likely followed further edits (a realistic, ordinary situation in an actively-worked codebase, not a testing mistake).

**Step 3 — fixed the real mechanism, verified with concrete state evidence, not a clean screenshot.** `useAppStore.ts` now calls `import.meta.hot.accept()` (no handler) — a genuine self-accept boundary, so Vite re-evaluates only this one file on its own edits and stops propagating to importing pages entirely; their mount effects no longer re-fire from an unrelated store-only change. As a second, independent layer, a `dispose`/restore pair snapshots the store's plain-data fields into `import.meta.hot.data` before disposal and restores them after re-creation — deliberately excluding function values, since an action snapshotted from the disposed module closes over that module's own stale `set`/`get` and would silently write into an orphaned store instance if restored verbatim. Also added `src/vite-env.d.ts` (`/// <reference types="vite/client" />`, the standard Vite scaffold file, absent until now) so `import.meta.hot`'s types resolve without a cast.

**Re-verified the full original sequence end-to-end with the actual fix in place, checking the real flag's value at every step — including, critically, a real mid-sequence edit to `useAppStore.ts` to reproduce the exact regression trigger**: fresh session (`0, false`) → first `/scenarios` visit (`3, true`) → deleted all 3 (`0, true`) → **a real edit landed mid-sequence** (`0, true` — held) → navigated away via a real click (`0, true`, url `/`) → navigated back via the new entry point (`0, true`, url `/scenarios`) → confirmed both via `getState()` and the rendered page text ("No scenarios to compare"). Cross-checked against the dev-server console log: the post-fix edit shows only `hot updated: /src/store/useAppStore.ts` (plus unrelated `index.css`) — no page components listed, confirming propagation is actually contained now, not just that the outcome happened to look right.

**Baseline**: `tsc --noEmit` clean, Vitest 294/294 (unchanged), Playwright 6/6 (unchanged — this fix is dev-server/HMR-specific; `import.meta.hot` is stripped entirely in a production build, so it has zero runtime effect outside `npm run dev`).

### D109 — [Dev] reset-demo-restores-scenarios-bug — "Reset demo" silently reseeded the 3 canonical scenarios instead of clearing them, because it triggers a genuine hard reload and `scenarios` has no persistence; fixed at the reset flow specifically, not in the shared `resetSession()` action

**Reported** (user, direct observation): "Manually deleting scenarios works, but try using the Reset Demo button within the Demo Settings dialog... It restores the 3 scenarios, instead of clearing all scenarios."

**Root cause, traced directly**: `DemoSettingsDialog.tsx`'s confirmed "Reset demo" button does `window.location.href = '/?reset=true'` — a genuine hard browser navigation, not React Router's client-side `navigate()`. Since `scenarios`/`scenariosSeeded` (`useAppStore.ts`) have no `persist()` wrapper (confirmed in D107/D108's own investigation), a hard reload always resets both to their in-memory defaults (`[]`/`false`), regardless of what the user had done beforehand. `FundSelectionEntry.tsx`'s `/?reset=true` handler calls `resetSession()` and explicitly re-derives several other fields for the same reason (providers, tax bracket selection — see its own D073/D081 comments) but never accounted for scenarios — so the very next visit to `/scenarios` legitimately saw `scenariosSeeded: false` and correctly (by D107/D108's own design) treated it as a genuine fresh session, reseeding the 3 canonical scenarios. This silently overrode an explicit prior deletion, and did so even for a user who had never touched scenarios at all — Reset demo would always leave 3 scenarios present, contrary to its own UI copy, which said nothing about scenarios one way or the other.
