**Why the fix does not belong in `resetSession()` itself**: that action is also called from `ExecutionSummary.tsx`'s `handleStartNewSale()`, after every completed transaction — wiping the user's other saved comparison scenarios there would be a real regression, unrelated to what "Reset demo" is for. `resetSession()`'s own existing comment ("scenarios are NOT cleared on session reset") is correct and stays correct for that caller; it's now clarified in-place to explain this is specifically about *that* caller, not a blanket rule Reset demo also follows.

**Fix**: `FundSelectionEntry.tsx`'s `/?reset=true` handler now calls `seedScenariosOnce([])` immediately after `resetSession()` — reusing the exact action D107 already built (a no-op once `scenariosSeeded` is true, otherwise sets both fields atomically) rather than adding a new one. Since this flow always starts from a fresh hard reload, `scenariosSeeded` is reliably `false` at this point, so the call unconditionally applies, marking seeding as "already done, with nothing" — the next `/scenarios` visit correctly shows the empty state instead of reseeding. `DemoSettingsDialog.tsx`'s "Reset demo" description updated to say "clears all completed transactions and saved scenarios" (previously silent on scenarios entirely, despite already silently restoring them).

**Live-verified**: clicked "Reset demo" → confirmed → landed on `/` → clicked the new Fund Selection entry-point banner into Scenario Analysis → **"No scenarios to compare"**, not the 3 canonical scenarios. Separately confirmed a genuine first-ever fresh session (a plain hard reload directly to `/scenarios`, not via `?reset=true`) still correctly seeds the 3 canonical scenarios — this fix is scoped to the reset flow specifically, not a change to first-visit seeding behavior.

**Baseline**: `tsc --noEmit` clean, Vitest 294/294 (unchanged), Playwright 6/6 (unchanged — no existing test exercised the Reset demo → Scenario Analysis path).

### D110 — [PM] Full git-history credential audit, ahead of any public/deployment planning — clean, on every angle checked

Before any repository-visibility or deployment planning could responsibly proceed, the entire git history (all 16 commits, the single `master` branch, no tags, no remotes ever configured) was audited for a leaked credential — not just the current working tree, per the task's explicit instruction that this gate everything else.

**Method — multiple independent angles, not one check treated as sufficient:**
1. **Every filename ever tracked, across all commits** (`git log --all --pretty=format: --name-only | sort -u`): only `dev/.env.example` (the committed placeholder file) ever appears. `dev/.env.local` was never staged or committed at any point — `git log --all --follow -- dev/.env.local` returns nothing.
2. **Real API key patterns, searched across every diff in history.** Started with prefix-based patterns for Gemini/Groq/Anthropic (`AIza…`, `gsk_…`, `sk-ant-…`), then re-verified against this project's own actual real key formats — checking only the first several characters of each real value in the live, gitignored `.env.local` (never printed in full, never logged) — since the real Gemini key turned out not to start with the assumed "AIza" prefix at all. Zero matches under either version of the search.
3. **A generic high-entropy sweep**: every token 30+ characters long, anywhere in the full diff history, following an `=` or `:`. All 44 unique results are legitimate code — dotted property-access chains, function names, local file paths — nothing secret-shaped.
4. **The two real `process.env.ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`/`GEMINI_API_KEY` matches that did surface** are safe test-fixture code (`narrationGenerator.test.ts`-style): reads whatever key is currently in the environment, temporarily deletes it to test the no-key failure path, restores it in a `finally` block. Variable references, never literal secret values.
5. **`.gitignore` coverage verified functionally, not just read**: `git check-ignore -v dev/.env.local` and `dev/node_modules` both confirm actual exclusion at their real nested paths (root `.gitignore`'s unanchored `.env.local`/`node_modules/` patterns correctly reach into `dev/`). Build output (`dist/`, `build/`, `.vite/`), Playwright artifacts, and OS/editor files are all covered too.
6. **No way a secret could be hiding in since-reverted history**: `git fsck --unreachable --no-reflogs` returns nothing — no dangling or unreachable objects exist anywhere in the object database. No remote has ever been configured (`git remote -v` empty), no tags. A fully linear, clean history with no rewritten/reset/amended commits that could be masking something.

**Result: clean.** No credential of any kind, at any point in this project's history. This finding gates the deployment planning that follows in the same session (`docs/progress.html`'s new "Next steps" section) — history does not need to be rewritten before this repo can safely go public.

**Baseline**: N/A — investigation only, no code changed.

Per this file's own standing rule ("regenerating means re-reading `CLAUDE.md` and `DECISIONS.md` fresh... never hand-patching the old version"), every item in the prior `docs/progress.html`'s "What's not built yet" list was re-checked against actual current state rather than assumed still accurate, and the underlying `CLAUDE.md` compliance table itself was corrected where the re-check found it stale — not just the generated page.

**New section added**: "Next steps: source control and deployment," documentation-only per the task's explicit instruction — no remote created, no step executed. Includes D110's credential-audit result inline, the exact checklist the task specified (visibility/naming → create repo → add remote and push, flagged as needing explicit confirmation → connect Vercel → configure env vars in Vercel's dashboard, flagged as a manual human-only step, real keys never typed by an agent into a third-party platform → deploy and verify both AI features actually work against Vercel's real environment, not just that the page loads → confirm the root README is accurate before going public). **The README check was performed now, not deferred**: there is currently no root-level README at all, only `dev/README.md`, which is significantly stale — describes the original pre-AI-layer application almost exclusively (no mention of narration or the what-if assistant anywhere in its "Optimization engine" or "Known gaps" sections), links to the *original* project's live deployment as if it were this repo's own, states a local dev port (5173) that doesn't match this project's actual dev server (54051), and names Anthropic as the sole backend provider despite Gemini being the real default since D042. This finding is recorded in the checklist item itself rather than fixed here, per item 3's explicit documentation-only scope — a genuine rewrite is needed, not a light edit, before this repository goes public.

### D112 — [Dev] Segment D's orientation-before-specifics rule tightened — one shared instruction, inconsistently honored across touchpoints (not duplicated copies), fixed by finally referencing a real research field that existed but was never used

**Reported**: reading real generated Segment D output cold showed Scenario Analysis correctly orients before stating specifics ("You're looking at selling about $15,000... A short-term gain means...") but Fund Selection's per-fund rows don't — VTSAX led directly with "Selling $15,000 of [fund]..." — the same shape as every other segment's opening, not what Segment D's own rule is supposed to produce. VBTLX's row was a softer, partial case.

**Self-audit — confirmed which of the task's two hypotheses actually applies, before touching anything.** Read `narrationPrompt.ts` directly: `SEGMENT_TONE.D` is one single shared template string, referenced identically by all four touchpoints via `SEGMENT_TONE[input.segment]` in `buildNarrationPrompt()` — not duplicated, independently-written copies. This rules out the "drifted copies" hypothesis outright; it's the second one — one identical instruction, interpreted inconsistently.

**Checked Order Confirmation and Execution Summary too, as instructed — neither had been checked before, and the assumption that only the two originally-reported examples had the gap turned out to be wrong.** Generated real Segment D output (real Gemini calls, same $25,000 VTSAX/VBTLX automated sale, at all five real call sites — Fund Selection's two per-fund rows, Scenario Analysis, Order Confirmation, Execution Summary) via a temporary script (`_scratch_segd_repro.ts`, deleted after use, never committed — same established pattern as `docs/narration-review-sample.md`'s generation scripts). Read cold:
- Fund Selection VTSAX: **no orientation** — opened "Selling $15,000.00 of your Vanguard Total Stock Market Index Fund..." Confirmed gap.
- Fund Selection VBTLX: partial — opened with an orienting clause before the dollar figure, but weaker than Scenario Analysis's. Confirmed the reported "softer, in-between" case.
- Scenario Analysis: correctly oriented first. Confirmed already right.
- Order Confirmation: correctly oriented first. **Not previously checked — confirmed already right, not a third instance of the gap.**
- Execution Summary: **no orientation at all** — opened "Sold $15,000.00 of Vanguard Total Stock Market Index Fund..." **A real, previously-unflagged third touchpoint with the same gap**, found only because the task explicitly required checking it rather than assuming the two known examples were exhaustive.

**Root cause, found by tracing why an "identical" instruction produces different results**: `SEGMENT_TONE.D`'s existing text interpolated `SEGMENT_PROFILES.D.goal` and `.toolMustAvoid` from `segmentResearch.ts`, but never `.toolMust` — whose real, quoted value is literally `"Orientation before action, plain-language rationale, comfort with incomplete sessions"`. The field existed in the research data the whole time; the prompt simply never referenced it. What the prompt said instead — "Acknowledge the outcome (gain, loss, tax cost) plainly before explaining it" — is a real but narrower instruction the model could (and did) satisfy while still leading with the dollar figure, e.g. VTSAX's actual real output: "...would result in a short-term gain... of $1,322.31, **which is a positive outcome**" — technically acknowledges the outcome's valence before elaborating further, but never withholds the specific transaction until after a genuine orienting sentence, because nothing in the instruction ever said to.

**Fix**: `SEGMENT_TONE.D` now interpolates `SEGMENT_PROFILES.D.toolMust` alongside `.goal`/`.toolMustAvoid` (grounding this in the real research this project already sources everything else in this string from, not inventing new language), and adds an explicit, concrete rule: the first sentence must orient the reader in general terms before any specific fund name or dollar figure appears, with the restriction stated to hold "regardless of tense" — plus one example opening for a proposed/unexecuted transaction and one for an already-completed one, since Execution Summary's failure was plausibly reinforced by the past-tense instruction's own example ("Sold $X of...") modeling a number-first opening with no competing signal to override it.

**Live-verified with fresh real generated output at all five call sites, read cold, after the fix**: every one — both Fund Selection rows, Scenario Analysis, Order Confirmation, and Execution Summary — now opens with a genuine orienting sentence ("You're looking over a proposed plan to sell...", "Here is a summary of the sale that was just completed...") before any fund name or dollar figure appears, in both prospective and past tense.

**Test coverage**: a new `narration.test.ts` case builds two real prompts from the same real engine fixture — one shaped like Fund Selection's per-fund call, one overridden to Execution Summary's past-tense shape — and asserts both contain the real `SEGMENT_PROFILES.D.toolMust` quote and the new explicit "FIRST sentence must orient" / "never open with... regardless of tense" rule text, proving the fix is the one shared instruction, not a per-touchpoint patch.

**`CLAUDE.md` §6 and `docs/progress.html` updated in place, not left stale**: this closes the last item either document had flagged as needing a live check (Segment D's corrected-content verification) — CD-2.x's compliance-table row, its supporting "explicitly deferred" note, and `docs/progress.html`'s Feature 1 card / compliance table / "What's not built yet" list (which now has zero `tag-verify` items) all updated to reflect both C and D being live-verified, plus a new Highlight entry on the page for this specific finding.

**Baseline**: `tsc --noEmit` clean, Vitest 295/295 (294 prior + 1 new), Playwright 6/6.

### D113 — [Dev] segment-d-example-anchoring — D112's own fix re-checked with genuinely different transactions, confirmed real: the model was echoing D112's single example sentence's wording, not just its structural pattern

**Reported**: D112's fix was structurally correct — orientation now consistently preceded specifics — but all three real outputs reviewed in D112 opened with near-identical phrasing ("You're looking at a plan to sell part of [your investment/two of your investments] to raise cash...") despite coming from three different touchpoints. Before treating D112 as fully resolved, this needed checking against genuinely different transactions, not just re-reading the same $25,000 VTSAX/VBTLX case.

**Generated real output for transactions meaningfully different from D112's fixture**, via the same live-call script pattern (temporary, deleted after use, never committed): a single-fund sale (VTIAX $12,000 — a fund D112 never touched), a different two-fund pairing at a different amount and ratio (VTIAX $9,000 + VBIRX $21,000 — neither fund used in D112, $30,000 total vs. D112's $25,000), and a second single-fund sale (VBIRX $3,000, a real $0-net-tax edge case). Read the actual opening sentences across all three, plus re-ran D112's own original VTSAX/VBTLX/Scenario-Analysis fixture for direct comparison.

**Confirmed the suspicion — this was genuine anchoring, not coincidental convergence.** All three new transactions' openings, structurally different (single-fund vs. two-fund, different funds, different dollar amounts, different touchpoints) still converged on the same opening template as D112's original three: "You're looking [at/over] a [proposed] plan to sell...". Across all 8 real outputs collected between D112 and this check, 7 of 8 prospective-tense openings were close paraphrases of the exact single example sentence D112's fix had added to the prompt (`"You're looking at a plan to sell part of two of your investments to raise cash."`) — the model was reproducing that sentence's specific wording pattern, not treating it as one illustration of a structural rule.

**Fix**: replaced the single canonical example sentence per tense with two deliberately differently-worded examples each, explicitly labeled "for illustration only, NOT a template to copy verbatim," plus an explicit instruction to vary the wording rather than default to a fixed opening phrase. The underlying structural rule (orient before naming any fund/dollar figure, regardless of tense) is unchanged — only the part that was giving the model a single phrase to latch onto was rewritten.

**Re-verified with fresh real output — the same three new transactions plus a re-run of D112's exact original fixture, six real Gemini calls total, read cold**: every single one still correctly orients before any specific fund or dollar figure (the structural fix holds, no regression) — and the wording is now genuinely varied: "Before any changes are finalized, here is a breakdown of what a proposed adjustment to your holdings would mean..."; "Here is a look at how a proposed adjustment to your holdings would affect your account, before anything is put into action..."; "This overview outlines how a prospective set of adjustments to your holdings would alter your overall investment mix..."; "This overview breaks down a proposed plan to adjust your holdings..."; "Here is a breakdown of what this proposed reallocation plan would mean for your portfolio...". Different opening words, different clause structures, different verbs — no longer a single repeated phrase, even though (expectedly, and not a concern) they still share the same general semantic frame a "proposed, not-yet-executed transaction" orientation always will.

**Test coverage**: a new `narration.test.ts` case asserts the prompt instructs wording variation ("vary the wording," "not a template to copy verbatim") and explicitly asserts the old single canonical sentence string is no longer present anywhere in the prompt — not just that new text was added alongside it.

**Baseline**: `tsc --noEmit` clean, Vitest 296/296 (295 prior + 1 new), Playwright 6/6.

### D114 — [PM/PD] Root-level README.md written — self-audit found the actual dev port claim in an earlier decision (D111) was itself wrong, not `dev/README.md`'s

**No root-level README existed before this** — only `dev/README.md`, carried forward from the original pre-AI-layer project's scaffold and, per D111's earlier finding, significantly stale in real ways: it links to the *original* project's live Vercel deployment as if it were this repo's own, names Anthropic as the sole backend provider despite Gemini being the real default since D042, and its directory-structure tree is stale (`store/vsr-store.ts` should be `store/useAppStore.ts`; `engine/optimizer.ts`/`types.ts` should be `engine/index.ts`/`verify.ts`; `tests/` mislabeled as Vitest coverage for the engine when it actually holds Playwright specs; a claimed `e2e/` directory and `public/` directory that don't exist; `api/` — now three functions, `narrate.ts`/`interpret.ts`/`demo-config.ts` — never mentioned at all; a "CI: GitHub Actions" tech-stack claim with no `.github/workflows/` anywhere in the repo).

**Self-audit found a real correction to make — to this project's own prior record, not just to `dev/README.md`.** D111 had claimed `dev/README.md`'s stated dev port (5173) "doesn't match this project's actual dev server" (54051, the port this session's Claude Code preview tooling happened to expose). That claim was never actually verified at the time — it was inferred from which port *this specific session's* preview tool showed, not from the project's own configuration. Checked directly this time: `vite.config.ts` hardcodes `port: process.env.PORT ? parseInt(process.env.PORT) : 5173` — 5173 unless a `PORT` env var overrides it, which only this session's own `.claude/launch.json` (`autoPort: true`) sets, for its own sandboxed browser-preview access. Independently corroborated two more ways: `playwright.config.ts`'s own `baseURL` is `http://localhost:5173` (written by an earlier session, never questioned since, and every Playwright run all session has been passing real tests against it), and a genuinely separate, already-running `vite.js` process (confirmed via its real Windows command line, a second one from the Claude-Code-launched instance) was found actually bound to and serving the real app on 5173 at the moment of this check — loaded directly in the Browser pane, title and content confirmed real. `dev/README.md`'s port was correct the entire time; D111's finding about it was the actual error. `DECISIONS.md` is append-only by design (per `CLAUDE.md` §0) — D111's own text is left as the historical record of what was believed at the time, not silently rewritten; this entry is the correction, and `docs/progress.html`'s corresponding checklist note (which had repeated the same wrong claim) is updated in place, since that page is a living summary, not an append-only log.

**Content, per the task's explicit minimum**: what the project is and what it adds (the narration layer, the Scenario assistant, framed as AI-*enabled* design exploration, not AI-assisted development — `CLAUDE.md` §1's own distinction); verified run instructions; the real required/optional env vars (`.env.example` as source of truth: `GEMINI_API_KEY` required for the default setup, `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`/`GROQ_API_KEY` optional, `NARRATION_PROVIDER`/`WHATIF_PROVIDER` optional) and how the in-app Demo Settings dialog's runtime override relates to them (per-request override wins when set, env var is the fallback — confirmed against `getActiveGenerator(override?)`'s real signature, and that the dialog's own dropdowns currently offer only Gemini/Groq per D072, not because Anthropic doesn't work); pointers to `CLAUDE.md`, `DECISIONS.md`, and `docs/progress.html` (naming its AI-approaches-landscape and design-lessons sections specifically, per the task's own instruction); and a scope note quoting the app's own "Reset demo" copy ("Not part of the production feature set — for demonstration purposes only") rather than writing a new disclaimer from scratch.

**Every command and path in the README was actually run, not just written as plausible instructions**: `npm install` (idempotent, clean), `npm run dev` (already confirmed live via the independent-process check above), `npm test` → real 296/296 pass, `npm run test:e2e` → real 6/6 pass (the actual npm-script alias, not just `npx playwright test` directly), `npm run build` → real successful Vite production build producing `dist/` (confirmed still correctly gitignored). The `cp .env.example .env.local` command's syntax was verified against a throwaway target rather than actually run against the real `.env.local` (which holds real keys) — running it for real would have destroyed a real secret file to prove a `cp` command works. `CLAUDE.md`/`DECISIONS.md`/`docs/progress.html` link targets confirmed to exist at their referenced relative paths from repo root. The Demo Settings "gear icon, top right" description was checked against a live screenshot, not assumed from memory.

**Scope, deliberately not exceeded**: `dev/README.md`'s own real errors (the deployment link, the Anthropic-only claim, the stale directory tree, the false CI claim) are left unfixed — the task was to write the *root* README, not repair `dev/README.md`, and the new root README is self-sufficient for what a visitor actually needs (what this is, how to run it, where to read more) without depending on `dev/README.md` being accurate for anything load-bearing.

**Baseline**: `tsc --noEmit` clean, Vitest 296/296 (unchanged — documentation only), Playwright 6/6 (unchanged).

### D115 — [PM] Pre-push credential re-audit — clean, but its own premise didn't match reality: zero commits have landed since D110, so the actual new material audited was the uncommitted working tree, not new commit history. Repository creation also blocked — no GitHub CLI in this environment

**Task premise checked, not assumed**: asked to re-check credential safety for "everything committed since D110's audit" — `git log --oneline | wc -l` shows exactly 16 commits, identical to the count D110 audited, tip still at `f5dcc63` ("D049"). **Zero commits have landed since D110.** Every decision from D050 through D114 — the large majority of this session's actual work — has remained uncommitted in the working tree the entire time. This doesn't match the task's stated premise ("several sessions of real work have landed since then" — true of the *work*, not of any *commits*).

**Audited the substantively equivalent material instead of trivially reporting "nothing to check."** Since nothing new has entered git history, the actual new material relative to D110's audit is the full uncommitted diff: 72 changed/untracked files (`git status --short`), covering effectively all of D050–D114's real work.
